## 4. Memory model and engine arbitration

### 4.1 Budget — AGREED, unified to Linux numbers

**RESOLVED (C3):** the brief carried both Windows (96 GB fixed) and Linux (~120 GB dynamic) numbers. Windows is gone, and the machine now has 192 GB. These are the binding numbers.

*v0.4.0 (S-21, S-38; D-14 option (e), D-31 option (a) and D-32 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* the always-resident set follows the D-14 placement. The router is the only companion left on the iGPU; the Qwen3 4B retrieval pair runs on the CPU, where the Engine Arbiter charges it as a host resident set of its own. Every engine is budgeted from the exact bytes of its files (D-31), and one LLM seat runs alone (D-32).

| Consumer | Budget | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Ubuntu Server, XFCE, Docker, Cockpit | 4 GB | XFCE adds roughly 0.5 GB over the headless figure in v0.1 |
| Open WebUI, ChromaDB, graph store | 3 GB | v0.4.0: the embedding model has its own row below (D-14 (e)); v0.3.5 counted it here, at 4 GB for the row |
| Kokoro, Whisper Large-v3-Turbo, PyAnnote | 3 GB | Always loaded. v0.4.0 (C-06): the diarisation pipeline is pyannote speaker-diarization-community-1, replacing 3.1, which V6 probes only as a diagnostic when community-1 is refused (14.1); nothing falls back to it |
| Orchestrator, Celery workers, Redis, Sentinel, ntfy, WG-Easy | 1–2 GB | |
| **CPU-side subtotal** | **~12 GB** | Inside the Arbiter's fixed 16 GiB host reserve, with transient CPU work and kernel overhead (S44, S48); the host-side consumers the reserve covers are bounded since S55 (below the table) |
| Eleanor's resident router model, Qwen3.5-4B `Q8_0`, on the iGPU | ~5.5 GiB | Always loaded. The whole GPU resident set since v0.4.0: a 4.48 GB file (4.17 GiB), about 0.53 GiB of `q8_0` KV at 4 × 8k and its compute buffers (INFERRED until the Arbiter measures the set at startup). Classification only: Eleanor's own persona work runs on GLM-5.3-Flash (P-10) |
| Qwen3-Embedding-4B and Qwen3-Reranker-4B `Q8_0`, on the CPU | ~9.6 GiB | Always loaded (D-14 (e), D-45 (a), C-02, C-03), replacing the bge pair. Two 4.28 GB files (2 × 3.99 GiB), each with about 0.30 GiB of `q8_0` KV at 4,096 tokens and a 0.5 GiB compute allowance: 9.57 GiB projected (INFERRED). They run with `--device none`, so the GPU memory counter never shows them; the Arbiter charges each unit its weights' bytes in full (they stay file-backed pages the kernel may drop and re-read, so VmRSS can show less than the file) plus its measured anonymous memory (RssAnon + RssShmem of the unit's main process: the KV cache and compute buffers), never below this projection, which stands in while a unit cannot be measured: the host resident set. The host-reserve warning (S44) looks past only that anonymous share, since clean file pages count as available; `atlas-admin arbiter status` applies the same rule. T-15 measures their real host footprint and their throughput on the CPU against the iGPU (to-do) |
| **Withheld from engines** | **~31 GiB** | The 16 GiB reserve, the GPU resident set (~5.5) and the host resident set (~9.6). v0.3.5 withheld about 22 to 24 GiB: the reserve and a GPU set of 6 to 8 GiB with the bge pair on the iGPU |
| **Usable for engines** | **~156 GiB (expected)** | The memory pool minus what is withheld above (v0.3.5 said ~164 GiB; v0.3.4 said ~180 GB minus ~17 GB, ~170 GB). The Arbiter computes it in GiB as the smaller of the GTT total and MemTotal (kernel 7.0.0-38 reports the requested 192 GiB, more than the node has; S47), minus the GPU resident set it measures (the router alone since v0.4.0, about 5.5 GiB), minus the host resident set (the CPU retrieval pair, about 9.6 GiB, D-14 (e)), minus a fixed 16 GiB reserve for the CPU-side rows above (about 12 GB) plus transient CPU work and kernel overhead, which the GPU memory counter never sees (v0.3.5, S44, S48). MemTotal is the real limit, every page of it able to hold GTT memory, because Phase 1 step 4 also puts `kho=off` on the kernel line (S49): with kexec handover on, as Ubuntu's 7.0 kernel has it by default, about 12 to 14 GiB of MemTotal (about 13 expected, INFERRED) would be CMA scratch that GTT pages can never use, so the pool would overstate what engines can get by that much and the largest seats would likely fail for want of memory in Phase 3 although the Arbiter granted them; V3a fails while any of it is set aside, and `kho=off` leaves MemTotal itself unchanged. MemTotal is expected near 187 to 188 GiB once Phase 1 step 4 has removed the 4.25 GiB crash-kernel reservation that kdump-tools puts on the kernel line (192 GiB less the UMA carve-out, 512 MiB at the BIOS minimum of 3.2, the kernel's page map of about 3 GiB and about 0.5 GiB of firmware and kernel; an estimate until V3a records the real figure, S48). The budget is then (187 to 188) − 5.5 − 9.6 − 16 = about 155.9 to 156.9 GiB, about 156 GiB expected (INFERRED). The largest Day 1 seat, GLM-5.3-Flash at 155.05 GiB projected, fits while MemTotal is at least 155.05 + 16 + 5.5 + 9.57 = about 186.1 GiB, which leaves 0.9 to 1.9 GiB to spare; DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 at 153.64 GiB needs about 184.7 GiB and leaves 2.3 to 3.3 GiB (table below). With the reservation still in place MemTotal would be about 183 to 184 GiB, below both thresholds (183.5 GiB refuses both seats), so V3a fails on any reserved crash-kernel memory. Every figure in the table below is measured against this budget, not against the raw 192 GB. **VERIFY V3** on first boot before anything depends on it |

*S55 (approved by the Principal on 2026-10-17; `research_notes/ATLAS v0.4.0 build record/answers.md` section 1.5, measures 5 to 9):* the host RAM the reserve covers, which the GPU memory counter never sees, is now bounded. Every llama-server renders an explicit prompt-cache cap, `--cache-ram` from the `engines.json` field `cache_ram_mib`: 4096 MiB for chat and vision (every seat), 1024 for the router and 0 for the embedder and reranker. The prompt caches' worst case is therefore 5 GiB (the router and one seat), where llama-server's default of 8,192 MiB per server allowed 16 GiB, the whole reserve (INFERRED from the flag semantics, which are VERIFIED at b11474; 4.4). The Phase 3 to-do `cache-ram` measures whether to change the caps. `/tmp` is capped at 4 GiB (it was 16 GiB; 3.5). The Celery `cpu` worker runs min(8, nproc − 2) prefork children, at least 1 (it was nproc − 2, 30 on this CPU; about 1.2 to 2.5 GiB less, 22 fewer children at 60 to 120 MB each, INFERRED; 9.7). Kokoro, speaches (Whisper) and Docling run under cgroup caps of 3, 4 and 8 GiB, swap included, since the node has none (INFERRED, not measured). Like the core containers' caps, they bound a runaway, such as a large scanned PDF in Docling; they free nothing and are not budgeted. The kernel's out-of-memory killer has an order: a score adjustment of +500 on the Celery `cpu` worker (`OOMScoreAdjust`) and on Docling (`oom_score_adj`), so background work dies first, and -500 on the orchestrator (its restart stops the resident seat and loses the measured footprints, B-03) and on every `llama-server@` unit (a killed engine loses its load). The 16 GiB reserve itself stays until host use has been measured on the node (answers.md measure 15, reserved for the Principal and not scripted as a to-do; `atlas-admin arbiter status` shows the CPU-side use against the reserve): these bounds make the reserve honest; they do not raise the budget.

**What each Day 1 seat takes, run alone (v0.4.0, S-21; projected by the Arbiter's own code at the profile `engines.json` renders, INFERRED until Phase 3 measures it, to-dos `kv-estimates` and `cache-ram`):**

| Seat, run alone | Projected footprint | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| GLM-5.3-Flash `UD-IQ4_XS` with its glm5v mmproj (157.95 GB = 147.10 GiB of files), 8 × 32k, f16 KV | 155.05 GiB: weights 147.10, KV 4.81, recurrent state 1.14, compute buffers 2.0 | Fits, with 0.9 to 1.9 GiB to spare: the thinnest margin of the set. It is refused if the GPU resident set exceeds about 6.4 GiB at a MemTotal of 187 GiB, or if MemTotal is below about 186.1 GiB. Relief, in order: Phase 3's measured footprint replaces the projection (4.2 rule 1); fewer GLM slots (D-48, 4.3); a `q8_0` cache if T-04 allows it (2.25 GiB less); a smaller reserve once host use has been measured. The to-do `kv-estimates` carries the measured figures for the Principal's decision |
| DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 `UD-Q8_K_XL` (161.87 GB = 150.75 GiB), 1 × 131,072, f16 KV | 153.64 GiB: weights 150.75, KV 0.84, per-slot state 0.04, compute buffers 2.0 (the `q8_0` rung: 153.24) | Fits, with 2.3 to 3.3 GiB to spare. Before D-31 the 161.87 GB file was read as 161.87 GiB and refused. The register's "about 13 GiB" of room (D-11) assumed the bge pair on the iGPU; with the CPU pair resident the room is this. Should Phase 3 find it short (T-06), the relief order is: stop the reranker, which has no Day 1 caller (about 4.8 GiB); cut the context; fall back to `UD-Q4_K_XL` (155.10 GB = 144.44 GiB, D-11 (b)). Its native 1,048,576-token window does not fit (6.72 GiB of f16 KV) |
| gpt-oss-120b F16-trunk GGUF, native MXFP4 experts (65.37 GB = 60.88 GiB), 8 × 32k, `q8_0` KV | 68.29 GiB: weights 60.88, KV 4.78, sliding-window cache 0.63, compute buffers 2.0 | Fits, with about 88 GiB to spare that stays unused: it runs alone like every seat (4.2 rule 3). The fast utility and the fallback floor every seat can use (D-12, D-40) |
| v0.3.5's two-engine pairings (142 GB and 126 GB) and the everyday text-and-vision pair | — | Withdrawn (D-32 (a), S-22): one LLM seat is resident at a time, and vision runs on the GLM unit itself (D-20 (a)). Nemotron 3 Super, Qwen3.5-122B, Qwen2.5-VL-72B and Meditron leave the Day 1 roster (D-13, D-21) |

The `gpt-oss-120b-abliterated` entry is unchanged by this revision; its 63 GB is still read as GiB, since it carries no byte counts (D-31).

**Phased seats (D-26 (c), D-42 (b)): not pulled on Day 1.** Each is checked against the measured budget before its pull. To-do: the post-Day-1 pull, with the same checksum and load tests; the event that brings it in is the Phase 3 gate passed with the measured budget recorded. Weights alone: Intern-S1 `Q5_K_S` 150.84 GiB and Baichuan-M3 `Q5_K_S` 150.76 GiB; Ling-3.0-flash-Fin `Q8_0` 126.32 GiB; Qwen3-Coder-Next `Q8_0` 78.99 GiB; Nemotron 3 Super `Q8_0` 119.65 GiB; Qwen3.8-Flash-Next `UD-Q6_K_XL` 157.55 GiB. Three of these no longer fit as the register sized them, which assumed about 164 GiB (INFERRED). Flash-Next's 157.55 GiB exceeds the ~156 GiB budget on weights alone, so D-28 option (c), `UD-Q5_K_XL` at 147.42 GiB, applies unless the measured budget allows (a). The two 150.8 GiB `Q5_K_S` rungs leave about 3 GiB for context after the 2 GiB compute allowance, short of the D-16 default of about 23K tokens at f16 (192,512 B/token). Their rung and context are re-checked when they are pulled, with `Q4_K_M` (D-16 (b)) as the longer-context rung. The Principal's context target for each is a to-do (`context-targets`, D-16), defaulted to `Q5_K_S` at about 23K.

### 4.2 Engine Arbiter — NEW, hard requirement

Nothing in the earlier brief named the component that enforces the residency and generation rules. It is defined here.

**The Engine Arbiter** is a single service inside the orchestrator through which every load and unload of any weight-bearing process passes: the LLM seats of `engines.json`, the always-resident companions (measured, never evicted), every Phase 4 engine, Chatterbox when invoked. Rules:

1. It holds a ledger of currently resident engines and their measured footprint. *v0.3.5 (S52): a measured footprint replaces the estimate only at the profile the engine ran when it was measured (context size, slots and cache type), and only until the orchestrator restarts: the measurements are held in memory and nothing re-measures an ordinary load, so after a restart the Arbiter's estimates govern every load and eviction again. v0.4.0 (S-22): with the co-resident profile gone, each seat has one standing profile.*
2. A load request states the engine and the context or batch size. The Arbiter computes the projected footprint against the live budget and either grants, queues, or refuses. *v0.4.0 (D-31 (a), S-17, S-20): the projection is the exact bytes of the engine's files (`files[].bytes`, from the Hugging Face tree, the mmproj included), no longer the decimal-GB figure read as GiB, plus the KV cache at the seat's cache type, the per-slot state (recurrent, sliding-window and compressor caches) and a compute-buffer term: 2 GiB for an LLM seat and 0.5 GiB for a companion, INFERRED planning figures that a measurement replaces.*
3. **One LLM seat may be resident; exactly one may generate at any moment** (v0.4.0, D-32 (a), S-23; this supersedes v0.3.5's "two engines may be resident" and C26). The two large Day 1 seats are 147 to 151 GiB of weights, and every seat, gpt-oss-120b included, runs alone with the whole budget. The always-resident companions, Chatterbox (class `external`, which evicts a seat only when the budget needs the memory, never for the limit) and the Phase 4 containers never count against the limit. The limit is a setting, `ATLAS_ARBITER_MAX_RESIDENT` in `orchestrator.env` (default 1, range 1 to 2, written once by Phase 2 step 2 and never reset by a re-run): 2 brings back v0.3.5's pairing of seats that fit (D-32 option (b)) without a code change, the Apex engine staying exclusive. This applies everywhere, including background work. Generation is bandwidth-bound, so two concurrent streams would each run at roughly half speed; that is why simultaneous generation is not adopted at all.
4. A second generation request queues behind the running one. *v0.4.0 (S-12, B-05): a swap no longer costs the 15 to 45 seconds v0.3.5 gave.* A resident 147 to 151 GiB seat leaves only about 10 to 25 GiB for page cache, so a switch between two large seats is a full cold read through LUKS: about 50 to 85 seconds per swap in all (the read alone 25 to 70 s, most likely 40 to 65 s from one Gen4 drive), plus re-prefill of the task's context unless a saved slot is restored: about 1.5 to 4.5 minutes at 16k to 32k and up to 13 minutes at 64k for DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 on stock Vulkan (all INFERRED from other machines' measurements; T-14 measures them in Phase 3, and 5.4 carries the figures). On Day 1 the queue stays FIFO, and GLM-5.3-Flash, which carries most seats, stays resident between them (D-23 (a)). Batching queued work by engine is phased after Day 1. To-do: order the queue by engine affinity with a starvation bound, and prefer to evict the fast engines that recover page cache (S-12). The event that brings it in: Day 1 complete, with T-14's measured swap cost recorded. The Celery `gpu` queue keeps exactly one worker, so a background job simply waits its turn (Section 9.7).
5. Before loading, it confirms the previous engine's memory has actually been released, by polling the GPU memory counters, not by trusting the process exit. This check is the difference between a stable node and one that crashes on the second swap. *v0.4.0, answering the Principal's question of 2026-10-16 (N-01, N-03: are the smaller engines loaded at the same time, after the RAM has been flushed, to avoid running out of memory?): no small model is part of a swap.* The router and the CPU retrieval pair start once at boot, are measured, and stay loaded; the Arbiter subtracts them, with the 16 GiB reserve, before it budgets a seat. A swap is: the current generation finishes (rule 6); the old seat is stopped; the GPU memory counter is polled until its memory is back, and if it is not back within 60 seconds the Arbiter halts, refusing every later load until a person intervenes, rather than risk a crash; the new seat loads alone into the room it left; and the request goes to it only once it reports healthy. Flushing the page cache by hand would add no headroom: the budget is taken from MemTotal, and on this integrated GPU llama.cpp loads the weights without mmap, so they are never held twice (VERIFIED at b11474). The headroom v0.4.0 gains comes from exact-byte footprints, correct KV figures, one seat at a time and the 4B retrieval pair (4.1). Host memory the budget cannot see is bounded since S55 (approved by the Principal on 2026-10-17; 4.1, 4.4): an explicit prompt-cache cap on every llama-server, which the Phase 3 to-do `cache-ram` measures, a 4 GiB `/tmp`, at most 8 Celery CPU workers, memory caps on the voice and document containers, and an order for the kernel's out-of-memory killer. *S55 (measure 10):* a seat whose start fails (`systemctl start` fails, or the unit never reports healthy) is stopped at once, and the stop also cancels the restart that `Restart=on-failure` would make 5 seconds later outside the ledger (INFERRED from systemd semantics). The Arbiter keeps the reservation while it polls the GPU memory counter back to its reading before the start, with this rule's poll, tolerance and 60-second time-box; only then does it release the reservation and log the error. If the stop fails, or the memory is not back in time, the Arbiter halts exactly as on a release timeout. The always-resident companions are not stopped after a failed start (their memory is measured before any seat, and `Restart=on-failure` is their recovery), and a class-external load starts and stops nothing (`atlas/arbiter.py` `_stop_failed_start`; VERIFIED in the code and its tests, not yet on the node).
6. It never preempts an engine mid-generation. A Sentinel job, a Celery task, or a second persona waits for the current generation to finish.
7. **Every LLM seat is exclusive** (v0.4.0, S-23; v0.3.5 reserved this for the Apex engine). Loading a seat unloads every other resident seat first, and nothing co-resides with it but the always-resident companions and a class-external load the budget allows. A later request for another seat evicts it when it is idle (rule 5) or queues while it generates (rule 6): residency is exclusive, not permanent. At a limit of 2 only the Apex engine (DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, 161.87 GB) is exclusive.
8. Deep Think and any multi-branch job must request their full footprint up front; if it does not fit, the Arbiter downgrades the depth tier (Section 9.1) rather than attempt the load. *v0.4.0 (D-22, S-43): the Deep Think pair, GLM-5.3-Flash and DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, takes turns, each turn a swap (rule 4); gpt-oss-120b gives the third opinion on Day 1, standing in for the phased Qwen3.8-Flash-Next.*
9. Every decision is logged with the task ID from the Celery layer (Section 9.7).

**Phase 4 engines under single-seat operation (v0.4.0, S-46; D-50 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register).** No Phase 4 engine runs beside an LLM seat: the two large seats leave no room beside them, and the rule holds for gpt-oss-120b too. Phase 4 step 2 unloads every resident seat through the Arbiter before its first engine loads, so Phase 4's Day 1 tests are unaffected: each engine is tested alone. After Day 1 a Phase 4 job evicts the resident seat the same way, runs alone, and the seat reloads on its next request; each eviction costs a reload plus re-prefill (rule 4) until slot save and restore lands (4.4). Image and video work is to be batched on the Celery `gpu` queue so one eviction serves several jobs; that batching is part of the phased swap batching (rule 4). The always-resident companions stay.

### 4.3 KV-cache protocol — AGREED, corrected

**RESOLVED (C4):** the proposed protocol mixed shipped features with unmerged research. Only shipped features are adopted.

| Setting | Value | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Flash Attention | On | Prerequisite for cache quantisation. v0.4.0: with `on`, an operation the device lacks would run on the CPU without a warning (INFERRED at b11474); T-04 checks GLM-5.3-Flash's placement on Vulkan |
| KV cache type, GLM-5.3-Flash (Ren, Gideon, Silas, Helena, Minerva, Victor, Alaric, Eleanor's persona work, vision, long documents) | `f16`, **provisional until T-04** (v0.4.0, S-18, D-29 (a)) | The cache is cheap: 19,712 B/token at f16, 0.60 GiB per 32k (VERIFIED at b11474 from its header and the cache code), so precision costs little. If flash attention cannot run for `glm5-next` on Vulkan, a quantised cache is impossible anyway; `q8_0` (10,472 B/token) would save 2.25 GiB at 8 × 32k if T-04 allows it. K and V types must match on this MLA architecture (VERIFIED) |
| KV cache type, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 (Arthur, the Apex engine) | `f16`, **the highest-precision coherent rung that fits, proven by ladder** (v0.4.0, S-42, D-29 (a); replacing v0.3.5's descent to a `q4_0` target): Phase 3 loads f16 first at the entry's own profile, and it stands if it loads, prints its KV proof lines and answers the coherence prompt; `q8_0` is tried only if f16 fails to load or answers incoherently; `q4_0` is never a rung. K and V types must be identical on this architecture | The cache is cheap: 6,880 B/token at f16, 0.84 GiB at 128k (VERIFIED at b11474), so the margin no longer forces a quantised cache. Quantised KV on `deepseek4` was disputed (llama.cpp #25382, #26423), so the fallback rung is measured, not assumed |
| KV cache type, gpt-oss-120b (fast utility, fallback floor, Deep Think third opinion on Day 1) | `q8_0` (v0.4.0, D-29 (a); was `q4_0`) | `q8_0` K and V is task-lossless in the research (mean KLD 0.0018; UNVERIFIED on this node), while `q4_0` cost measurable accuracy; `q4_0` was chosen only for co-residency headroom, which one seat at a time no longer needs. Its sliding-window cache prints two KV lines, both `q8_0` |
| KV cache type, `gpt-oss-120b-abliterated` | `q4_0`, as in v0.3.5 | Unchanged by this revision (header) |
| KV cache type, resident companions | `q8_0`: the router at 4 × 8k on the iGPU, the Qwen3 embedder and reranker at 4,096 tokens on the CPU (v0.4.0) | Unlike the bge pair, the Qwen3 pair are causal models with a KV cache (147,456 B/token at f16; VERIFIED). The vision engine is the GLM unit itself (D-20 (a)), so it has no row of its own |
| Per-model quantisation check | Day 1 verification script | **VERIFY V4**: unsupported architectures silently fall back to full precision. v0.4.0: GLM-5.3-Flash prints two KV size lines (its attention cache and its indexer cache), and both must show the requested type (VERIFIED at b11474) |
| Context size | Explicit per model, never the default | Default contexts are small and silently truncate agent history. v0.3.5 (S52): the pinned llama.cpp allocates the cache for the whole `--ctx-size` when the engine loads and writes every page, so the memory is committed until it unloads (VERIFIED in the source); reserved but unused context costs memory, not decode speed |
| Context shift with `n_keep` | **Passed explicitly as `--context-shift --keep <n>`** on every launch line (it is off by default in current llama-server); `n_keep` covers system prompt + persona directive + router state. *Corrected in v0.3.5 (S52) and re-verified at b11474 (v0.4.0, S-44):* the pinned llama.cpp switches it off, logging "ctx_shift is not supported", on every Day 1 chat engine: both gpt-oss engines (sliding-window cache), the router (IMROPE), DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 (its compressed cache) and GLM-5.3-Flash, whose text model could shift (it uses no RoPE) but whose unit always loads the glm5v projector, and the server switches context shift off for a multimodal model (VERIFIED in the source; Phase 3 records each engine's log line). The flags stay on every launch line | Corrected in v0.3.5 (S52): earlier versions said generation never hard-stops. On every engine, context shift or not, a prompt longer than its slot is rejected with an error (HTTP 400, `exceed_context_size_error`), never truncated: context shift acts only during generation (VERIFIED in the source at both pins). With it off, generation also stops at the slot end and `--keep` has no effect; the per-slot window row below covers every engine |
| Per-slot window (v0.3.5, S52) | Enforced by the orchestrator on every request to every chat engine: the prompt is counted by the engine itself and must leave a bounded reply reserve inside one slot of the profile the unit runs: the `max_tokens` asked for (4,096 when a chat request sets none), capped at a quarter of the slot or 512 tokens, whichever is larger. That is 4,096 on a 32k slot (8,192 when a client asks for the whole window), 2,048 on the router's 8k slot, and on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731's 128k slot the 4,096 default, or up to 32,768 when a client asks for more (v0.4.0; Meditron's 4k slot left with D-21). Only a conversation whose prompt does not leave it loses history: the oldest whole exchanges are dropped after the stable prefix (4.4), the fewest that leave the reserve, and the current turn is kept; when the prefix and the current turn alone cannot leave it, every earlier exchange goes. A conversation that leaves the reserve is sent whole, whatever `max_tokens` it asks for. The reply is then capped to the room left, so prompt and reply never reach the slot end; a message too large for the window on its own (the stable prefix and the current turn leave less than 512 tokens for the reply, or less than the reply asked for if that is smaller) is refused with a clear error (status 413, `context_window_exceeded`, naming the engine, its window and the prompt size: the HTTP status of a non-streamed request; a streamed one, as Open WebUI sends, has already been answered HTTP 200, so the 413 arrives in its error chunk with the `[ATLAS]` text) and nothing is sent | Context shift cannot be relied on (row above), and a hard stop at the slot end would cut a reply off mid-sentence. The reserve is bounded so that neither a large `max_tokens` nor the 4,096 default on a small slot (LightRAG's extraction calls on the router) costs a conversation history that fits; the quarter-slot cap is a policy figure, not a measurement. The window is read from the unit's own settings on every request, never a fixed figure: 32k on GLM-5.3-Flash, vision included, and on gpt-oss-120b; 128k on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 (1 × 131,072, whichever rung the ladder keeps); 8k on the router (v0.4.0, S-44) |
| Parallel slots | **Always passed explicitly** (`--parallel` defaults to auto, and an explicit `--parallel` keeps `--kv-unified` off; VERIFIED at b11474). `--ctx-size` is the total pool across slots, so 32k per slot × 8 is `--ctx-size 262144`. Day 1 profiles (v0.4.0, S-44; stated defaults until D-48 is measured, below): GLM-5.3-Flash 8 × 32k without `--kv-unified`; DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 `--parallel 1 --ctx-size 131072`; gpt-oss-120b 8 × 32k; the router 4 × 8k; the Qwen3 pair 1 × 4,096. v0.3.5's standing 2-slot vision profile went with the pairing it served (S-22), with its `overrides.json` coresident setting | KV at these profiles (INFERRED): GLM 4.81 GiB at f16 plus 1.14 GiB of recurrent state; DeepSeek 0.84 GiB at f16 plus 0.04 GiB of per-slot state; gpt-oss 4.78 GiB at `q8_0` plus 0.63 GiB of sliding-window cache (Phase 3 measures the real figures, to-do `kv-estimates`). One generation runs at a time (4.2 rule 3), so GLM's extra slots serve as warm prompt caches for the seats it carries, not concurrency (INFERRED). Without `--kv-unified` each slot is also a hard limit per request |
| Hard pre-flight | Engine Arbiter | The actual OOM backstop |
| Long-document recall | Vector Cortex retrieval, never KV tricks | Context shift keeps anchors and recency; it does not recall a mid-document fact once evicted. v0.4.0 (D-38 (a)): the long-document route `[ARTHUR:LONG]` (`arthur-long`, which replaces `arthur-qwen`) goes to GLM-5.3-Flash, where each request is held to its 32k slot and the orchestrator's trimming recalls nothing either, so long documents stay on retrieval. The automatic trigger moves a document only to an engine whose per-slot window is no smaller than the routed engine's: Arthur's DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 holds 131,072 tokens in its one slot, so his long documents stay there until D-48 gives GLM a longer profile; a director bound to GLM as an override is moved under the same rule (6.2); the typed `[ARTHUR:LONG]` always goes to GLM (7.1). The 24,000-token trigger stays until GLM's slot profile is measured (to-do below, S-30). `--kv-unified`, which would let one request use the whole pool at the cost of the other slots' cached prompts, is recorded for D-48, not adopted |

**Not adopted:** DuoAttention, SnapKV. Real research, not merged into llama.cpp or Ollama. Watch-list only.

**Asymmetric quantisation** is real in this design only at the per-engine level (each persona's engine loads separately), not per attention head.

**Slot and context profiles (v0.4.0, S-44; D-48 is a Phase 3 measurement, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register).** The Day 1 profiles above are stated defaults. D-48 sets each seat's slots and context from the Phase 3 `kv-estimates` measurement and T-04: option (b), GLM-5.3-Flash on `--kv-unified` with its 8 slots sharing one pool and the per-slot window rule rewritten for a pooled window, if the parity and coherence tests show coherent output under it; otherwise option (c), GLM on `--parallel 1` with one large context, the other conversations kept by slot save and restore (phased, 4.4). The register's premise that `glm5-next` needs `--kv-unified` for more than one sequence has no support in the source at b11474: no assertion requires it, and its cache builds one stream per slot (VERIFIED). So Day 1 runs 8 separate slots, and T-04 checks their coherence on the node. To-dos for the Principal, each built with its stated default: the DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 context target (default 131,072 tokens in one slot; its native 1,048,576 needs 6.72 GiB of f16 KV, which the budget does not have beside its weights, 4.1); GLM's cache type and slot profile (T-04, D-48); and the long-document trigger, 24,000 tokens until GLM's profile is measured (D-38, S-30). The scripts raise the three as the to-do `seat-profiles` (Phase 3 step 2), with the defaults read from `engines.json` and `router-rules.json`.

### 4.4 Prompt-cache layering — NEW

Prompt processing is slow here; every dispatch that changes the system prompt from the top invalidates the cached prefix and pays full prefill. The system prompt is therefore built in fixed layers, most stable first:

1. Persona core (Ren or Arthur or a director): never changes within a session.
2. Governance block (approval tiers, never-delegate rule, disclosure rule): static.
3. Domain injections (Section 8.4): change per task force, appended after the static layers.
4. Retrieved memory and scars: appended last.
5. Conversation.

Slot-level cache reuse in llama-server keeps layers 1–2 resident across dispatches. Layer 3 changes cost only their own prefill.

*v0.3.5 (S52):* when a conversation no longer leaves its engine's bounded reply reserve inside the per-slot window (4.3: the `max_tokens` asked for, 4,096 by default, capped at a quarter of the slot or 512 tokens, whichever is larger), the orchestrator drops only the oldest exchanges of layer 5, the fewest that leave it; a conversation that leaves it is sent whole and only its reply is capped; when even the stable prefix and the current turn cannot leave it, every earlier exchange goes and the reply is capped to the room left (refused under 512 tokens, 4.3). Layers 1 to 4 travel as the leading system message, which is never trimmed, so the cached prefix keeps its hits; the conversation after a dropped exchange is processed again (INFERRED). Each slot keeps one cached prefix. *v0.4.0:* GLM-5.3-Flash carries seven seats, Eleanor's persona work and vision on one unit, so its 8 slots can keep several personas' stable prefixes warm at once (INFERRED); v0.3.5's 2-slot vision profile is gone (S-22).

*v0.4.0 (phased):* a swap discards the outgoing seat's cached prefixes and the task's context, which is processed again when the seat returns (4.2 rule 4). Saving each task's slot before its engine leaves and restoring it when the engine returns (D-44 (a), S-35) is phased after Day 1. To-do: the save and restore hooks in the Arbiter and the slot client. The event that brings it in: T-13 shows on the node that restore works for every Day 1 architecture at b11474. `--slot-save-path` is already set on every chat unit (Appendix B), and the `/slots` routes exist at b11474 (VERIFIED). Host-RAM prompt caches: each llama-server keeps up to 8,192 MiB of prompt cache in host RAM by default, and b11474 adds `--cache-idle-slots`, on by default, which saves idle slots into it (VERIFIED). This memory is outside the Arbiter's budget. *S55 (B-33, measure 5, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-17):* every engine now renders an explicit `--cache-ram` from `engines.json` `cache_ram_mib`: 4096 MiB for chat and vision (every seat), 1024 for the router, and 0 for the retrieval companions, which disables their cache and with it the idle-slot copies (VERIFIED at b11474). `--cache-ram`, `-cram` and `LLAMA_ARG_CACHE_RAM` are reserved, so only that field sets the cap. The worst case for the router and one seat falls from 16 GiB to 5 GiB (INFERRED from the flag semantics); the context checkpoints stay at llama-server's default of 32 per slot. The Phase 3 to-do `cache-ram` measures each server's cache and checkpoints against the cap it renders and asks whether to change a cap or set `--ctx-checkpoints`. Fewer cached prompts mean more re-prefill when GLM-5.3-Flash moves between its seats (INFERRED).

## 5. Inference stack

### 5.1 The Day 1 engines and their quantisations — CLOSED (rewritten in v0.4.0)

**RESOLVED (C23):** v0.1 ran everything at 4-bit because 128 GB forced it. With 192 GB the rule becomes: use the highest quantisation that leaves working headroom, except where a model's native release is already low-bit. *v0.4.0 (S-19; D-02, D-10 to D-13, D-21, D-29 option (a) and D-59 option (b), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* a new resolution from C28 supersedes C23 (Section 18). The rule is now the Principal's standing rule (Section 1): each seat runs the least-quantised rung that fits it alone, with its measured quality cost stated. No file is sized for a co-resident partner any more, because every LLM seat runs alone (4.2 rule 3). The table replaces v0.3.5's five core engines, Meditron and the vision engine with the LLM entries of `engines.json`; the three always-resident companions are in 5.3.

| Engine | Architecture | Quantisation | File | Decode | Role |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **GLM-5.3-Flash** (`glm-5.3-flash`, MIT) | 320B MoE, 18B active (`glm5-next`, first in llama.cpp b11279, hence the re-pin, 3.4); natively multimodal through its glm5v projector | **`UD-IQ4_XS`**, the one lossy rung of the set (published mean KLD 0.117 and 88.2% same top token, UNVERIFIED on this node); provisional on every seat until the Phase 3 KLD run (T-03, D-02) | 157.95 GB: 156.82 GB in five shards plus the 1.13 GB `mmproj-F16.gguf` at the repository root (147.10 GiB) | 12–14 tok/s projected (Section 2) | **The default resident seat (D-23).** Ren, Gideon, Silas, Helena, Minerva, Victor, Alaric and Eleanor's persona work (P-10); the vision engine (D-20 (a)); the long-document route (D-38 (a)); the Deep Think generator (D-22) |
| **DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731** (`deepseek-v4-flash`, MIT) | 284B MoE, 13B active (`deepseek4`) | **`UD-Q8_K_XL`**, raised from `UD-Q4_K_XL`: bit-exact to the released weights (D-11 (a), S-42) | 161.87 GB in five files (150.75 GiB) | About 7 tok/s projected on mainline Vulkan, a floor (Section 2) | **The Apex engine and Arthur's default seat (P-02).** TF_OMEGA, the Deep Think adversary and synthesis (D-22), strong cross-checks (D-39 (a)). Runs alone |
| **gpt-oss-120b** (`gpt-oss-120b`, Apache 2.0) | 117B MoE, 5.1B active (`gpt-oss`) | **F16 trunk, experts in native MXFP4** (D-59 (b)) | 65.37 GB, one file (60.88 GiB) | 57–60 tok/s projected; 53.8–55.6 measured with the MXFP4 file on 128 GB Ryzen AI Max+ 395 machines | **The fast utility and the fallback floor** (D-12 (a), D-40 (a)): drafting, Sentinel escalation (9.3), the Day 1 Deep Think third opinion (9.1) and the Day 1 fallback of every seat whose planned runner-up is phased (6.2). No longer the default resident engine |
| gpt-oss-120b abliterated (Huihui) | Same weights, refusals removed | **MXFP4, re-quantised** (Section 23: the abliteration is applied to the BF16 upcast; the MXFP4 GGUF is a requant, same size and speed, the "no accuracy cost" argument below does not transfer) | 63 GB | Same | Valerie default; Ren and Arthur on explicit override |

The abliterated row is v0.3.5's text, unchanged by this revision; its "Same" is v0.3.5's 30–55 tok/s band, which its `engines.json` entry keeps. Leaving the Day 1 roster (S-11; D-13 option (b), D-21 option (a)): Nemotron 3 Super, v0.3.5's Arthur default, which returns as a phased pull (below); Qwen3.5-122B-A10B, whose long documents move to GLM-5.3-Flash; Qwen2.5-VL-72B and Meditron-70B (paragraphs below); and the bge retrieval pair (5.3). Phase 3 now pulls the four LLM repositories of `engines.json`, about 448 GB and about 10 hours at 100 Mbps, where v0.3.5 pulled about 690 GB (D-42 (b)); the companions come in Phase 2 (5.3).

Decode speed follows bytes read per token, not parameter count (Section 2). That is why gpt-oss-120b, with 5.1B active, stays the fast engine, and why DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 at `UD-Q8_K_XL` is the slowest seat, about 7 tok/s projected against about 11 at `UD-Q4_K_XL` (INFERRED). Quality comes first (Section 1): Phase 3 records each seat's measured rate beside its projection and fails a seat only below a floor the Principal sets (V10, D-33 (c); to-do `decode-floors`).

**Why gpt-oss-120b keeps its native MXFP4 experts.** Its expert weights were released natively in MXFP4. There is no higher-precision original to return to; a `Q8` build would upcast the same 4-bit values, adding 60 GB and halving the speed for no accuracy. MXFP4 is this model's full-quality form. *v0.4.0 (D-59 option (b)):* the Day 1 file is the F16-trunk GGUF, 65.37 GB against the 63.39 GB file v0.3.5 named. The experts stay native MXFP4; the remaining tensors, about 2% of the parameters, are held at F16 instead of `Q8_0`. No independent measurement compares the two files, so this is the higher-precision choice, not a measured gain (INFERRED). The argument does not transfer to the abliterated engine (its row).

**Why DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 is now `UD-Q8_K_XL` (v0.4.0, S-42; D-11 option (a)).** v0.3.5 kept `UD-Q4_K_XL` because `UD-Q8_K_XL` seemed to leave about 2 GiB for the KV cache. Both inputs to that figure were wrong: the 161.87 GB file was read as 161.87 GiB instead of 150.75 GiB (below), and the Arbiter charged the cache at 327,680 B/token where the `deepseek4` cache takes 6,880 B/token at f16, 0.84 GiB at 128k (VERIFIED at b11474). The experts, about 96% of the weights, ship natively low-bit and are identical in both rungs; `UD-Q8_K_XL` keeps the remaining tensors at release precision, so the file is bit-exact to the released weights. Run alone at 1 × 131,072 tokens with an f16 cache it projects to 153.64 GiB, 2.3 to 3.3 GiB inside the expected budget (4.1). If Phase 3 finds it short (T-06), the relief order in 4.1 ends at `UD-Q4_K_XL` (155.10 GB, D-11 (b)). v0.3.5's 25–32 tok/s band had no measurement behind it and is withdrawn (Section 2).

**Footprints in exact bytes (v0.4.0, S-20; D-31 option (a)).** File sizes in this section are decimal GB, as Hugging Face lists them. The Arbiter budgets in GiB from the exact bytes of each entry's `files[]`, the mmproj included, taken with their sha256 from the Hugging Face tree (4.1, 4.2 rule 2). v0.3.5 read the decimal-GB figure as GiB, which overstated every file by about 7%: that is how the 161.87 GB DeepSeek file became 161.87 GiB and was refused. Only the unchanged abliterated entry, which carries no byte counts, is still read that way (4.1).

**Meditron-70B leaves the roster (v0.4.0, S-11; D-15 option (a) and D-21 option (a), reopening D12 through D18, Section 19).** v0.3.5 included it in Phase 3 at `Q8_0` (~74 GB, ~3 tok/s, a 4k context) as Minerva's secondary cross-check. Her cross-check moves to Baichuan-M3-235B, which is phased (below); on Day 1 Arthur on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 checks her drafts (9.2). Its `join_into` path and Phase 3's 74 GB transient copy are gone. C5 holds for every engine: all of them run through llama.cpp, not the PyTorch layer.

**The vision engine is GLM-5.3-Flash itself (v0.4.0, S-10; D-20 option (a), reopening D10 and superseding C25 through D17 and C29, Sections 18 and 19).** The glm5v projector (`mmproj-F16.gguf`, 1,128,047,296 bytes) loads on the GLM unit, whose entry is therefore `mode: vision`, so an image costs no swap while GLM is resident; one image takes at most 8,000 tokens plus two markers (VERIFIED from the projector's header and the image code at b11474). v0.3.5's Qwen2.5-VL-72B (`Q8_0`, 79 GB, ~3 tok/s) is retired, and with it its co-residency with gpt-oss-120b (S-22). The OCR companion that would transcribe scanned documents (C-04) is phased (5.3); it transcribes and does not reason, so ATLAS keeps one vision engine.

**Sampling and reasoning (v0.4.0, D-30 option (b)).** Each entry carries its vendor sampling: GLM-5.3-Flash at temperature 1.0 and top-p 0.95; DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 at temperature 1.0 with reasoning switched on at high effort, which its template leaves off by default; gpt-oss-120b at high reasoning effort. A fixed reasoning setting per seat is chosen after the Phase 3 quality and KLD runs (T-03; in the detail of the to-do `kld-run`); these are the provisional Day 1 settings.

**Phased after Day 1 (v0.4.0; D-26 option (c), D-42 option (b)).** These engines are recorded in `engines.json` under `phased`, which no script reads: nothing is pulled, rendered, loaded or routed from it, and no persona, route or preset names a planned key. Each is checked against the measured budget (4.1), then appended to `engines[]` after the Day 1 set, so no Day 1 port moves, with its exact file bytes and KV figures, and comes in only through a Phase 3 load test. To-do for each: the post-Day-1 pull, with the same checksum and load tests. The event that brings them in: the Phase 3 gate passed with the measured budget recorded.

### 5.2 Backend — CLOSED (D1): llama-server

| Option | Vulkan on 26.04 host | Slot-level prompt cache | Nemotron 3 Super GGUF support | Maturity on gfx1151 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| llama-server (llama.cpp) | Mature, validated by multiple Strix Halo builders | Yes, per slot, save/restore | Yes | Highest |
| Ollama | Vulkan backend introduced as experimental; ROCm backend ships its own ROCm runtime as a separate download, and Ollama's Linux guide calls host ROCm optional; ATLAS runs ROCm only in the Phase 4 container (3.4; corrected in v0.3.5, S51: earlier versions said it needed host ROCm) | Coarser | Had loader incompatibilities with this model earlier in 2026 | Lags llama.cpp |

**Decision:** llama-server, accepted by the Principal. Ollama is not installed. Both expose an OpenAI-compatible API, so Appendix B retains the Ollama equivalents only as a fallback reference if the primary path ever fails on this hardware.

*v0.4.0:* D1 stands. llama-server is now built from build tag b11474 (3.4), because GLM-5.3-Flash's architecture and its glm5v projector first appear in b11279. The table's Nemotron column was v0.3.5's concern; Nemotron 3 Super is now a phased pull (5.1). Per-slot save and restore exists in llama-server at the pin (VERIFIED), but the orchestrator's use of it across swaps is phased (4.4, D-44).

### 5.3 Resident small models — CLOSED (D4; reopened and closed again in v0.4.0)

| Role | Requirement | Day 1 model (v0.4.0) |
|---|---|---|
| Eleanor's router and classifier | ~4B, instruction-following, fast, always resident, separate from the seat | Qwen3.5-4B `Q8_0` (`router-qwen3.5-4b`), 4.48 GB, on the iGPU: unchanged (C-01) |
| Embeddings | Multilingual-ready, strong retrieval | Qwen3-Embedding-4B `Q8_0` (`embed-qwen3-4b`), 4.28 GB, on the CPU, its vectors truncated to the 1024-dim schema (C-02, S-41, 10.1) |
| Reranker | Improves RAG precision | Qwen3-Reranker-4B `Q8_0` (`rerank-qwen3-4b`), a 4.28 GB conversion that carries the rank head, on the CPU (C-03) |

Until v0.4.0 (D4): the Principal accepted these as recommended: Qwen3.5 4B-class instruct for routing, bge-m3 for embeddings, bge-reranker-v2-m3 for reranking.

*v0.4.0 (S-36; D-14 option (e), D-45 option (a), C-01 to C-03, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register; a new decision row from D16 reopens D4, Section 19):* the bge pair is replaced by the Qwen3 4B pair. Its published scores are 69.45 against bge-m3's 59.56 on MTEB multilingual and 72.74 against bge-reranker-v2-m3's 58.36 on MMTEB-R (UNVERIFIED on the Principal's corpus; T-15 compares hit rates). The 8B pair scores about a point higher but would leave neither Day 1 seat usable room (INFERRED: right-sized, the 8B pair takes about 16.4 GiB, leaving about 149.6 GiB, so DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 is refused and GLM-5.3-Flash keeps about 3.6 GiB beyond its weights; the register's D-14 analysis, `research_notes/ATLAS v0.4.0 build record/``answers.md` §2.4). The pair runs on the CPU (`--device none`), off the iGPU's compute queue, right-sized at 4,096 tokens with a `q8_0` cache and `--cache-ram 0`, and the Arbiter charges it as the host resident set (4.1). This departs from C-02, which recommended serving the embedder with `-ub 8192` (8,192-token inputs, as bge-m3 took): at that size the pair would take about 3.8 GiB more and leave GLM-5.3-Flash about 6.7 GiB of room instead of 10.6 (INFERRED, `research_notes/ATLAS v0.4.0 build record/``answers.md` §2.3, §2.4), so the D-14 (e) budget holds it to 4,096 tokens, and LightRAG is told the same limit (`atlas/memory.py` `EMBEDDING_MAX_TOKENS`). To-do: T-15 re-checks a longer input profile against the measured budget. On the CPU a query embedding takes about 0.1 to 0.4 seconds per chat turn and an ingest about 20 seconds per 10,000 tokens, against about 5 on the iGPU (INFERRED; T-15 measures both, to-do). The change is made before Day 1 (D-45 (a)) because vectors from two embedders cannot be compared, even at the same dimension: Phase 2 step 4 indexes the still-empty corpus with the new model (10.1). The reranker has no caller on Day 1: no orchestrator code posts to `/v1/rerank`, and Open WebUI has no reranker configured. To-do: wire it into retrieval, capped at about 8 to 10 candidates of 512 tokens or fewer while it runs on the CPU (INFERRED). Stopping it is also the first relief if DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 is short of room (4.1).

**Why the router stays at 4B (P-10, answering the Principal's question of 2026-10-16: why is Eleanor's model only 4B, and why not a 32B?).** The router runs on every request, in the single generation slot before the request's own generation, so its latency is added to every chat. It must stay resident and separate from the seat: it decides which seat the Arbiter loads, and it has to answer while a swap is in flight. Its job is narrow: one JSON object from a closed set of labels, at temperature 0. Every resident GiB comes off every seat's budget (4.1). A 32B router at `Q8_0` (about 32 GiB, INFERRED) would leave neither Day 1 seat able to load, and at about 6 to 7 tok/s it would add 10 to 20 seconds to every request (INFERRED; a 27B dense model measured 7.3 to 7.7 tok/s on Strix Halo machines). The privacy membrane never depends on it: the keyword hard rules run first and always win (7.2). No routing benchmark exists for ATLAS's classifier, so the evidence that matters is the Principal's own traffic. To-do: once the node is live, build a labelled routing set from the routing ledger and the overridden-routing strikes (9.4), and measure the 4B's error rate. If the error is material, there are two options: a second opinion from the resident seat on ambiguous verdicts, which costs no resident memory, or a 9B router at about 4.7 GiB of every seat's room (INFERRED). What changes now is Eleanor's own work: her persona work (scheduling, triage, TF_RHO, domain 16) runs on GLM-5.3-Flash, a 320B-class model, normally with no swap because GLM is the default resident seat (6.2, 6.3). The 4B keeps classification, LightRAG extraction and the small helper calls.

**Phased companions (D-26 option (c)).** EmbeddingGemma 2 (`Q8_0`, 0.31 GB, b11452 or later, Apache 2.0) as an optional code and multimodal side index, never the primary embedder (C-07, N-11), is recorded in `engines.json` `phased`. To-do: keep or drop it (D-60) and, if kept, build the side index. The event: Day 1 complete, with the primary index in use. The OCR companion (C-04), the speech companions (C-05, C-09 and C-10: a WER test on the Principal's audio, Chatterbox Turbo and Qwen3-TTS), Nemotron 3 Diarization (C-06) and the image tier (C-11) are phased the same way. Each waits on its own node test (T-19 to T-25), and a resident OCR model would take about 1.3 to 1.5 GiB from every seat's budget, so its test re-checks the budget (4.1). The one exception approved for now is C-06's switch of pyannote from speaker-diarization-3.1 to community-1 (Section 14).

### 5.4 Model swapping — AGREED, figures restated in v0.4.0

| Step | Between the two large Day 1 seats, through LUKS from one Gen4 drive (v0.4.0; INFERRED from other machines' measurements until T-14) |
|---|---|
| Stop the previous seat and confirm its memory is back (4.2 rule 5) | Seconds to about 10 s (unmeasured); the release is polled, and the Arbiter halts if it is not back within 60 s |
| Cold read of 157.95 GB (GLM-5.3-Flash) or 161.87 GB (DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731) | 25–70 s, most likely 40–65 s, at the 2.4 to 6.3 GB/s measured on Strix Halo machines. A resident 147 to 151 GiB seat leaves only about 10 to 25 GiB for the page cache, so the other large seat is never still cached |
| Read of 65.37 GB (gpt-oss-120b) | About 10–27 s at the same rates. While it is resident, most of the memory stays free for the page cache, so a large seat it replaced may still be cached when it returns |
| Process start, file parse, allocation and warm-up | Seconds; v0.3.5 allowed 5–10 s (unmeasured) |
| Total per swap | **About 50–85 s** between two large seats (v0.3.5 said 15–45 s for any swap) |
| Re-prefill of the task's context when a seat returns, unless a saved slot is restored (phased, 4.4) | DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 on stock Vulkan: about 1.5–4.5 min at 16k–32k and up to 13 min at 64k. GLM-5.3-Flash: roughly 3 to 4 min at 32k, from its card's prefill of 256.4 tok/s at 2,048 tokens and 95.9 at 32k (Section 2) |

*v0.4.0 (S-12, B-05; D-23 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* v0.3.5's figures assumed reads of 4 to 6 GB/s and a page cache kept warm by two-engine residency; neither holds now. With one LLM seat resident (4.2 rule 3), every change of seat is a swap, while vision costs none as long as GLM-5.3-Flash is resident, because it runs on the GLM unit (5.1). Re-prefill, not the disk, dominates once contexts are long. The cost is contained in three ways: GLM-5.3-Flash carries most seats and stays resident between them (6.3); Deep Think phases are batched (9.1), while on Day 1 each strong cross-check swaps on its own (9.2); and three phased items, batching queued work and strong cross-checks by engine (6.3, 9.2) and saving and restoring slots across swaps (4.4), would remove most of the rest. Phase 3 records each seat's swap time and its prefill at 512 and about 8k tokens (V10 rows). To-do: the re-prefill at 32k and 64k per seat (T-14), which the Day 1 load test does not measure and against which the phased batching and slot restore are judged; the event: Day 1 complete, before the batching is built.

| Override engine | gpt-oss-120b abliterated, on Principal's order | GLM-5.3-Flash for deep document reasoning, the long-document route (v0.4.0, D-38 (a); was Qwen3.5-122B-A10B); **and the abliterated engine on the Principal's explicit command (C6 addendum)** |

**RESOLVED (C6):** the original brief bound Arthur's override to the abliterated engine "for legal dissection." Abliteration removes refusals and slightly increases errors; it does not improve reasoning. Arthur's standing override is therefore Qwen3.5 for long-document work. **Addendum accepted by the Principal:** Arthur may additionally be switched to the abliterated engine on explicit command, mirroring Ren. It is a manual escalation, never a default, and its output passes the same approval gate (R13). *v0.4.0 (D-38 option (a)):* the standing long-document override is GLM-5.3-Flash (`[ARTHUR:LONG]`, route `arthur-long`, 7.1), which replaces the retired Qwen3.5-122B-A10B; the addendum is unchanged by this revision.

**Seats with no benchmark (D-49 option (a)).** No board measures Victor's or Alaric's work, nor Australian law, HR, aviation, logistics, AEC, SCADA, game design, growth marketing, customer success or physical security and OSINT, so GLM-5.3-Flash carries those seats unproven. To-do: the Principal supplies a test set for each (T-27); until then the stated default stands, the general leader on every such seat. The event: after Day 1, once the node is live.

**Fallback engines (v0.4.0, D-40 option (a)).** Each persona file records a `fallback_engine`: the engine that carries the seat if its own fails or defers in Phase 3. It is the approved runner-up where that is a Day 1 engine (Arthur on GLM-5.3-Flash; Gideon and Helena on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731). It is gpt-oss-120b, the floor every seat can use, where the runner-up is phased (Ren and Victor: Qwen3.8-Flash-Next; Silas: Ling-3.0-flash-Fin; Minerva: Intern-S1), where it is GLM itself (Alaric), and for Eleanor's persona work. The field is a record, validated as an `engines.json` key, never a binding: neither the router nor the cross-checker picks it, and no code switches a seat to it at run time. Moving a failed seat to its fallback is the Principal's call from the Phase 3 record. Valerie's file has none (header).

**RESOLVED (C7):** the original brief bound Gideon and Minerva to the abliterated engine "for zero-hallucination" reasoning. Hallucination is controlled by retrieval over the Principal's own documents, mandatory citations, and a verifier pass, not by model choice. Both use the standard engine. The brief's "Silas runs exclusively on Nemotron for absolute mathematical precision" is retained with the correction that precision comes from the code interpreter; Nemotron orchestrates and audits. *v0.4.0:* GLM-5.3-Flash, on which Gideon and Minerva now run, is a standard engine, so C7 stands. Silas's engine is GLM-5.3-Flash (P-04) and the correction stands with it: precision comes from the code interpreter, and the engine orchestrates and audits.

**AGREED — External correspondence.** Directors correspond externally under their own identities (mailbox or alias, signature, voice) so that Ren and Arthur are reserved for matters that warrant them. Every outbound item passes the approval gate (Section 16.2) at the tier shown above.

### 6.3 Work grouping to minimise swaps — AGREED; the default resident engine changed in v0.4.0

*v0.4.0 (D-23 option (a), S-12, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register; v0.3.5 kept gpt-oss-120b resident by default):* GLM-5.3-Flash is the default resident seat and carries most of the day: Ren, Gideon, Silas, Helena, Minerva, Victor, Alaric, Eleanor's persona work, vision and long documents, so most work changes no engine. It loads on its first request like every seat, nothing preloads it, and it stays until another seat is requested (4.2 rule 7); because most requests need it, it is normally the one resident. DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 loads for Arthur's hemisphere, TF_OMEGA, the Deep Think adversary and synthesis, and strong cross-checks of GLM drafts. gpt-oss-120b loads for fast utility work, as the Day 1 third opinion and as a fallback. The abliterated engine loads only on explicit override or a Valerie sandbox task, as in v0.3.5. Each change of seat costs about 50 to 85 seconds plus re-prefill (5.4).

On Day 1 the Arbiter's queue stays first-in, first-out (4.2 rule 4), so v0.3.5's "the orchestrator batches queued work by engine before swapping" does not describe the Day 1 build. Batching is phased after Day 1. To-do: route a hemisphere's or persona's queued work to the resident seat before swapping, with a bound on how long other work may wait, and prefer to evict the fast engines that recover page cache (S-12); batch strong cross-checks (9.2) and Phase 4 image and video work (4.2) the same way. The event that brings it in: Day 1 complete, with T-14's measured swap cost recorded (5.4).

### 6.4 Persona register — AGREED

Ren and Arthur speak differently to the Principal than to external recipients. The register is a property set by the orchestrator on the outbound draft, not a prompt instruction the model can forget. Directors' external register is professional and role-appropriate; their internal register to Ren/Arthur is terse.

## 7. Routing and the privacy membrane

### 7.1 The 4-Way Router — AGREED, relocated

**RESOLVED (C8):** the brief described the router as "an Open WebUI pipeline." It lives in the orchestrator; the Open WebUI Filter is a thin relay into it. Reason: the same router must govern Sentinel, Celery tasks, email ingestion, and any future entry point, not only chat.

| Route | Trigger | Engine |
|---|---|---|
| Ren | Corporate, enterprise, infrastructure, AEC subject matter | GLM-5.3-Flash (v0.4.0; was gpt-oss-120b) |
| Arthur | Estate, health, logistics, family; any privacy keyword | DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 (v0.4.0; was Nemotron 3 Super) |
| Ren, override | Explicit Principal prefix | gpt-oss-120b abliterated |
| Arthur, override | Explicit Principal prefix (`[ARTHUR:LONG]`) or long-document trigger | GLM-5.3-Flash (v0.4.0, route `arthur-long`; was `arthur-qwen` on Qwen3.5-122B-A10B) |

*v0.4.0 (S-30; D-38 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* `router-rules.json` routes `ren` and `arthur-long` to `glm-5.3-flash`, and `arthur` and `deep-think:deep` to `deepseek-v4-flash`; the classifier stays `router-qwen3.5-4b` (P-10). The long-document route is renamed `arthur-long`, and the router refuses a rules file that still names `arthur-qwen`, so a stale file fails loudly. The trigger stays at 24,000 tokens, set for a 32k slot, until GLM's slot profile is measured (to-do `seat-profiles`, 4.3; D-48). Because GLM-5.3-Flash also carries Ren and most directors, a long document usually costs no swap. The trigger moves a document only where the long-document engine's per-slot window is no smaller than the routed engine's (`atlas/router.py`): on Day 1 Arthur's 131,072-token DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 slot is larger than GLM's 32,768, so his long documents stay on DeepSeek, with the reason `long-document:kept on deepseek-v4-flash` in the routing ledger, until D-48 gives GLM a longer profile; the typed `[ARTHUR:LONG]` still goes to GLM. The uncensored routes and prefixes are unchanged by this revision, and no new prefix is added (D-37 option (a), 8.4).

### 7.2 Routing rules — AGREED

1. **Keyword hard rules always win.** The list includes at minimum: family, medical, health, vault, trust, estate, will, children, and the names of family members. A hit routes to Arthur regardless of anything else and is logged.
2. **Eleanor's resident classifier decides everything the keywords miss.** Example of why this is needed: "my mother's company needs a contract" trips "family" but is corporate work; the classifier can flag it for Ren with Arthur's privacy tags attached.
3. **Task-force detection.** Any cross-domain request triggers the matching task-force preset (Section 8.3). This is mandatory, not advisory.
4. **Manual overrides** are short prefixes typed at the start of a message. The set is fixed in configuration and logged.
5. **Every routing decision is logged** with the reason, so the Principal can see why a message went where it went.

### 7.3 What isolation actually is — AGREED, stated plainly

On one machine with sequential engines, the separation between Ren and Arthur is logical, not physical. It is enforced by separate memory collections, separate context windows, the router's hard rules, and the outbound gate. It is not enforced by separate hardware. The brief says this honestly rather than implying otherwise.

The tags decide default routing; they delete nothing.

### 8.4 JIT domain injection — NEW, size-bounded

Domains are knowledge, not agents. A domain never spawns a model. It is injected into the running director's system prompt for one dispatch. Because prompt processing is this machine's weakest capability, injection is bounded:

1. Each of the thirty-six domain profiles is stored in two forms: the full seven-field profile (reference, retrievable) and a compact card of roughly 300 to 500 tokens (injected). The card carries the strategic frame, the compliance list for Australia, the cognitive method, and the tooling names.
2. At most three domain cards per dispatch. A task force preset names which cards it pulls; a request that would need more than three is split into a relay (Section 8.5).
3. Cards are appended after the static persona and governance layers so the cached prefix survives (Section 4.4).
4. Tier C cards load only on an explicit match, never speculatively.
5. The full profile is available to the director through a retrieval tool if a card proves insufficient mid-task, which costs one retrieval, not a re-prefill of everything.

**How a seat is reached (v0.4.0, S-29; D-37 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register).** A domain card never selects an engine. An engine is reached only through the director's binding (default and override list) or a task-force preset's `engine` field, and loading a card never switches it: every extra engine is a swap of about 50 to 85 seconds plus re-prefill (5.4), so binding engines to cards could turn one three-card dispatch into three swaps. On Day 1 only TF_OMEGA carries an engine field, `deepseek-v4-flash`, which must also be in the dispatching director's binding (Ren's and Arthur's). Every other preset runs on its owner's default: GLM-5.3-Flash, except Valerie's four presets, whose default is unchanged by this revision. The specialist seats arrive the same way when they are pulled (phased, 5.1): Ling-3.0-flash-Fin on TF_GAMMA, TF_DELTA and TF_EPSILON and as a Silas override; Qwen3-Coder-Next on TF_MU; Baichuan-M3-235B and Intern-S1 on TF_UPSILON. Each preset's notes in `task-forces.json` name them now. No prefix is added for them; one is added only for a seat the Principal asks to call directly.

### 8.5 How a task force executes — AGREED

The director is the sole lead and the sole inference session for a task force. Other directors join only as a sequential relay, one loaded engine at a time. There is no parallel swarm; the hardware cannot host one and the governance model does not want one.

**Dispatch sequence:**

1. The router or an explicit command identifies the task force.
2. The orchestrator resolves the owning director, or directors for APEX and dual-owner task forces.
3. The domain cards named by the preset are compiled into that director's prompt (Section 8.4).
4. The Engine Arbiter loads the director's engine if it is not resident.
5. The director executes, calling tools as the domain requires.
6. If the preset spans more than one director, the output, never the live context, relays to the next director as its own dispatch with its own task ID and log entry.
7. The result reports to Ren or Arthur for synthesis and the approval-tier check.

**Worked example, TF_ALPHA.** Gideon loads with the M&A and Legal cards, drafts the structuring and risk analysis. His output relays to Silas, who loads with the CFO and Quant cards and runs valuation through the code interpreter. Silas's output relays to Ren, who synthesises and puts the brief through the gate. Three loads, three logged steps, one answer. *v0.4.0:* Gideon, Silas and Ren are all on GLM-5.3-Flash, so the same relay is three dispatches on one load, with no swap when GLM is already resident (6.3).

The task-force rule matters operationally: a single-director task force is one load and fast; a genuinely cross-domain one costs a relay. The orchestrator chains only the directors whose domain the request touches, never the whole board.

### 8.6 Agentic delegation — RESOLVED (C10)

The domain profiles' original line, "Stateless MRTR, dispatch payload and decouple," is replaced in all thirty-six by:

> Isolated-context dispatch. Spawns with a clean, task-scoped context; reports completion or failure to the spawning director under a task ID; subject to the same tiered approval and Ouroboros logging as any other action.

The stateless half is kept: clean context per dispatch. The decoupled half is removed: nothing fires without a return path, because a decoupled action cannot be approved, cannot be logged as a strike, and would leave incomplete work on the Principal.
