## 9. Autonomous protocols

### 9.1 Deep Think (adversarial optimisation) — AGREED, corrected

Trigger: `[DEEP THINK: problem]` or the router's task-weight estimate. Eleanor's classifier picks a depth (up to standard since v0.4.0: the deep tier needs the Principal's prefix, D-43, below); the Principal can force any depth with a prefix. Nothing is hard-capped; a task declared heavy runs as long as it needs.

| Depth | Shape | Engine swaps | Typical time |
|---|---|---|---|
| Quick | Generator and adversary on the same loaded engine with different role prompts (v0.4.0: whichever LLM seat is resident, normally GLM-5.3-Flash) | 0 | 1 to 2 min |
| Standard | Ren generates three trajectories on GLM-5.3-Flash, Arthur scores on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, Ren refines the winner on GLM | 2 | 5 to 8 min, of which about 1.5 to 3 min is swapping before any re-prefill (INFERRED) |
| Deep | Standard plus a second expansion and scoring round, a third opinion on documents (gpt-oss-120b on Day 1, standing in for the phased Qwen3.8-Flash-Next) and **the Apex engine (DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731) delivering the final synthesis** | 3 to 4 | 15 to 30 min, of which about 2.5 to 5.5 min is swapping before any re-prefill (INFERRED); re-prefill on DeepSeek can add minutes per swap (5.4) |

**Corrections adopted:** the adversary runs at low temperature, not zero (Nemotron in v0.3.5, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 since v0.4.0), because reasoning models loop at exactly zero. Scores out of 100 are rubric-based judgements with named criteria, not mathematics; where a real figure exists, Silas computes it through the code interpreter and Arthur scores against the computed number. Phases are batched, never round-by-round ping-pong between engines.

*v0.4.0 (S-43; D-22 option (a), D-43 option (a) and D-47 option (b), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* the Deep Think pair is GLM-5.3-Flash, the generator and Ren's engine, and DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, the adversary and synthesis engine and Arthur's: two families and two training recipes, for only the swap latency, since every engine runs alone (4.2 rules 3 and 8). v0.3.5 ran gpt-oss-120b against Nemotron 3 Super. The deep tier's third opinion on documents runs on gpt-oss-120b on Day 1; Qwen3.8-Flash-Next takes it when it is pulled (phased, 5.1). The Arbiter's tier lists name the same engines, so a tier that cannot fit is downgraded before any load (4.2 rule 8).

**Deep-tier latency (D-43 option (a)).** The deep tier's 3 to 4 swaps cost about 2.5 to 5.5 minutes before any re-prefill, so a deep run is reserved for the Principal's explicit acceptance; the `[DEEP THINK:DEEP]` prefix is that acceptance. Built in v0.4.0: when the classifier chooses the deep tier, through its task-weight estimate or for a `[DEEP THINK: ...]` that types no depth, the router runs it at the standard tier and records `deep-think:deep->standard(D-43: explicit acceptance required)` in the routing ledger; only the typed `[DEEP THINK:DEEP]` reaches the deep route (`atlas/router.py`).

**Deep-tier speed floor (D-47 option (b)).** The Principal sets a minimum decode rate for the deep tier before the V4.1 trial (5.1), and the same floor sets the per-seat floors of V10 (D-33 (c)); the trial measures both greedy decoding and the node's temperature 1.0. No floor is set yet. The stated default is none: Phase 3 records each seat's rate, gates none on speed, and raises the to-do `decode-floors` while any seat has no floor (Section 1). At the projections the deep tier's adversary and synthesis engine decodes about 7 tok/s (5.1).

### 9.2 Cross-checking — AGREED

Any answer involving numbers, legal claims, or code can be verified by a second persona on a different engine. Cheap version: the currently loaded engine with a critic prompt. Strong version: an engine swap. The router applies the strong version automatically to the sensitive tier and to anything the Principal marks important.

*v0.4.0 (S-31; D-39 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* every engine that is not uncensored may serve a strong cross-check, the Apex engine included. v0.3.5 never used DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 for one because it was exclusive; it is now Arthur's default and, on Day 1, the only other family beside GLM-5.3-Flash. The checker is the other hemisphere's lead on the first of his engines that differs from the draft's: a corporate draft on GLM is judged by Arthur on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, an estate draft on GLM by Ren on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, and Arthur's own drafts by Ren on GLM. Minerva's medical drafts are judged by Arthur on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, so the health data stays in the estate hemisphere; her planned checker, Baichuan-M3-235B, is phased (5.1). The abliterated engine never checks, as in v0.3.5. A strong check is now a swap of about 50 to 85 seconds plus re-prefill (5.4) unless the checking engine is already resident. Batching strong checks by engine is phased with the swap batching (6.3), with its to-do and event; on Day 1 each strong check swaps on its own.

### 9.3 Sentinel Protocol (autonomous monitoring) — AGREED, corrected

| Element | Design |
|---|---|
| Schedule | systemd timer, hourly. **RESOLVED (C11):** not a WSL2 cron; there is no WSL |
| Feeds | Outbound allowlist only. **CLOSED (D6):** CoinDesk, an index feed for ASX and US markets, RSS news, node telemetry |
| Detection | Statistics first: deviations against recent history, thresholds. A model is invoked only when a threshold trips |
| Engine | Eleanor's resident router model writes every BLUF, as built. *v0.4.0 (S-06, D-12):* an escalation goes to the resident LLM seat first, and to gpt-oss-120b, the Sentinel escalation engine, only when it is the resident seat: Sentinel never forces a swap, and never swaps an engine out from under an active session; the Engine Arbiter queues it. No Day 1 code escalates past the router |
| Owners | Alaric for threats, Silas for markets, under Arthur. **RESOLVED (C12):** not Ren |
| Alert | Structured BLUF entry to the log and a push to the Principal's phone through self-hosted ntfy over WireGuard, email fallback |
| Idle | The resident engine stays loaded. "Sleep to save VRAM" does not apply; idle inference costs nothing and unloading would cost a swap |

### 9.4 Ouroboros Protocol (self-healing) — AGREED, refined

| Element | Design |
|---|---|
| Strike input | Manual `[LOG STRIKE: error_name]` **and** automatic: failed tool call, rejected draft (a rejected configuration proposal, once Domain 20 submits them through the queue, is a `rejected-draft` strike like any other rejected draft, so the Principal's retention preferences are learned without any rule change; S53), failing sandbox test, overridden routing decision |
| Storage | A dedicated scar collection in the Vector Cortex; each scar tagged to persona and domain, holding context, error, and the correction that worked |
| Injection | Before any task, the closest few scars above a similarity threshold are injected (layer 4, Section 4.4). Not all scars; a pile of irrelevant corrections degrades quality within months. *v0.4.0:* the threshold, a distance ceiling of 0.9, was chosen for bge-m3 and is not re-derived for Qwen3-Embedding-4B (10.1); to-do: tune it on the node against real scars (T-15) |
| Curation | The Principal can review and retire scars. Scars are exempt from the 72-hour general pruning sweep (Section 9.6) |
| Boundary | Scars change behaviour through retrieved guidance. Any change to ATLAS's own code or configuration is a proposal requiring the Principal's approval (Section 16.3) |
| Guarantee | Injected guidance raises the odds of avoiding a repeat; verification (tests, cross-checks) is what guarantees it. The brief states both |

### 9.5 AEGIS Backup Protocol (disaster recovery) — AGREED, corrected

| Element | Design |
|---|---|
| Tool | restic: incremental, deduplicated, AES-256, integrity-checked. **RESOLVED (C13):** not a zip |
| Schedule | Nightly automatic plus the manual `[EXECUTE AEGIS BACKUP]` trigger |
| Freeze | The orchestrator pauses the Celery queues, ChromaDB and the graph store take consistent snapshots, then writes resume |
| Contents | Vector Cortex, graph store, scars, workspace, sandbox, orchestrator code and configuration, Open WebUI database, the vault as-is (still encrypted, never opened) |
| Excluded | Model and engine weights (re-downloadable; would add 400+ GB per snapshot). A manifest of exact model versions and checksums is backed up instead |
| Destination | The 4 TB second drive. **RESOLVED (C14):** a copy on the same 8 TB drive is not disaster recovery. Optional rotating external encrypted drive for off-site |
| Retention | 30 nightly, 12 monthly (**D9 closed**) |
| Passphrase | Off-node, with the LUKS recovery key (D3). A backup whose passphrase died with the machine is useless |
| Restore test | Quarterly restore to a scratch directory, verified by checksum, logged |

### 9.6 Semantic graph pruning — AGREED, scoped

A sweep every 72 hours over both memory layers (Section 10), enqueued by a systemd timer and run by Celery on the `cpu` queue (S23, S53; VERIFIED in the code at this commit, nothing has run on the node): `atlas-prune.timer` fires daily at 03:10 (plus up to 30 minutes of random delay) and `atlas-prune.service` enqueues the sweep only when its stamp is older than 71 hours, so the cadence is at least 71 hours and survives reboots. The stamp is touched after the enqueue succeeds, not after the sweep finishes. Permanent facts stay anchored. What goes is temporal: a document its writer marked temporal whose expiry has passed or, when the writer gave no expiry, one older than `PRUNE_WINDOW_HOURS` (72; a visible setting in `orchestrator.env` since S53, Appendix B). That key is a fallback only: both temporal writers today set their own TTL (raw chat turns 90 days, Sentinel BLUFs 366 days, D9), so it governs no current writer, and the cadence is the timer, not the key. Temporal operational data, for example a mutex notification from Tuesday, is removed from the active graph and vector collections and compressed into a dated archive under `/srv/cold` on the second drive (`prune/prune-<UTC stamp>.tar.zst`, read back and verified before anything is deleted): vector documents with their text and metadata and, since S53, graph documents with their text, so a pruned note of either kind can be restored (no restore command exists; one is built only if the Principal ever asks for a pruned note back, S53). Expired raw chat turns are the one exception: purged without archive, because D9 keeps the summary and an archive under `/srv/cold` would sit inside restic's include set (10.4). Scars are exempt (Section 9.4). Vault-tagged content is never written to memory in the first place (Section 10.5), so it is never pruned or archived; one found in memory is a breach: strike, phone alert, deletion without archive. A sweep that raises or finishes with errors pushes one ntfy line to the Principal (S53): because the stamp records the enqueue, such a sweep is not retried before the next 71-hour slot, and the alert is what makes it visible; the ledger's task row and the journal hold the full error. Retention changes are proposals under 16.3 item 6; the sweep never tunes itself (the measurement layer and the proposal path are phased items, S53).

### 9.7 Celery Shadow Broker — AGREED, with one correction

Redis and Celery in the Docker core. Long CPU-bound work, a multi-hour backtest, a GraphRAG indexing pass, a Docling ingestion of a document set, runs as a Celery task on the CPU workers, and the chat interface returns to the Principal immediately. Celery also carries AEGIS's schedule, Sentinel's pulse, Ouroboros's automatic strikes, and the 72-hour pruning sweep, replacing separate schedulers.

**Queues:** a `cpu` queue with many workers (min(8, nproc − 2) prefork children, at least 1, since S55); a `gpu` queue with exactly one worker, which requests engines through the Engine Arbiter. **Correction (C15):** Celery decouples CPU work from the interface. It does not put two things on the GPU at once. A background task that calls an LLM partway through still queues behind the resident engine.

---

## 10. Memory

### 10.1 Vector Cortex (ChromaDB) — AGREED

Local ChromaDB with a local embedding model (D4). Collections: `corporate`, `estate`, `scars`, `documents_corporate`, `documents_estate`, `sentinel`. Each collection is bound to a hemisphere; the router's hard rules decide which collections a dispatch may read. Ingestion runs through Docling (Section 15.1) for PDFs, Office documents, and scanned material, producing structured chunks with page and table provenance so that Gideon's and Minerva's citations point at a page.

*v0.4.0 (S-41; D-45 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* the embedding model is Qwen3-Embedding-4B on the CPU (5.3), adopted before Day 1 so that the collections are first indexed with it and no re-embed is ever needed; vectors from two embedders cannot be compared, even at the same dimension. The six collections keep their 1024-dimension schema: the server returns 2,560 dimensions, and the orchestrator's client keeps the first 1,024 and re-normalises them (Matryoshka truncation). Queries, never stored documents, carry the model card's instruction prefix (`Instruct: <task>` then `Query:`); the task text is `EMBEDDING_QUERY_INSTRUCTION` in `memory.env`, written once, so a change the Principal makes survives a re-run of the step. The collection metadata names the model and the truncation. Phase 2 step 4 checks that the native vector has at least 1,024 dimensions and that a query's vector differs from the same text embedded as a passage. It stops rather than mix vectors if a collection was created for another embedder: such a collection is deleted while the corpus is still empty (Chroma 1.5.9 never compacts, R27). The scar threshold set for bge-m3 is retuned on the node (9.4, to-do).

### 10.2 Graph layer (GraphRAG) — AGREED, additive

A second memory layer answering relationship questions, "what else touches this contract, this company, this person," which vector similarity cannot answer. It supplements ChromaDB; it does not replace it. **RISK R6:** Microsoft's GraphRAG indexing makes many LLM calls per document and would be slow here. Indexing runs as a Celery `gpu` task during idle periods using Eleanor's resident model for extraction, escalating to a large engine only for documents the Principal marks important (*v0.4.0, S-06:* that large engine is the resident LLM seat, normally GLM-5.3-Flash, so the escalation costs no swap; as built, LightRAG's extraction model is the router alone, `LLM_MODEL=router-qwen3.5-4b`, and no Day 1 code escalates it). Its vectors come from the same embedder, in the document form and truncated the same way (10.1, S-41). **CLOSED (D7):** LightRAG, chosen over Microsoft's GraphRAG for its lighter indexing on this hardware.

### 10.3 Scar collection — see 9.4.

### 10.4 Retention — CLOSED (D9)

Open WebUI keeps its own chat database, so ChromaDB supplements rather than literally replaces chat logs. The binding rule: chats are retained 90 days in Open WebUI, then summarised into the Vector Cortex and purged; operational logs 30 days hot on the second drive, then archived; Sentinel logs 12 months; scars permanent under curation; backups per Section 9.5. Vault sessions are never retained (Section 10.5).

As implemented (S53, VERIFIED at this commit): the D9 values are code constants that no proposal may treat as tunable: the raw chat-turn TTL (90 days, `atlas.api`), the Sentinel TTL (366 days, `atlas.tasks.sentinel`) and the ledger's 30-day window (`atlas.ledger`); a proposal to change one is a request to reopen D9 and is labelled so, never scheduled as a tuning step. Open WebUI's own 90 days is a second, independent value, `OPENWEBUI_CHAT_RETENTION_DAYS=90` in `orchestrator.env`, applied by the nightly 04:15 chat-retention task that summarises each expired chat into the Vector Cortex and the graph and then deletes it (Appendix B). The ledger's 30-day purge of its operational rows (`Ledger.purge_expired`, which refuses to run without an archive callback) is written but not yet wired into the sweep: a phased item (S53, R26); until it is, those tables grow.

### 10.5 Vault and memory — RESOLVED (C16)

An earlier version placed Arthur's estate memory collection inside the vault. The vault was then simplified to "top-secret files, opened on command, locked when not in use." A memory store inside a folder that is locked most of the time would leave Arthur without memory most of the time. Resolution:

- Estate memory lives on the always-mounted LUKS data volume like everything else. It is protected at rest by LUKS and in operation by the router's hard rules.
- The vault holds top-secret files only. Anything read from the vault is tagged `vault` for the life of that session; vault-tagged content is not written to any memory collection, not summarised, and not backed up outside the vault itself, unless the Principal explicitly says "remember this."

### 10.6 Backups — see 9.5. Passphrases and recovery keys are the Principal's responsibility off-node (D3).

---

## 11. Vault — AGREED, simplified

An encrypted gocryptfs folder under `/srv/atlas/vault` (ciphertext in `vault/cipher`, which is backed up; plaintext mounted at `vault/open` only while unlocked, never backed up), on top of the LUKS data volume. Opened by a button in the interface that prompts for the passphrase directly; the passphrase never passes through a model or a chat message. Locked by command and auto-locked after 15 minutes idle (**D13 closed**). No RAM scrubbing, no forensic claims; those were removed from the design at the Principal's instruction. Contents are backed up as ciphertext only. Session handling of vault content is in Section 10.5.

---

## 12. Interface and access

### 12.1 Open WebUI — AGREED

Open WebUI is the face, not the brain. It runs in Docker, handles chat, voice, and file upload (installing it as a home-screen app needs TLS on the WireGuard address, a Day 2 addition, Section 23 S27), and presents the orchestrator as a single model named A.T.L.A.S. Ren and Arthur are additionally exposed as direct models for when the Principal wants one hemisphere alone. The 4-Way Router's Filter function relays every prompt to the orchestrator (Section 7.1).

**Offline hardening, mandatory:** offline mode and Hugging Face offline flags set; update checks, community sharing, web search, and any external embedding fetch disabled; speech settings pointed at local Kokoro and Whisper; chat retention per D9. Without this, the node quietly is not zero-cloud.

**Alternatives considered and rejected:** AnythingLLM (no prompt-intercept hook), LibreChat (heavier for one user), Lobe Chat (weaker offline story), a custom app (months before parity).

### 12.2 Remote access — AGREED

| Element | State |
|---|---|
| VPN | WG-Easy in Docker. Generates the phone QR code and laptop config. Admin page bound to LAN and WireGuard interfaces only, never internet-facing |
| Router | UDP 51820 forwarded to the node. **Done by the Principal.** |
| Domain | `sovereign-node.link` on Cloudflare. **Done by the Principal.** |
| DNS record | `vpn.sovereign-node.link` A record, maintained by a dynamic-DNS updater on the node through a scoped Cloudflare token, checked every few minutes, updated only on change. Client configs reference the hostname so they never need reissuing |
| CGNAT | **VERIFY V5:** the port-forward succeeding implies a routable public address, but confirm the node is reachable from mobile data before relying on it |
| Notifications | Self-hosted ntfy on the node, phone app subscribed over WireGuard; email fallback |
| Admin | SSH and Cockpit over LAN or WireGuard from the Principal's Windows PC |

### 12.3 Cloudflare token — CLOSED, automated in Phase 2 step 6b

The token is currently in a plain-text file named `CLOUDFLARE.txt`. The Principal asked for this to be automated rather than done by hand, and it is: Phase 2 step 6b performs it and V23 proves it. The specification the script implements is to create a scoped API Token with `Zone:DNS:Edit` on `sovereign-node.link` only, never the Global API Key; store it in an environment file with mode 600 owned by the updater's service account, outside `/srv/atlas`, outside any git-tracked path, and outside restic's include set; delete the plain-text file. The token value has not been shared in this conversation, so no rotation is required, only relocation.

### 12.4 The Windows PC — AGREED; mount deferred to the live ATLAS (v0.3.3)

Accessed on demand only, with write capability, as a shared folder the node mounts when a task needs it and releases afterwards. No software installed on the PC. Most of the Principal's data lives in cloud services (Section 13), so the PC is a minor source. It is not a server. **Day 1 scope (v0.3.3):** on the Principal's instruction the share is not configured on Day 1; Phase 2 step 9 is skipped while `WINDOWS_SHARE` is blank and the item sits on the live ATLAS to-do list (`input-windows-share`), to be picked up with the Principal once the node is running.

### 12.5 Trust zones and outbound allowlist — AGREED

| Zone | Members | Reach |
|---|---|---|
| Node | ATLAS services | Everything internal |
| LAN | Windows PC, home devices | Interface, SSH, Cockpit, ntfy, file share |
| WireGuard | Principal's phone and laptop | Same as LAN |
| Internet, inbound | Only UDP 51820 | WireGuard handshake only |
| Internet, outbound | Firewall allowlist | Google APIs, Xero, Cloudflare API, Wix, Pay.com, RewardPay, Sentinel feeds, package mirrors during builds, Hugging Face during model pulls, Canonical's NTS time servers (chrony only, v0.3.4). Everything else denied and logged |

*v0.4.0 (S-49; D-53 option (b), D-55 option (b) and B-69, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* the allowlist does not change for Day 1. The build-time hosts S29 added (Meta's weight host, the OpenAI tokenizer host and the Playwright download hosts) are confirmed (B-69). The DeepSeek V4.1 trial's build sources are recorded here only, because the trial is phased after Day 1 (15.5): antirez/ds4 and Luce-Org/lucebox on GitHub, kyuz0's ROCm images on Docker Hub, and AMD's ROCm repository. They need no new host (`github.com`, `codeload.github.com`, `registry-1.docker.io`, `auth.docker.io`, `production.cloudfront.docker.com` and `stable.repo.amd.com` are listed), except `repo.radeon.com` if the trial image installs ROCm from AMD's apt repository. They are admitted for the trial's builds only, by the Principal's approval under 16.3 item 8, and the trial runs offline; Hugging Face pulls are already allowed. squid has no time window, so the admission is enforced by a logged reload of the allowlist before and after each trial build. To-do: that reload step, with the trial's build script (S-51). The event: the trial starts after Day 1, with D-46, D-47 and D-51 to D-54 answered. Lucebox's appliance software (its updater, cloud fallback, crash reporting, Tailscale and Connect) is never installed (D-55, 15.5).

## 13. Integrations

| Service | Path | Capability | Approval |
|---|---|---|---|
| Google Workspace and personal Gmail | Gmail and Calendar APIs, one-time OAuth per account via a Principal-owned Google Cloud project | Read, send, label, calendar both ways. Each account tagged corporate or estate for the router | Per message tier (Section 16.2) |
| Google Drive | Mounted as a folder on the node (rclone), both accounts | Read and write, appears as local storage to every persona | Writes to shared folders: standard tier |
| Xero | Official API plus Xero's own MCP tool server | Ledgers, invoices, contacts, bank feeds, reports as structured data for Silas | Any posting or payment: sensitive tier |
| Cloudflare | Full API, scoped token | DNS, domains, security settings | All changes: standard tier, DNS for sovereign-node.link itself: sensitive |
| Wix | Partial API for content and business data, browser for site design | Helena updates content by API, design changes through the browser | Standard tier |
| Pay.com | Developer API for payment data | Reading and reporting | Any payment action: sensitive tier, two-factor stays on the Principal's phone |
| RewardPay | Browser only, no public API found | Agent-driven browser with the Principal approving each action | Sensitive tier, two-factor stays on the Principal's phone |
| Anything without an API | Local browser automation, Playwright text-based DOM reading, UI-TARS-1.5-7B for vision-driven GUI work (15.2; corrected in v0.4.0, B-11: UI-TARS 2.0 has no open weights and stays on the watch-list, 15.5) | Works with every engine in the set | Per action tier |

APIs are preferred over the browser wherever they exist: faster, unaffected by page redesigns, and they deliver structured data. Money never moves without the Principal; two-factor prompts are never automated.

**Day 1 scope (v0.3.2):** Day 1 installs the Google OAuth tokens and proves Gmail, Calendar and Drive are reachable (V20); the Xero, Cloudflare, Wix, Pay.com and RewardPay connectors and the outbound send channels behind the approval gate are Day 2 work. On Day 1 the approval queue holds, logs and approves exactly as Section 16.2 requires, and an approved item reports "no send channel configured" rather than sending (V15 proves the gate logic with a stub sender; Section 23 S26).

Each candidate's licence is accepted with the candidate as it is approved (D-35 option (a)).

### 14.2 What makes a voice human — AGREED

The reference audio, not the model. Cloning from five to thirty seconds of a real recording with the target quality is the single largest factor. Prosody control (pauses, emphasis, tone shifts) and a small amount of natural imperfection carried over from the source are the next two. Kokoro reads flat by design and is right for routine lines; Chatterbox is the layer for personas that must carry character.

Kokoro ships 54 named presets; the English set is 11 American female, 9 American male, 4 British female, 4 British male. Matching below is by name and register only; nobody has heard these presets against the descriptions yet. V7 is a listening test once Phase 2 is up.

| Alaric Stone | Male. Grounded, authoritative, gravelly veteran security director | No preset delivers gravel | Chatterbox clone from a reference recording |
| Minerva Hale | Female. Calm, analytical, articulate, clinical | af_kore, alternate bf_isabella | Kokoro preset |
| Victor Vale | Male. Crisp, rapid, flawlessly polite, aviation concierge | bm_lewis, alternate bm_daniel | Kokoro preset |
| Gideon Vance | Measured, meticulous, deep, litigator in absolute facts | bm_george if Arthur takes bm_daniel, else am_onyx reassigned | Kokoro preset or Chatterbox clone to separate from Arthur |

**Known conflict:** Arthur, Victor, and Gideon all pull toward the same four British male presets. Once heard, expect to spread them apart or move one to a Chatterbox clone, as Alaric is.

### 14.4 Vision — AGREED, policy

ATLAS sees on demand. The Principal, or a defined trigger, says "look now"; one frame is sampled through the vision engine, GLM-5.3-Flash with its glm5v projector on its own unit (v0.4.0; D-20 option (a), D17 reopening D10, C29), so a look costs no swap while GLM-5.3-Flash, the default resident seat, is loaded (5.1, 6.3). Continuous video inference is not a default: at any real frame rate it would compete for the GPU against everything else and break the sequential design, and an always-on camera feed is a privacy commitment that deserves an explicit decision. Continuous monitoring exists only as a separately enabled capability under Alaric's estate-security domain, tied to specific cameras, with SAM 2 for tracking, and it is logged like any other sensitive action.

## 15. Multimodal engines and tools

Two categories. **Tools** are CPU-bound or lightweight, always available, installed in Phase 2. **Engines** carry weights, compete for memory, load through the Engine Arbiter, and are built in Phase 4 inside ROCm containers (Section 3.4). *v0.4.0 (S-46; D-50 option (a)):* no Phase 4 engine runs beside an LLM seat: a Phase 4 load evicts the resident seat, which reloads on its next request, and Phase 4 tests each engine alone (4.2).

### 15.1 Phase 2 tools — AGREED, all confirmed real and GPU-independent

| Tool | Job | Owner |

| IfcOpenShell and Bonsai (Blender BIM) | IFC parsing, authoring, geometric clash detection | Domain 8, Valerie |
| MCP4IFC | MCP server for LLM-driven parametric IFC creation and editing, this is the "Arch-DiT / BIM-GPT" capability | Domain 8, Valerie |
| Radiance | Physically accurate lighting and daylighting simulation for real coordinates and time, the photometric half of "Lumina-PBR" | Domain 8, Valerie |
| OpenStudio and EnergyPlus | HVAC and building-energy simulation, the fluid-dynamics half of "DeepRoute-AEC" | Domain 8 and 9, Valerie |
| Python via code interpreter | Voltage drop and other formula engineering, all of Silas's arithmetic | All directors |
| KiCad CLI | PCB design, DRC, export, driven through its Python API by the coding engine | Domain 29, Valerie |
| Docling | Document conversion for PDFs, Office files, scans into structured chunks with tables and page provenance | Ingestion for every domain |
| Cross-platform build service | Android NDK plus Gradle for .apk, MinGW-w64 for .exe, containerised. This is the "OmniBuild-KVM" capability, Android and Windows only | Domain 1 and 5, Valerie |
| Playwright and browser automation | Text-based DOM browsing for platforms without an API | All directors, per tier |

*v0.4.0 (S-34; D-41 option (a) and D-42 option (b), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* v0.3.5's seven-engine set (gpt-oss-120b and its twin 126 GB, Nemotron 3 Super 120–123 GB, Qwen3.5-122B-A10B 130 GB, DeepSeek V4 Flash `UD-Q4_K_XL` 155 GB, Qwen2.5-VL-72B 79 GB, Meditron-70B ~74 GB) gives way to the Day 1 set above. Only the core seats are pulled in Phase 3; the phased engines follow in a post-Day-1 pull with the same checksum and load tests. No file is joined any more, so Meditron's 74 GB transient copy is gone and Phase 3's free-space check is the download plus 5%. Model weights stay out of the backups, and the manifest of versions and checksums is backed up instead (9.5).

Download time over the Principal's Wi-Fi at roughly 100 Mbps, the planning figure until the Principal states a measured one (B-27, Section 22): about 10 hours for the Day 1 Phase 3 pull (v0.3.5: about 15), minutes for Phase 2's companions, about 19 hours more for the phased engines at their post-Day-1 pull, and about 37 hours for everything but Phase 4 (about 1.7 TB), which is why Phase 3 pulls only the core seats. The Phase 4 engines take several more hours (about 40 to 42 hours in all). All of it is less predictable than a wired link. Both phases run detached and resumable, so this costs time, not attention.

### 15.5 Engine watch-list — considered, not adopted (additions in v0.4.0)

Engines the Principal has asked about that fail a standing rule today. Each carries the rule it fails and the single event that would bring it in. Nothing on this list is built on Day 1; ATLAS re-checks the list when a later phase adds engines. *v0.4.0 (S-52, S-56 and S-57; D-24 option (a), D-46 option (b), D-55 option (b), D-61 and D-64 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* the standing rules now include Section 1's arsenal rules (the 70B floor, each engine alone in the budget, quality before speed, the least-quantised rung that fits). ## 16. Governance

### 16.1 Standing rules — AGREED

1. The Principal speaks only to Ren or Arthur. Shadow Cabinet output is never surfaced directly; the hemisphere synthesises and speaks.
2. Directors correspond externally under their own identities for routine and standard matters; Ren and Arthur are reserved for matters that warrant them.
3. Every outbound action passes the approval gate in code (16.2). This is a hard code path, not a prompt instruction.
4. ATLAS never discloses its AI nature externally. Recorded as a legal exposure in R12; the approval gate is the control.
5. ATLAS never delegates work back to the Principal. A rewrite pass removes any task-shaped request to the Principal; only decisions and approvals may be asked.
6. Register differs between Principal and external recipients (6.4).
7. Cross-domain requests must trigger their task-force preset (8.3).
8. Money never moves without the Principal. Two-factor prompts are never automated.
9. *v0.4.0 (S-50; D-56 options (a) and (b), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* routed engines use only lossless accelerations: the default expert count, speculative decoding with exact verification, and prefix caching. An approximate switch (a per-box router bias, a bounded sliding-window replay in the decoder, sparse prefill) is allowed only inside the V4.1 trial, each scored against the same file without it; vendor speed settings that change results, such as running fewer experts, never.
10. *v0.4.0 (S-13):* every engine is admitted under Section 1's arsenal rules: no LLM seat under about 70B total parameters, each engine alone with the whole engine budget, quality before speed, and the least-quantised rung that fits, with its measured quality cost stated.

### 16.2 Approval tiers — AGREED

| Tier | Examples | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| Routine | Meeting scheduling, confirmations, acknowledgements | Pre-approved categories, sent automatically, logged for review |
| Standard | Replies with substance, requests, negotiations, content updates, DNS changes; configuration proposals from Domain 20 (retention, window, maintenance; S53), submitted by a hemisphere lead in the Principal register | Drafted, held until the Principal approves. For a configuration proposal the approval records the decision and the Principal applies the change as root (16.3 item 6); ATLAS applies nothing |
| Sensitive | Legal, financial, medical, estate, security, any payment, anything under a sensitive-tier task force | Drafted, held, flagged with the director's reasoning; strong cross-check applied automatically |

The queue lives in the interface; nothing external executes until the Principal taps approve. A push notification through ntfy announces items waiting. The configuration-proposal kind itself is not built on Day 1: S53 records it as a phased item and names the event that brings it in.

### 16.3 What ATLAS may never do without the Principal — CLOSED (D8)

Accepted by the Principal and binding:

1. Move money, initiate a payment, or change a payment method.
2. Sign, accept, or bind to a contract or terms.
3. Send external correspondence above the routine tier.
4. Change DNS, domain, or Cloudflare security settings.
5. Delete, move, or modify vault contents or backups.
6. Modify its own code, configuration, approval tiers, router rules, or the allowlist. Domain 20 proposes; the Principal approves.
7. Enable continuous vision or audio capture.
8. Install software or pull models from outside the allowlist.
9. Share any Principal data with a third party not already connected under Section 13.
10. Create new external identities, accounts, or mailboxes.

*v0.4.0 (S-49, S-56; D-53 option (b), D-55 option (b), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* item 8 governs the phased V4.1 trial. Its build sources come in only by the Principal's approval at the time of the build (12.5). Of Lucebox's software only its Apache 2.0 engine may ever be built, from a pinned commit inside the trial container, and only if the trial reaches its second stage; the appliance stack is never installed (15.5).

### 16.4 Sandbox — AGREED

The AEGIS Sandbox runs code under an operating-system-level cap: a container with a hard memory limit, CPU quota, no network unless the task's tier grants it, and a timeout. Arthur's code review is a second layer over this cap, not the guard itself.

### 16.5 Legal flags — recorded, not argued

The brief keeps its rules. These are recorded so the Principal decides with eyes open:

- Fictitious directors corresponding externally under their own names, combined with non-disclosure of AI nature, can engage misleading-conduct provisions of the Australian Consumer Law and disclosure expectations in some professional contexts. Gideon's sensitive-tier check and the approval gate are the controls.
- The Privacy Act 1988 reforms of 2024 to 2026 raise obligations around automated decisions touching individuals; Arthur's division holds family and health data and should treat every external disclosure as sensitive-tier.
- macOS virtualisation on non-Apple hardware was considered and is excluded; the Apple build path is removed from the design entirely (D11 closed).
- *v0.4.0 (S-27; D-03 to D-05, D-34 option (b), D-35 option (a), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* each Day 1 engine's licence is read and recorded in its `engines.json` entry, and each phased engine's licence is accepted with that engine as it is approved. None of them is research-only. The Qwen Community License 1.0 of Qwen3.8-Flash-Next asks for the model's name to be displayed above set user and revenue thresholds; ATLAS's internal use is exempt (D-03). Qwen3-Coder-Next's Apache 2.0 and Ling-3.0-flash-Fin's MIT are stated on their cards only, with no licence file in the repository. MedGemma 1.5 4B's HAI-DEF terms are accepted only if the Principal adds it (15.2, C-12).

## 17. Day 1 Execution Protocol

**Implemented by `scripts/day1/`** (entry point `atlas-day1.sh`; `README.md` there gives the commands in order, the prompts and what to have ready; `COVERAGE.md` maps every step and every V item below to its file). Four phases. One entry command per phase. Every phase is idempotent: re-running skips what is complete. Every phase writes a log and ends with a printed pass/fail table. Phases 3 and 4 run detached under systemd so a dropped SSH session cannot kill them, and both are resumable at the file level.

**Policy v0.3.3 — a missing input from the Principal never stops a phase.** Whatever a step needs from the Principal (a token, an SSH key, a Google account, an OAuth client file, a voice recording, a licence acceptance, a test from the phone or the Windows PC) is asked for once, in plain words with an example, and may be skipped. A skipped item is recorded as `deferred` in the verification table (never `fail`), written to the to-do list at `/var/lib/atlas/day1/todo.jsonl` (shown by `atlas-day1.sh status`) with the exact command that completes it later, and the phase continues. Gates never block on `deferred`. Only broken machinery stops a phase. The live ATLAS takes the to-do list up with the Principal.

### Phase 1 — Platform (reboot in the middle)

1. Pre-flight, using only what a bare host has (one exception since v0.3.4, below): confirm Ubuntu Server 26.04.1, kernel 7.0, both NVMe drives present, fTPM enabled (V2), and the GPU present and identified from `lspci` and `/sys/class/drm`. **The `rocminfo` confirmation of `gfx1151` belongs to the Phase 4 container self-test (V11), because ROCm is never installed on the host (3.4).** *v0.3.4: the one package step 1 fetches is systemd's TPM2 library set (libtss2-rc0t64, which the server image lacks, and esys/mu/tcti-device when a minimized install lacks those too), from the Ubuntu archive before the proxy and firewall exist; exactly one TPM is required, since `tpm2-device=auto` refuses two (S42). Every package install through the scripts' installer and every package-list update first waits, up to 15 minutes, for apt-daily/unattended-upgrades to finish; the remaining apt calls wait on the dpkg lock for 5 minutes.*
2. LUKS2 on the data volume, TPM2 enrolment, recovery key printed once for off-node storage (D3).
3. Mount layout per Section 3.5; swap off; tmpfs for `/tmp`, capped at 4 GiB by a `tmp.mount` drop-in from the next boot (*S55, 2026-10-17;* it was 16 GiB).
4. System update; kernel parameters (V3); firewall baseline; SSH hardening; Cockpit. *v0.3.4: tpm2-tools is installed before the update and every initramfs is rebuilt with TPM2 support before the reboot (S32); time sync is proven through the closed firewall (S35). v0.3.5: before the update the node is held on the GA 7.0 kernel line; HWE metapackages over the same 7.0 image are swapped for `linux-generic` after a clean apt simulation, anything else (an OEM kernel, an image outside 7.0, a kernel metapackage held with `apt-mark`) becomes the to-do `kernel-line` (S45). v0.3.5, option (c) of 2026-10-10 (S48): kdump is switched off without removing the package (debconf answer `false` and `USE_KDUMP=0`, `kdump-tools.service` disabled and masked, a loaded crash kernel unloaded), and `/etc/default/grub.d/zzz-atlas-crash.cfg` removes every `crashkernel=` and `panic=` from the kernel line and appends `panic=10`; after update-grub the step stops if `grub.cfg` still carries `crashkernel=` or its first kernel line (the default entry) does not carry exactly one `panic=10`. v0.3.5, 2026-10-10 (S49): the same drop-in also removes every `kho=` and appends `kho=off` after `panic=10`, the step stops unless that first kernel line carries exactly one `kho=` word and it is `kho=off`, and its log records the kexec handover scratch the running kernel holds (on the first run that boot still carries the crash-kernel reservation, which adds about 8.5 GiB to the scratch, S49). v0.3.5, 2026-10-11 (S50): last before the reboot, the step finds the hardware watchdog without starting it: it loads `sp5100_tco` by name, since Ubuntu blacklists every watchdog module, and does not take a zero exit status as proof (only `/sys/class/watchdog` counts, where a device named exactly `SP5100 TCO timer` and bound to the `sp5100-tco` driver must appear); otherwise it unloads the module, tries `wdat_wdt` only when ACPI has a WDAT table, and with neither chooses `softdog soft_panic=1` for the next boot (not loaded now) and raises the to-do `watchdog`, which a later run that finds a hardware watchdog closes. It never opens `/dev/watchdog*`. It then writes `/etc/systemd/system/atlas-watchdog-driver.service` (a plain `modprobe` of the chosen driver before `sysinit.target` at every boot), enabled without reloading systemd, and `/etc/systemd/system.conf.d/90-atlas-watchdog.conf` (`RuntimeWatchdogSec=2min`, `RebootWatchdogSec=10min`), both rewritten whole on every run, and logs what it found (the SMBus controller's PCI ids, whether ACPI has a WDAT table, and the device's state, timeout and boot status, or the driver's kernel lines when it made no device). systemd arms the watchdog from the reboot on. A missing hardware watchdog never stops the step; a unit that cannot be enabled does.*
5. Reboot. Post-reboot: `vulkaninfo` shows the Radeon 8065S, and the GTT pool read from `/sys/class/drm` matches the kernel parameters (V3, first half). The `llama-cli --list-devices` check (llama.cpp's Vulkan backend lists the GPU as `Vulkan0` with at least 160000 MiB, about 156 GiB, of device memory, or the largest Day 1 seat's projected footprint if that is larger (v0.4.0, D-36 option (c)); the figure is recorded and is not the engine budget) moves to the Phase 2 gate, since llama.cpp is installed in Phase 2 (V3, second half). *v0.3.5: V3a also proves the crash settings of S48: no `crashkernel=` on the booted command line and no crash-kernel memory reserved, exactly one `panic=10`, `kernel.panic` 10 and `kernel.panic_on_oops` 0. Since S49 it also fails unless the booted command line carries exactly one `kho=` word, the literal `kho=off`, and on any CMA memory: kexec handover scratch still set aside (the zone `cma` pages of `/proc/zoneinfo` less `CmaTotal`, which never counts the scratch) or a named CMA area (`CmaTotal` above 0). Three read-only records follow: the kernel line (the to-do `kernel-line` closes once it is GA 7.0, and is raised again with the state after the reboot otherwise, S45), what the unused NPU shows (PCI revision, driver, `/dev/accel`; 3.7 W-NPU), and whether a kernel panic's log can be kept across the restart (the pstore backend, `systemd-pstore.service`, archived records and kdump's state; S48), which warns but never stops. v0.3.5, 2026-10-11 (S50): right after V3a, V24 proves that systemd has armed the watchdog step 4 installed, with both timeouts in effect, pings it and is the only holder of the device; a hardware watchdog passes, the softdog fallback is recorded deferred (to-do `watchdog`), and anything else, no watchdog device at all included, is a fail that stops the step as V3a does. Last, step 5 raises the to-do `w-drill`, the crash and watchdog tests the live ATLAS runs later with the Principal present (3.7 W-DRILL).*
5b. XFCE and xrdp installed, bound to LAN and WireGuard only; Google Chrome in the desktop session (v0.3.3); one RDP connection tested from the Principal's Windows PC (V19; no connection within 10 minutes is deferred with a to-do, not failed).
6. Docker with GPU device passthrough; service account in `render` and `video`.
7. WG-Easy, Cloudflare dynamic DNS with the relocated scoped token (R7), ntfy.
8. **Gate:** table of V2, V3 first half, V5, V19 and V24 results. Phase 2 does not start on a red row. V1 is recorded as informational only, since the node runs on Wi-Fi. *v0.3.5 (S50): V24 is required; its deferred row (the softdog fallback, to-do `watchdog`) never blocks, like every deferred row.*

### Phase 2 — Engines and services (fast, no large downloads)

1. llama-server, Vulkan build. *v0.4.0 (S-01, S-02, S-37; D-01 option (c), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* built from the pinned build tag b11474 (3.4), with `llama-perplexity` among the installed binaries; beside it, v0.4.1's `llama-perplexity` alone, from a git worktree into `/opt/atlas/llama-parity`, for the Phase 3 parity test V25 only. Up to 20 engine slots (ports 8101 to 8120); the Day 1 set is seven units (S-33). The optional ROCm container for tuned prefill is phased after Day 1 (3.4, D-52 option (b)).
2. Redis, Celery workers (`cpu`, `gpu` queues), orchestrator scaffold with the Engine Arbiter, router, approval queue, task ledger. *v0.4.0 (D-32 option (a)):* `ATLAS_ARBITER_MAX_RESIDENT=1` is seeded once into `orchestrator.env`, so a value the Principal sets survives a re-run (4.2 rule 3). *S55 (2026-10-17):* the Celery `cpu` worker's concurrency is min(8, nproc − 2), at least 1 (`CELERY_CPU_CONCURRENCY` in `orchestrator.env`), and the units carry the out-of-memory order (`OOMScoreAdjust` 500 on the Celery `cpu` worker, -500 on the orchestrator and on `llama-server@`).
3. Open WebUI with offline hardening (12.1); A.T.L.A.S., Ren, Arthur registered as models; the router Filter installed.
4. ChromaDB, LightRAG graph store (D7), and the three resident small models, named in D16 since v0.4.0 (reopening D4): the Qwen3.5-4B router on the iGPU, and Qwen3-Embedding-4B and Qwen3-Reranker-4B on the CPU (`--device none`; D-14 option (e), D-45 option (a)). All are small and belong in this no-large-downloads phase, and the new embedder is in place before the first document, so the empty corpus is indexed with it from the start (10.1). The step keeps the 1024-dim schema by truncating and re-normalising each vector, seeds the query instruction once into `memory.env`, checks that the embedder returns at least 1,024 dimensions and different vectors for a query and a passage and that the reranker ranks, and stops on a collection another embedder wrote. Docling ingestion service, under an 8 GiB cap and first in the out-of-memory order with the Celery `cpu` worker (S55). The OCR companion is phased (5.3, C-04).
5. Kokoro, Chatterbox (0.1.7), Whisper Large-v3-Turbo, pyannote speaker-diarization-community-1 (V6; v0.4.0, C-06: it replaces 3.1, which V6 probes only as a diagnostic when community-1 is refused). The other new speech candidates are phased (14.1). *S55:* Kokoro and speaches run under cgroup caps of 3 and 4 GiB.
6. Phase 2 tools (15.1): IfcOpenShell, Bonsai, MCP4IFC, Radiance, OpenStudio/EnergyPlus, KiCad CLI, Playwright, the cross-platform build container (Android and Windows targets only).
6b. Relocate the Cloudflare token automatically: scoped token into a mode-600 environment file owned by the updater service, outside `/srv/atlas` and outside restic's include set, then delete `CLOUDFLARE.txt` (R7, D-closed).
6c. Google OAuth pause: the script prints one authorisation link per account, waits for the Principal to approve in a browser, then continues. Two accounts, roughly five minutes total (V20).
6d. AEGIS sandbox image (Section 16.4): built and proved by killing a runaway process under the memory cap (V17). *Added in v0.3.2; the build showed Section 17 had no home for V17.*
7. restic repository on the second drive; nightly timer; first backup; first restore test.
8. Sentinel timer, enabled with the feeds closed under D6: CoinDesk, an ASX and US index feed, RSS news, node telemetry. Pruning timer.
9. Windows PC share mount unit, on-demand. *Skipped on Day 1 (v0.3.3, Section 12.4): runs when `WINDOWS_SHARE` and the credential file exist.*
9b. Vault (Section 11): gocryptfs initialised with a passphrase the Principal types once, the open/lock/idle mechanics proved, and the memory rule proved (V18). *Added in v0.3.2.*
10. **Gate:** every service healthy; V3 second half (`llama-cli --list-devices` lists `Vulkan0` with at least the larger of 160000 MiB and the largest Day 1 seat with its context memory, as the Arbiter projects it; v0.4.0, D-36 option (c)), V6, V7 (listening test, deferred if the reference recordings do not exist yet), V12, V13, V14 first half, V15, V16, V17, V18, V20, V23 recorded, plus V10's resident-router half. The Arbiter's refusal logic is unit-tested here against stub footprints; the real-engine tests are V14b and V21 in Phase 3 (v0.4.0: V21 proves single residency, S-24). Tool installs whose inputs could not be verified in advance (Bonsai, Blender, OpenStudio/EnergyPlus, MCP4IFC, the build container) record a deferred `T-<tool>` row and never block the gate; `--force 06` re-runs them.

### Phase 3 — Core LLM pull (long, detached, resumable)

1. Pull, with checksum verification, at the quantisations fixed in Section 5.1. *v0.4.0 (S-34; D-42 option (b), approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register):* the four LLM repositories of `engines.json`: gpt-oss-120b (F16 trunk, native MXFP4 experts), gpt-oss-120b abliterated (MXFP4, unchanged), GLM-5.3-Flash (`UD-IQ4_XS`, with the `mmproj-F16.gguf` from the repository root) and DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 (`UD-Q8_K_XL`). About 448 GB, where v0.3.5 pulled about 690 GB. No file is joined any more, so the free-space check is the download plus 5%. The phased engines come in a post-Day-1 pull with the same checksum and load tests (5.1).
2. For each LLM seat, in turn: load through the Engine Arbiter; confirm the KV cache type actually applied (V4: GLM-5.3-Flash prints two lines, its attention and indexer caches, and both must show the type); measure decode and prefill at 512 and 8k tokens; measure swap time; unload; confirm memory returned. *v0.4.0 (S-25, S-26, S-42; D-29 option (a), D-33 option (c)):* decode is recorded beside the seat's projection (V10) and fails only below a floor the Principal sets; none is set, so the step records and raises the to-do `decode-floors` (D-47). DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 loads f16 first at its own 1 × 131,072 profile and keeps it if it loads, proves its KV lines and answers the coherence prompt; `q8_0` is tried only if f16 fails, and `q4_0` never. The Apex and every engine newer than v0.4.1 (GLM-5.3-Flash) have their own V22 row, and a failure there is deferred without blocking the phase (R19). GLM-5.3-Flash is measured at its 8 × 32k profile with its projector loaded. *v0.3.5 (S52): each load (each rung of the DeepSeek ladder that loaded) also keeps a record, never a verdict, of what that load's own log says: its context memory (the K/V cache, the recurrent state of the hybrids and DeepSeek's compressor state) beside the Arbiter's projection for the same profile, its compute buffers and slot window, whether llama-server switched context shift off, and the prompt-cache and checkpoint lines; and of its host RAM, read-only from the unit's main process, after the load and after the tests. A line not found is UNKNOWN, nothing in the record fails anything, and the record itself changes no engine setting. The size lines, like V4's proof line, are logged only because every chat engine runs at llama-server verbosity 4 (`--verbosity 4`, rendered into each unit by `phase2/engine-env.py`; the pinned build drops them at the default 3). v0.4.0: the records read the same lines at b11474, and the projection now includes the per-slot state and the compute buffer (4.2 rule 2).*
2b. *New in v0.4.0 (S-02, V25):* output parity between the pin b11474 and the old pin v0.4.1, on the chat engines both builds load (gpt-oss-120b, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 and the router). With every LLM seat unloaded through the Arbiter and generation idle, the old build's `llama-perplexity` writes base logits for a fixed corpus (16 × 512 tokens from the domain profiles), the new build scores them with `--kl-divergence`, and both run the 24-task deterministic set `phase3/parity-tasks.txt`. Flash attention on gives the verdict; off is recorded. The thresholds, confirmed by the Principal on 2026-10-17 (S55): a mean KLD of at most 0.01, the same top token at least 98% of the time, and no fewer tasks correct. GLM-5.3-Flash (the old pin cannot load it) and the embedder and reranker (no next-token logits) are listed as not covered, and a file the old build cannot load records `deferred`. Memory is polled back after every run; the results go to `parity.json`.
2c. *New in v0.4.0 (S-03):* a quality record per LLM seat on the pin, the unchanged abliterated entry excepted: the corpus perplexity and the task score at the seat's own KV type, written to `quality.json` and raised as the to-do `kld-run` with each seat's reference status. It is never a verdict. The reference-based KLD run (T-03) needs reference files that Day 1 does not pull: DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731's `UD-Q8_K_XL` is bit-exact by construction, and GLM-5.3-Flash's `Q8_0` reference (317 GiB) exceeds memory, so its check is a task delta against published full-precision scores.
3. *v0.4.0 (S-24; D-32 option (a)):* single-residency test, replacing v0.3.5's two-residency test (gpt-oss-120b beside Qwen2.5-VL-72B at about 142 GB, which went with D-32 (a) and S-22). gpt-oss-120b is loaded through the Arbiter, then GLM-5.3-Flash, and the Arbiter must evict the first: its unit stopped, the ledger holding GLM alone, and the GTT delta GLM's alone, capped at the live budget. Two generation requests through the orchestrator must queue, the second starting only after the first finishes (V21). Slot save and restore across the swap is phased (4.4, T-13) and recorded as not tested. Then, with nothing resident, the Arbiter's refusal of an over-budget load and its Deep Think downgrade against the real engine set (V14b). A rule-5 leak in the cleanup fails V21 and stops the phase. The results go to `residency.json`.
4. **Gate:** printed table of load success, KV type, tok/s, swap seconds per engine. This is the day-one load test the whole design depends on (V10). *v0.4.0 (S-26; D-33 option (c)):* the residency, parity and quality summaries print with it, and the gate is `gate phase3 V4 V10 V14b V21 V25 -- V22`: V25 is required, and V22, with its own row for the Apex and for GLM-5.3-Flash, whose llama.cpp support is newer than the rest, is recorded and never blocks the phase (R19). *v0.3.5 (S52): before the table, the records of step 2, plus the resident router's current log and host RAM (read-only), become two to-dos for the Principal: `kv-estimates` (each engine's measured context memory against the Arbiter's estimate, and whether to correct the estimates) and `cache-ram` (each server's host RAM, what its log says of the prompt cache and the checkpoints against the cap its env file renders since S55, and suggested `--cache-ram` and `--ctx-checkpoints` values, INFERRED: whether to change a cap, which goes in `engines.json` `cache_ram_mib`, or set `--ctx-checkpoints`). Both are always raised, with UNKNOWN where a figure is missing, and neither affects the gate.*

### Phase 4 — Multimodal engines (long, detached, per-engine pass/fail)

1. Inside the base ROCm container, confirm `rocminfo` reports `gfx1151`, then self-test AMD's gfx1151 PyTorch wheels (pinned ROCm 10.0.0 / torch 2.13.0 / torchvision 0.28.0 / torchaudio 2.11.0.2): tensor on GPU, matmul, a small diffusion step, and, since S51, torchaudio is exactly the pinned build (V11; whether it imports is recorded, and a failed import fails only the two engines that use it). This is the first and only place ROCm runs.
2. Build green engines in order of value: FLUX.1-dev, Wan2.2, Florence-2, Chronos, UI-TARS-1.5-7B, Rad-DINO, SAM 2 with the extension flag, Stable Audio Open, CosyVoice2, OpenVLA (corrected in v0.4.0, B-11: TimesFM and UI-TARS 2.0 were dropped in v0.3.1, 15.2). The vision engine is not here: it is GLM-5.3-Flash with its glm5v projector, a GGUF engine pulled in Phase 3. *v0.4.0 (S-37, S-46; D-50 option (a)):* the step first unloads every resident LLM seat through the Arbiter, so each engine is tested alone (4.2); the image tier's Z-Image-Turbo and Qwen-Image-2512 join this list after Day 1, and the OCR companion is a llama-server unit, not a Phase 4 engine (15.2, 5.3).
3. Attempt yellow engines: TRELLIS, Blender Cycles HIP with CPU fallback. Log pass or fail, never block.
4. Verify PointLLM (V8) and Clay or Prithvi (V9); mark deferred if they fail.
5. Register every passing engine with the Engine Arbiter with its measured footprint.
6. **Gate:** per-engine table: built, loaded, sample output produced, footprint, pass/fail/deferred.

### Expected durations

| Phase | Time |
|---|---|
| 1 | 30 to 60 minutes including reboot |
| 2 | 30 to 60 minutes |
| 3 | Download-bound: ~10 hours at 100 Mbps over Wi-Fi for ~448 GB (v0.4.0; v0.3.5: ~15 hours for ~690 GB); plus the load, parity and quality tests, longer than v0.3.5's ~30 minutes now that each large seat takes about a minute to load and decodes at 7 to 14 tok/s (INFERRED; Phase 3 records the times) |
| 4 | Download and build-bound: several hours; can run overnight |

## 18. Alignment findings: contradictions found and resolved

| # | Contradiction or ambiguity | Resolution |
|---|---|---|
| C1 | Backend named as llama.cpp, then Ollama, then llama-server at different points | llama-server primary on Vulkan; Ollama optional. D1 confirms |
| C2 | Ubuntu 26.04 chosen, but Phase 4 needs ROCm PyTorch, which does not install cleanly on the 26.04 host. Out of date since v0.3.5 (S51): AMD's ROCm 10.0.0 support data lists this chip on 26.04 with the GA 7.0 kernel and the in-box driver, and 26.04's universe carries ROCm 7.1 (VERIFIED) | ROCm lives inside containers; host runs Vulkan only (3.4). The resolution stands for the design's own reasons: isolation, exact pinning, and independence from Ubuntu's ROCm line (3.4, S51) |
| C3 | Windows 96 GB and Linux ~120 GB budgets both appeared | Linux numbers are binding (4.1) |
| C4 | KV-cache proposal mixed shipped features with unmerged research | Only shipped features adopted (4.3) |
| C5 | Meditron listed among PyTorch engines | Runs through llama.cpp (5.1) |
| C6 | Arthur's override bound to the abliterated engine "for legal dissection" | Override is Qwen3.5 (6.1). *v0.4.0:* that override is now GLM-5.3-Flash, since Qwen3.5-122B-A10B left the roster (6.1) |
| C7 | Gideon and Minerva bound to the abliterated engine "for zero-hallucination" | Standard engine, retrieval-grounded (6.2) |
| C8 | Router described as an Open WebUI pipeline | Lives in the orchestrator; Filter is a relay (7.1) |
| C9 | Domain 27 phrased as "memetic warfare" and "sentiment manipulation" | Retained under Helena with phrasing softened to legitimate growth and attribution work |
| C10 | Domain profiles' "Stateless MRTR, dispatch and decouple" removes the approval gate | Isolated-context dispatch with tracked completion (8.6) |
| C11 | Sentinel described as a WSL2 cron job | systemd timer; there is no WSL (9.3) |
| C12 | Sentinel fed to Ren | Alaric and Silas under Arthur (9.3) |
| C13 | AEGIS described as a single zip | restic (9.5) |
| C14 | AEGIS destination on the same drive | Second drive plus optional external (9.5) |
| C15 | Celery framed as freeing "a background hardware thread" for a backtest that may call an LLM | CPU work decouples; GPU calls still serialise (9.7) |
| C16 | Estate memory inside the vault versus the vault as on-demand top-secret storage | Estate memory on the LUKS volume; vault content never persisted (10.5) |
| C17 | Four engine names that do not exist | Real replacements (15.3) |
| C18 | "Vector Cortex replaces chat logs" versus Open WebUI's own database | Supplements; retention rule under D9 (10.4) |
| C19 | Desktop recommended for a Linux novice, then Server chosen, then the Principal required a graphics interface | **Reopened and re-decided:** Server 26.04.1 plus XFCE and xrdp, not the full Desktop image; Cockpit retained (3.1) |
| C20 | Fish Audio requested, then excluded as non-local and research-licensed | Kokoro plus Chatterbox (14.1) |
| C21 | Ubuntu 24.04.5 versus 26.04.1 left open | 26.04.1. 24.04.5 brings the 7.0 kernel through HWE but stays on Mesa 25.2.8, so hardware support is close, not identical; 26.04.1 wins on support life (May 2031 against May 2029), graphics stack and toolchain (3.1; corrected in v0.3.4, S37) |
| C22 | Whether to adopt the amd64v3 or amd64v4 optimised archives | Neither. The gain is on CPU-bound distribution packages, not GPU inference (3.1) |
| C23 | Everything quantised to 4-bit under a 128 GB constraint that no longer exists | Q8_0 for Nemotron, Qwen3.5, Qwen2.5-VL-72B and Meditron; MXFP4 retained for gpt-oss as its native form; Q4_K_XL for the Apex engine (5.1). **Superseded by C28 in v0.4.0** |
| C24 | Two domains held in reserve pending a stated need (old D5) | Reserve closed. Six new domains, five subspecialty folds (8.2) |
| C25 | Vision split across GLM-4.6V-Flash, Qwen2.5-VL-7B and a 32B that v0.1 wrongly said did not exist | One engine: Qwen2.5-VL-72B at Q8_0. The 32B is real and official, but the Principal chose maximum quality (15.2). **Superseded by C29 in v0.4.0** |
| C26 | "Two engines at once" ambiguous between residency and generation | Two may be resident; exactly one generates, everywhere, including Celery jobs (4.2). **Superseded by C30 in v0.4.0** |
| C27 | Ubuntu Server 26.04.1 versus Fedora 44 KDE Plasma, raised by the Principal on 2026-10-07 | Ubuntu Server 26.04.1, re-confirmed 2026-10-08: five years without a major upgrade, a fixed kernel series, a dependable Remote Desktop login without autologin, AMD's vendor support for the chip, and no rewrite of the reviewed scripts (3.1). New in v0.3.4 |
| C28 | C23's rule (the highest quantisation that leaves working headroom) sized files for a co-resident partner and kept DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 at `UD-Q4_K_XL` on a misread size; the Principal's directives of 2026-10-15 put result quality first | **Supersedes C23 (v0.4.0, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register; D-11 (a), D-29 (a), D-59 (b)).** Each seat runs the least-quantised rung that fits it alone, with its measured quality cost stated: DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 at `UD-Q8_K_XL` (bit-exact), GLM-5.3-Flash at `UD-IQ4_XS` (the one lossy rung, provisional until T-03), gpt-oss-120b with an F16 trunk and its native MXFP4 experts; the KV cache at f16 where it is cheap, `q8_0` elsewhere and `q4_0` nowhere, the abliterated entry unchanged (Section 1, 4.3, 5.1) |
| C29 | C25 made Qwen2.5-VL-72B at `Q8_0` the one vision engine; with one LLM seat resident, a separate 79 GB vision engine would cost a swap for every image | **Supersedes C25 (v0.4.0, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register; D-20 (a), D-21 (a)).** Still one vision engine: GLM-5.3-Flash with its glm5v projector on its own unit, so a look costs no swap while GLM is resident. Qwen2.5-VL-72B is retired. The phased OCR companion transcribes and does not reason, so it is not a second vision engine (5.1, 14.4, 15.2) |
| C30 | C26 read "two engines at once" as two resident and one generating; the two large Day 1 seats hold 147 to 151 GiB of weights each, so neither fits beside any other seat, and one seat at a time is made the rule for every seat | **Supersedes C26 (v0.4.0, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register; D-32 (a)).** One LLM seat is resident at a time, and exactly one generation runs, everywhere, Celery jobs included. The limit is the setting `ATLAS_ARBITER_MAX_RESIDENT` (default 1, range 1 to 2), so two-seat residency can return without a code change; the companions, Chatterbox and the Phase 4 engines are not seats (4.2 rules 3 and 7) |

## 19. Decisions — closed (D15 closed 2026-10-05; D16 to D27 closed 2026-10-16)

*v0.4.0 (D-25 option (a)):* D16 to D27 record the action register the Principal returned on 2026-10-16: every row approved, P-10 and P-17 marked "Approve with changes": P-10 with the Principal's change (Eleanor's own persona work on GLM-5.3-Flash, her 4B router for classification only, 5.3); P-17 is among the uncensored-seat items, unchanged by this revision. D16, D17 and D18 reopen D4, D10 and D12. The register's own numbers (D-01 to D-64) are kept beside each answer; D16 onward are this document's rows.

| # | Decision | The Principal's answer | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Inference backend: llama-server or Ollama | llama-server, Ollama not installed | Accepted as recommended |
| D2 | Secure Boot: disable, or enrol keys | Disabled on a headless node behind a firewall | Accepted as recommended; **superseded by D15 (enabled)** |
| D3 | Where the LUKS recovery key and restic passphrase live | On the node and on an external USB drive | Changed by the Principal. See R16: the USB copy is the one that survives node loss, so it must be stored away from the node |
| D4 | Router model and embedding model | Qwen3.5 4B-class instruct, bge-m3, bge-reranker-v2-m3 | Accepted as recommended; **reopened in v0.4.0 by D16** |

| D9 | Retention rule for chats, logs, backups | Defaults in 10.4 and 9.5 are binding | Accepted as recommended |

| D15 | Secure Boot and the TPM binding (reopens D2) | **CLOSED 2026-10-05: option (a), Secure Boot enabled.** With Secure Boot off, PCR 7 is the same for any boot medium, so the data volume would unseal for any OS booted on this hardware. Enabling Secure Boot reverses D2 at no cost: this stack has no out-of-tree kernel modules (amdgpu and WireGuard are in-tree, Docker needs none). The Principal enabled it in the BIOS (confirmed 2026-10-13); it is a precondition in Section 22. Should pre-flight find it off, it warns, every V2 row notes the state and the to-do `secure-boot` stays open (no acknowledgement key exists any more; options (b) and (c) are withdrawn) | **Decided by the Principal: (a).** R23 mitigated: the BIOS setting confirmed made on 2026-10-13; pre-flight verifies it on Day 1 |

Qwen3-Embedding-4B and Qwen3-Reranker-4B `Q8_0` replace bge-m3 and bge-reranker-v2-m3, on the CPU (D-14 option (e)), adopted before Day 1 so the empty corpus is indexed with the new embedder (D-45 option (a)), the 1024-dim schema kept by truncation (C-02, C-03), the embedder served at a 4,096-token context with `-ub 2048`, reduced from C-02's `-ub 8192` for the D-14 (e) budget (5.3; a T-15 to-do).
