## 20. Risk register

| # | Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | PyTorch on gfx1151 fails in Phase 4. As first written: no official gfx1151 wheel, and the community wheel may break on update. Amended (S15, S51): AMD now publishes the wheels (ROCm 10.0.0, torch 2.13.0); what remains is the open hang report #6530 (3.4); S51 pinned torchaudio, the one package that could have pulled a build for another ROCm version into the image | High | High for Phase 4, none for the LLM layer | Pin versions (every package the Dockerfile names from AMD's index, torchaudio included since S51), per-engine pass/fail, containerised so the host is untouched |
| R2 | Ollama's Vulkan path immature on 26.04 | Medium | High if chosen | D1: llama-server primary |
| R3 | ROCm 7.2.x does not install on the 26.04 host kernel. Amended in v0.3.5 (S51): out of date, since AMD's ROCm 10.0.0 support data lists this chip on 26.04 with the GA 7.0 kernel and the in-box driver, and 26.04's universe carries ROCm 7.1 (VERIFIED) | Low since S51 (was Certain) | Medium | Never install ROCm on the host, containers only (3.4), now for isolation, exact pinning and independence from Ubuntu's ROCm line |
| R4 | KV quantisation silently falls back to full precision on an unsupported architecture. *Restated in v0.4.0 (S-48):* GLM-5.3-Flash and DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 run an f16 cache (D-29 option (a)), so on Day 1 only gpt-oss-120b, the router and the CPU pair, and the unchanged abliterated entry (`q4_0`, as in v0.3.5), run a quantised one. GLM may need flash attention off on Vulkan (T-04), which would rule out a quantised V cache for it. Each architecture prints its own number of KV proof lines (two on GLM, its attention and indexer caches), and a phased engine's count is set at its pull. The restatement was acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17. | Medium | High, unexpected OOM | V4 per-model check, on every proof line an engine prints (`kv_proof_lines`) |
| R5 | A second engine loads before the first releases memory, node crashes | Medium without an arbiter | High | Engine Arbiter polls GPU memory before every load (4.2) |
| R6 | GraphRAG indexing is LLM-heavy and slow here | High | Medium | Idle-time Celery job on the resident small model, D7 LightRAG |
| R7 | Cloudflare token in a plain-text file | Certain today | High | Scoped token, mode 600, outside backups and repo (12.3) |
| R8 | Open WebUI phones home by default | Certain unless hardened | High for the zero-cloud rule | Offline hardening in Phase 2 (12.1) |
| R9 | 10GbE driver unavailable | Not applicable | None | The node runs on Wi-Fi by the Principal's choice; V1 is informational |
| R19 | DeepSeek V4 Flash llama.cpp support is newer than the other engines. *Generalised in v0.4.0 (S-26, S-47):* every engine whose support is newer than the rest; on Day 1 the Apex, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, and GLM-5.3-Flash (`glm5-next`, first in b11279); at its pull Qwen3.8-Flash-Next (`qwen4exp`). The restatement was acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17. | Medium | Medium for the Apex; high for GLM-5.3-Flash, which carries seven seats, vision and long documents | Its own V22 row in the Phase 3 gate; a failure defers the engine without blocking the phase or the other engines, and its seats move to their recorded fallbacks by the Principal's decision (6.2, D-40) |
| R20 | Wi-Fi throughput and stability over a ~690 GB download. *Restated in v0.4.0 (S-34):* about 448 GB in Phase 3 (D-42 option (b)), about 842 GB of phased engines in a later pull, about 1.9 TB in all (D-41 option (a)). The restatement was acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17. | Medium | Low, time only | Phases 3 and 4 detached and resumable; retries are free; only the core seats are pulled before Day 1's tests |
| R21 | XFCE and xrdp widen the surface beyond a headless server | Low | Medium | Bound to LAN and WireGuard only, never internet-facing; no desktop autologin |
| R22 | The `atlas` service user is in the `docker` group, which is root-equivalent on the host; the orchestrator needs the socket for the sandbox and Phase 4 containers | Medium | High if the orchestrator is ever compromised | Accepted for Day 1 and recorded in every script header; Day 2 options are rootless Docker or a socket proxy that allows only the sandbox and engine images. New in v0.3.2 |
| R23 | TPM2 unlock bound to PCR 7 with Secure Boot disabled unseals for any boot medium (see D15) | Medium | High for physical theft of the node | D15 closed as Secure Boot enabled (v0.3.3); the Principal confirmed the BIOS setting made on 2026-10-13 (workbook sheet 5), so the risk is mitigated subject to pre-flight's own check on Day 1: were it found off, pre-flight warns, V2 notes it and the to-do `secure-boot` stays open; the enrolment itself proceeds. New in v0.3.2 |
| R24 | xrdp is the LAN service with known unpatched flaws reachable before login: 26.04 packages 0.10.1 in `universe`, eighteen 2026 xrdp CVEs (fixed upstream in 0.10.6 and 0.10.6.1) are still "needs-triage" for 26.04, Ubuntu Pro's esm-apps carries no fix, and 0.10.1 runs as root. (SSH, Cockpit, Open WebUI and ntfy are also reachable before login from the LAN; none has a comparable open list.) | Medium | High (code execution as root) if a LAN device or a WireGuard peer is hostile | v0.3.4: port 3389 only from the LAN subnet and WireGuard; the VNC proxy entry (vnc-any, the CVSS 9.8 CVE-2026-41252 path, also commented out upstream in 0.10.6.1), the NeutrinoRDP proxy entry (its module is not shipped) and Xvnc switched off; no root login, no alternate shell, TLS only; optional `RDP_ALLOW_FROM` (addresses inside the LAN, never the WireGuard bridge, checked before the firewall is touched) narrows the LAN side to the Principal's PC, and step 5b records the to-do with the LAN address the PC connected from. Residual: the remaining pre-login bugs from those hosts. Watch for a 26.04 xrdp security update; a later option is to keep 3389 on WireGuard only. New in v0.3.4 |
| R25 | Unattended upgrades install security updates from the release and security pockets but never reboot, and do not update Docker CE or Google Chrome (third-party repositories), so kernel and library fixes take effect only at the next reboot | Medium | Medium | Accepted for Day 1: an automatic reboot would interrupt long jobs. Day 2: the live ATLAS reports "reboot required" and pending Docker CE/Chrome updates through ntfy and schedules the reboot with the Principal. New in v0.3.4 |
| R26 | The ledger's 30-day purge of operational rows (D9, 10.4) is written (`Ledger.purge_expired`, which refuses to run without an archive callback) but nothing calls it: `arbiter_decisions`, `routing_decisions`, `tasks` and decided `approvals` (30 days) and `sentinel_pulses` (365 days) grow without bound (VERIFIED at this commit; `strikes` are permanent by design) | Certain that the tables grow; Low that it harms (one owner, small rows, a WAL SQLite file on the 8 TB drive, in every nightly backup) | Low | Phased item (S53, with the measurement layer in the first months): the sweep calls `purge_expired` with the cold archive as its callback. Until then the growth is slow and bounded only by disk, which Sentinel's hourly disk telemetry watches; the measurement layer also records the table sizes. New in v0.3.5 (S53) |
| R27 | The pinned Chroma 1.5.9 soft-deletes: a deleted vector is only marked in the HNSW index, labels are never reused, the index's capacity grows to the next power of two of everything ever added and never shrinks, and Chroma has no TTL (feature request #2139, open since 2024) and no compaction, so each collection's index tracks inserts ever, not live notes (VERIFIED in the source at the tag and in its hnswlib fork); a query falls back to brute force once more than a fifth of a small index is deleted | Medium over years (chat turns are inserted and expire continually; Sentinel BLUFs yearly) | Low (disk and RAM under the container's 8 GiB cap; one owner's volumes, roughly 4 to 6 KiB per vector, INFERRED) | The sweep's measurement layer (phased, S53) records live counts per collection and cumulative inserts, the proxy for the tombstone count Chroma does not expose; a collection rebuild (re-insert the live documents into a fresh collection, re-embedding them, outside a backup freeze) when the inserted-ever to live ratio warrants it, as a Principal-approved maintenance proposal: W-CHROMA-REBUILD, at these volumes a yearly question at most. New in v0.3.5 (S53) |
| R28 | A sweep that failed inside the Celery worker was silent: `atlas-prune.service` touches its cadence stamp after a successful enqueue (no `--wait`), so systemd records success, nothing retries before the next 71-hour slot, and only the ledger's task row and the journal held the error; a broken store, a locked index or a full `/srv/cold` could have gone unnoticed for weeks (VERIFIED at e6cadea) | Medium (any sweep failure; a long AEGIS freeze only spools, which is not a failure) | Low (expired notes linger; nothing is lost, since the archive is written and verified before any delete) | Since S53 the task pushes one ntfy line ("ATLAS prune: sweep failed", priority high) whether it raised or finished with errors, after the ledger row and before re-raising; the push can neither mask the error nor block the sweep. The sweep is still not retried before the next slot: the alert is what makes it visible, and the weekly summary with its two-in-a-row rule is a phased item. New in v0.3.5 (S53) |
| R29 | The re-pin changes every engine's behaviour: b11474 is 510 commits after v0.4.1, and the Vulkan fixes between them include wrong KV rows without flash attention (llama.cpp #28956) and flash-attention fixes (#29591, #29988), so results can depend on the `-fa` setting (UNVERIFIED on this node). New in v0.4.0 (S-47); acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17 | Medium | High (every engine) | V25, required at the Phase 3 gate: the old and new builds compared on gpt-oss-120b, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 and the router, flash attention on for the verdict and off recorded (3.4). GLM-5.3-Flash cannot be compared, since the old pin cannot load it, so V4, V22, the coherence prompt, T-03 and T-04 carry its checks. The pin changes only by a deliberate edit |
| R30 | GLM-5.3-Flash's only fitting rung is lossy (`UD-IQ4_XS`: published mean KLD 0.117, the same top token 88.2% of the time; UNVERIFIED on this node), and it carries seven seats, vision and long documents. New in v0.4.0 (S-47); acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17 | Certain that the rung is lossy; Medium that it matters on the Principal's work | Medium to high: hard reasoning magnifies quantisation loss (INFERRED from the 2026 review) | Provisional on every seat until the KLD run (D-02 option (c), T-03); the Phase 3 quality record and the to-do `kld-run`; the reasoning and Apex seats on the bit-exact DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731; strong cross-checks on the other family (9.2); the Principal's test sets (D-49) |
| R31 | Swap and re-prefill latency: with one LLM seat resident, every change of seat is a swap of about 50 to 85 seconds through LUKS, plus re-prefill of the task's context (DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 about 1.5 to 4.5 minutes at 16k to 32k and up to 13 at 64k; INFERRED until T-14). New in v0.4.0 (S-47); acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17 | Certain on every swap | Medium (usability; nothing is lost) | GLM-5.3-Flash carries most seats and stays resident (6.3); Deep Think phases are batched (9.1); on Day 1 each strong cross-check swaps on its own (9.2); deep runs are reserved for the Principal's acceptance (D-43, built, 9.1); Phase 3 records each swap; the phased swap batching and slot restore (4.4, 6.3; T-13, T-14) |
| R32 | Companions shrink the engine budget: every resident GiB comes off every seat. GLM-5.3-Flash at 8 × 32k projects to 155.05 GiB against about 156 GiB expected and is refused below a MemTotal of about 186.1 GiB, and the CPU pair's host memory and the servers' host-RAM prompt caches sit at the edge of the Arbiter's budget or outside it (INFERRED). New in v0.4.0 (S-47); acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17 | Medium | High for the seat refused (never an out-of-memory crash: the Arbiter refuses first) | The Qwen3 pair on the CPU, charged its weights in full plus its measured anonymous memory, never below its projection (D-14 option (e)); Phase 3's measured footprints replace the projections (to-do `kv-estimates`); the relief orders of 4.1; the to-do `cache-ram`; the prompt-cache caps and the other host-RAM bounds of S55 (4.1, 4.4); the OCR companion's test re-checks the budget before it is added (5.3) |
| R33 | The phased DeepSeek V4.1 trial engine: ROCm on gfx1151 for an LLM (stability with the inbox driver only; llama.cpp #25992, ROCm #6186), a fast-moving source (1,984 commits in six months for the Lucebox engine), published speeds measured with greedy sampling only, and a router-bias switch that trades fidelity for speed. New in v0.4.0 (S-47); acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17 | Medium, after Day 1 only | Low (evaluation only, outside the routed roster) | Phased (15.5); the inbox amdgpu driver only, no DKMS, locked memory for that container alone, the image digest, source commit and file hashes pinned and never refreshed by themselves (D-52, D-57, B-36, B-37); speeds measured greedy and at temperature 1.0 (D-47); approximate switches only in scored trials (16.1 rule 9); promotion only by a blind-scored win (T-30) |
| R10 | Sustained thermal load in a small chassis | Medium | Low | Monitor temperatures in Cockpit; the chip's configurable ceiling is 120 W and the Principal is adding external cooling |
| R11 | CGNAT prevents inbound WireGuard | Low, port forward already succeeded | High for remote access | V5 from mobile data, static IP from the ISP if needed |
| R12 | Fictitious directors corresponding as humans, AI non-disclosure | Medium | Medium to high, legal | Gideon's sensitive-tier check, approval gate, 16.5 |
| R13 | Abliterated engine used for external output | Medium | Medium | Abliterated output always passes the same gate, never routine tier |
| R14 | Chatterbox continuation quirk in production speech | Medium | Low | V7 testing, Kokoro fallback per line |
| R15 | Prompt bloat from domain injection makes every dispatch slow | High without limits | High, usability | Three-card limit, compact cards, stable-prefix ordering (8.4, 4.4) |
| R16 | Backup passphrase or LUKS key lost with the node | Low | Catastrophic | **Sharpened under D3.** The Principal keeps a copy on the node and on an external USB drive. The on-node copy is convenience only; the USB copy is the one that matters, and it must live away from the node, since a fire or burglary that takes the node takes anything beside it. Quarterly restore test |
| R17 | PyAnnote gated model not accepted, diarisation silently absent. *Restated in v0.4.0 (S-48, C-06):* the gated pipeline is now speaker-diarization-community-1 (CC-BY-4.0, an automatically approved form). Once the phased Nemotron 3 Diarization (OpenMDW-1.1, not gated) is primary, community-1 is only its fallback for recordings with more than eight speakers, and the risk narrows to that case. The restatement was acknowledged by the Principal on 2026-10-17. | Medium | Low | V6, recorded deferred with the to-do `input-hf-token` while the token or the licence is missing |
| R18 | Continuous vision enabled by drift rather than decision | Low | High, privacy | Separate capability flag under Alaric, logged, D8 item 7 |

## 21. Verification matrix: what Day 1 must prove

| # | Proof | Where |
|---|---|---|
| V1 | Network link up and stable. Informational only: the node runs on Wi-Fi, so this is no longer a gate | Phase 1 pre-flight |
| V2 | fTPM present and enabled, TPM2 enrolment succeeds | Phase 1 step 2 |
| V3 | Kernel parameters accepted on 7.0 and the GTT pool matches them (Phase 1; since S48 the same check also proves the crash settings: no `crashkernel=` on the booted command line and no crash-kernel memory reserved, exactly one `panic=10`, `kernel.panic` 10 and `kernel.panic_on_oops` 0; since S49 also exactly one `kho=`, the literal `kho=off`, and no CMA memory: no kexec handover scratch and no named CMA area); `llama-cli --list-devices` then lists the GPU as `Vulkan0` with at least the larger of 160000 MiB (156.25 GiB) and the largest Day 1 seat with its context memory as the Arbiter projects it (v0.4.0, D-36 option (c), S-28: 155.05 GiB for GLM-5.3-Flash on Day 1, so 160000 MiB stands; the weights alone before the venv exists) of Vulkan device memory, and records the figure, which is not the engine budget (Phase 2); the measured heap decides every rung above about 149 GiB (T-01). `rocminfo`'s `gfx1151` confirmation sits in the Phase 4 container under V11 | Phase 1 gate, then Phase 2 gate |
| V4 | Quantised KV cache actually applied per model, no silent fallback (v0.4.0, S-48: the requested type, f16 included, on every proof line an engine prints; `kv_proof_lines` is four on DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 and two on GLM-5.3-Flash and gpt-oss-120b) | Phase 3 step 2 |
| V5 | WireGuard reachable from mobile data via vpn.sovereign-node.link | Phase 1 step 7 |
| V6 | The pyannote gated pipeline accepted and loading: speaker-diarization-community-1 since v0.4.0 (C-06; it was 3.1, which V6 now probes only as a diagnostic when community-1 is refused) | Phase 2 step 5 |
| V7 | Voice casting listening test, Alaric and Gideon clones sourced | Phase 2 gate |
| V8 | PointLLM builds and runs on ROCm in the container | Phase 4 step 4 |
| V9 | Clay or Prithvi builds and runs | Phase 4 step 4 |
| V10 | Every Day 1 LLM seat (gpt-oss-120b, its abliterated twin, GLM-5.3-Flash and DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731) loads, generates, swaps and releases memory at its fixed quantisation. *v0.4.0 (S-24, S-25; D-33 option (c)):* decode is recorded beside each seat's projection and fails only below a per-seat floor the Principal sets; none is set, so Phase 3 records only and raises the to-do `decode-floors` (D-47); the abliterated entry keeps its v0.3.5 band and rule; the GLM and Apex lines point to their V22 rows. Eleanor's resident 4B router model is verified separately at the Phase 2 gate (V10a), since it is pulled there | Phase 3 gate |
| V11 | Inside the ROCm container: `rocminfo` reports `gfx1151`, and AMD's gfx1151 PyTorch wheels (pinned ROCm 10.0.0 / torch 2.13.0 / torchvision 0.28.0 / torchaudio 2.11.0.2) pass tensor, matmul and diffusion self-tests; since S51 torchaudio's installed version must also be exactly the pin, `2.11.0.2+rocm10.0.0` (whether it imports is recorded, not required: an import failure fails CosyVoice 2 and Stable Audio Open, not Phase 4) | Phase 4 step 1 |
| V12 | Open WebUI makes no outbound connection after hardening (verified with the firewall log) | Phase 2 step 3 |
| V13 | restic backup completes and a restore verifies by checksum | Phase 2 step 7 |
| V14 | Engine Arbiter refuses an over-budget load and downgrades a Deep Think depth when the projected footprint exceeds budget. Unit-tested against stub footprints at the Phase 2 gate, then proved against real engines at Phase 3. *v0.4.0 (S-24):* the Phase 3 half runs with nothing resident, against the Day 1 engines and the tier lists of 4.2 rule 8 (deep: GLM-5.3-Flash, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 and gpt-oss-120b), with GLM-5.3-Flash as the probe | Phase 2 gate (stubs), Phase 3 gate (real) |
| V15 | Approval gate holds a standard-tier email until approved, routine-tier auto-sends and logs | Phase 2 gate |
| V16 | Router hard rule routes a "medical" message to Arthur even when the classifier disagrees, decision logged | Phase 2 gate |
| V17 | Sandbox memory cap kills a runaway process without affecting the node | Phase 2 gate |
| V18 | Vault opens by button, locks on idle, vault-tagged content absent from memory collections afterwards | Phase 2 gate |
| V19 | XFCE desktop reachable over xrdp from the Principal's Windows PC, and refused from outside LAN and WireGuard | Phase 1 step 5b |
| V20 | Google OAuth completed for both accounts at the Phase 2 pause; Gmail, Calendar and Drive reachable | Phase 2 step 6c |
| V21 | Single residency (v0.4.0, S-24; D-32 option (a); replacing v0.3.5's two engines resident together at ~142 GB): with gpt-oss-120b resident, a load of GLM-5.3-Flash makes the Arbiter evict it (the Arbiter at the D-32 limit of 1, its grant naming gpt-oss-120b as evicted, its unit stopped, the ledger holding GLM alone, the GTT delta GLM's alone, capped at the live budget), and a second generation request through the orchestrator queues until the first finishes. No Day 1 pair of non-abliterated seats fits the budget together, so the budget alone would force this eviction; the seat limit itself is proven by V14a's unit test. Slot restore across the swap is phased and recorded as not tested (T-13) | Phase 3 step 3 |
| V22 | The engines newer than the rest load and generate (v0.4.0, S-26; R19 generalised): DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 at `UD-Q8_K_XL` (its f16 rung, or `q8_0` if f16 fails) and every engine whose first-support llama.cpp build is after v0.4.1's b10964, on Day 1 GLM-5.3-Flash (`glm5-next`, b11279), each on its own row with a summary; a failure is deferred without blocking the phase, and its V10 line points here | Phase 3 gate (recorded, not blocking) |
| V23 | Cloudflare token relocated to a mode-600 environment file and `CLOUDFLARE.txt` deleted | Phase 2 step 6b |
| V24 | The hardware watchdog is armed (S50): systemd holds the platform's watchdog device (`SP5100 TCO timer` expected) and pings it, with `RuntimeWatchdogSec=2min` and `RebootWatchdogSec=10min` in effect, the device active with a 120-second timeout, its driver unit enabled and active, and no other process holding a watchdog device. When step 4 found no hardware watchdog, the softdog fallback armed the same way is recorded deferred (to-do `watchdog`); no watchdog device at all is a fail. It proves the timer is armed, not that this board's reset completes | Phase 1 step 5; Phase 1 gate |
| V25 | Output parity between the pin b11474 and the old pin v0.4.1 (new in v0.4.0, S-02; the condition D-01 set on the pin): on the chat engines both builds load (gpt-oss-120b, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731, the router), base logits for 16 × 512 tokens of the domain profiles written by the old build and scored by the new with `--kl-divergence`, and a 24-task deterministic set run on both. The verdict is taken with flash attention on (off is recorded): a mean KLD of at most 0.01, the same top token at least 98% of the time and no fewer tasks correct (thresholds confirmed by the Principal on 2026-10-17, S55). A file the old build cannot load records deferred; GLM-5.3-Flash and the embedder and reranker are listed as not covered | Phase 3 step 2b; Phase 3 gate (required) |

**Scope notes added in v0.3.2.** V15 proves the gate logic (hold, auto-send-and-log, cross-check required for sensitive) against a stub send channel; the real channels are Day 2 (Section 13). V14's second half and V21 prove the Arbiter against real engines, but the Deep Think rubric weights and the Ouroboros similarity threshold are untuned until the first real sessions, and Section 9.1's timings are estimates until then. The scripts also record rows that are not V items: `T-<tool>` (deferred Phase 2 tool installs), `P4-wheels` (the Phase 4 wheel-index pre-flight) and the Secure Boot state in every V2 row; they appear in the gate tables and in the workbook's evidence column, never as pass/fail rows of their own.

**Scope note added in v0.3.3.** Six V items can be recorded `deferred` when the Principal's input is missing and are never a red row in that case: V5 (no handshake from the phone yet), V19 (no RDP session yet), V6 (no Hugging Face token, or licence not yet accepted), V20 (no Google accounts or no OAuth client file), V23 (no Cloudflare token or zone id), and V7 as before (no reference recordings). Each leaves a to-do with the `--force` command that records the real result later. *v0.3.4:* V2 is also recorded `deferred` (not pass) when the only gap is an unencrypted OS volume, with the to-do `os-volume-encryption`; the data volume's TPM2 proof still has to pass. *v0.3.5 (S50):* V24 is recorded `deferred` (not pass) when Phase 1 step 4 found no hardware watchdog and the softdog fallback is armed in its place, with the to-do `watchdog`. That is a limit of this board (its firmware may have switched the timer off), not a missing input, and it never blocks the gate; every other gap in V24 is `fail`.

**Scope note added in v0.4.0.** V25 is new and required at the Phase 3 gate. Its thresholds were proposed as INFERRED and confirmed by the Principal on 2026-10-17 (S55); a fail there is a finding about the pin for the Principal, recorded with its figures, before anything is trusted on the new build. V21 and V22 changed meaning (single residency; every newer engine), and V10 records decode instead of gating it. Two Phase 3 records are not V items and never gate: the quality record of step 2c (to-do `kld-run`) and the decode figures beside the projections (to-do `decode-floors`). `tools/fill-workbook.py` fills V1 to V25.

## 23. Day 1 script build — corrections folded into the baseline (v0.3.1)

The Day 1 scripts under `scripts/day1/` were written after a fact-checking pass over every package, image, model repository and flag the baseline names. Where the checked fact disagreed with the document, the scripts follow the fact and the document is amended above. None of S1–S23 reopens a decision (D1–D14) or a resolution (C1–C26); each is a correction of a literal the baseline typed before it was checked. S24–S29, added when the scripts were complete, record what the build itself had to add or could not deliver on Day 1; S25 reopened D2 as D15, which the Principal closed on 2026-10-05 (Secure Boot enabled). S30–S31 record the Principal's two Day 1 scope instructions of 2026-10-05: no phase stops on a missing input, and the Windows share waits for the live ATLAS. S32–S38 come from the Ubuntu-versus-Fedora comparison of 2026-10-08: five script defects fixed, two factual corrections, and the re-confirmed OS choice. S39–S43 come from the adversarial reviews and the stock-image sweep of those fixes the same day. S44–S47 come from the Principal's question of 2026-10-09 about the Linux 7.4 NPU report and the research behind the fixes: a memory-budget defect in the Arbiter, the kernel line held on GA, the NPU's corrected reason, and a V3a check that would have failed on the current kernel; none reopens a decision. S48 records the Principal's decision of 2026-10-10, option (c): the crash-kernel memory reclaimed, kdump switched off without a purge, a kernel panic made to restart the node, and the Arbiter's reserve back to 16 GiB. S49 records the Principal's second decision of that day, from the review of option (c): kexec handover switched off with `kho=off`, so the memory Ubuntu's 7.0 kernel would set aside for it at boot can hold GPU memory. S50 records the Principal's decision of 2026-10-11: the hardware watchdog, armed by systemd, so a node whose kernel or systemd stops is reset by the hardware; where the board has no usable hardware watchdog, the build lets the software watchdog `softdog` stand in by default (V24 deferred, to-do `watchdog`) until the Principal decides whether to keep it. S51 records the Principal's question of 2026-10-11, whether Ubuntu would adopt AMD's ROCm 10.1 (no, by design), and the fixes the Principal approved from its review: torchaudio pinned, the one way a 10.1 build could still have reached the Phase 4 image, and the hang-report references and the stated reason for keeping ROCm off the host corrected; 10.1 stays a deliberate re-test after Day 1, and no decision changes. S52 records the Principal's question of 2026-10-11 about an XDA article on context windows that waste memory (ATLAS already sets every context explicitly), and what the Principal approved from its review: the vision engine's two-slot profile made standing, so the everyday pair forms in production; every request kept inside its engine's per-slot window, because the pinned llama.cpp rejects a prompt longer than its slot on every engine and context shift is off on six of the eight (so a reply would stop at the slot end); and each engine's context memory and host-RAM caches measured in Phase 3 and raised as two to-dos; no decision changes. S53 records the Principal's question of 2026-10-12, whether the 72-hour pruning should be improved or made usage-adaptive (the design stands; nothing has run, so there is nothing to measure yet), and what the Principal approved from its review: three small gaps closed before Day 1, the window key made a visible setting that a re-run never resets, a failed sweep pushed to the phone, and the graph archive given the documents' text so a pruned graph note is restorable; the measurement layer and the approval-gated proposal path recorded as phased items with the event that brings each in; fully automatic adjustment excluded; no decision changes. S54 records the script corrections that follow the Principal's approval of the action register on 2026-10-16: unlike S1 to S53 it accompanies a new baseline version, v0.4.0 (D-25 option (a)), whose decision rows D16 to D27 reopen D4, D10 and D12 and whose resolutions C28 to C30 supersede C23, C25 and C26. S55 records the Principal's approvals of 2026-10-17 within v0.4.0, with no new version number: the six RAM fixes the v0.4.0 review had left open, the V25 pass marks, and the acknowledgement of the new and restated risks; no decision changes. Listed here so the Principal can see what moved and why.

| # | Where | What the baseline said | What is true (September 2026) | Effect on Day 1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | 5.1 | Abliterated gpt-oss is "MXFP4, native" | Huihui abliterates the BF16 upcast; the MXFP4 GGUF is a re-quantisation (noctrex). Same 63 GB, same speed | None on sizing; the "no accuracy cost" reasoning applies only to the standard gpt-oss |
| S2 | 4.3, App. B | Apex engine KV cache `q4_0` | Quantised KV on `deepseek4` produced garbage in July–August 2026; the fix is unmerged; K and V types must be equal | Phase 3 ladders f16 → q8_0 → q4_0 with a coherence check and records the winner; an f16-only result caps the context and still passes V22. *v0.4.0 (S-42):* the ladder is f16, then `q8_0` only if f16 fails; `q4_0` is never a rung and there is no f16 context cap |
| S3 | 5.1 | "DeepSeek V4 Flash" | Two checkpoints exist; the 0731 release (MIT) supersedes the April preview | Pinned to `unsloth/DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731-GGUF`; launched with `--parallel 1` (the auto slot count OOMs on this architecture) |
| S4 | 4.3, App. B | Context shift with `n_keep` on | Off by default in current llama-server; `--parallel` defaults to auto; `--ctx-size` is the total pool across slots | Every launch line carries `--context-shift --keep <n> --parallel <n>`; 32k per slot × 8 is `--ctx-size 262144`. v0.3.5 (S52): in the pinned build a prompt longer than its slot is rejected on every engine and context shift works on Nemotron and Meditron only, so the orchestrator keeps every request inside one slot; the vision engine runs 2 slots (`--ctx-size 65536`). *v0.4.0 (S-22):* context shift is off on every Day 1 chat engine, and the vision engine is GLM-5.3-Flash at 8 × 32k |

| S40 | 17 step 2, D15 | The secure-boot to-do says `--force 02` re-enrols the TPM binding | Step 2 skipped enrolment whenever a TPM2 token already existed, so after Secure Boot was switched on the old tokens stayed and the unlock failed | Every run of step 2 now re-seals both tokens to the current PCR 7 (a no-op when it is unchanged; otherwise new token first, old tpm2 slots wiped with the new one excluded, VERIFIED in systemd-cryptenroll), so `--force 02` and a plain re-run of an unfinished step both work. The TPM itself is checked first; its own authorisation is tried before anything is asked; only when it refuses is the OS passphrase asked once, and the data volume's recovery key when no usable copy is on the node, each checked on the spot. Any previous recovery key is revoked only after the new one is confirmed WRITTEN DOWN, and on an encrypted OS a typed key restores a missing on-node copy instead of being replaced. The `secure-boot` to-do closes once Secure Boot is on |

| S44 | 4.1, 4.2 | The Arbiter's budget is the GTT pool minus the resident set, the resident set read from the GPU memory counter before any engine loads | That counter (`mem_info_gtt_used`) sees GPU buffer objects only. The CPU-side rows of 4.1 (about 12 GB: Ubuntu, XFCE, Docker, Open WebUI, ChromaDB, the voice models, the orchestrator stack) never appear on it, so the budget came out as the whole pool less about 6 GB, roughly 12 to 16 GiB more than 4.1 intends, on a node without swap (present since the first build; read from the code by the 2026-10-09 NPU review, not measured) | The Arbiter keeps a fixed 16 GiB host reserve: the CPU-side rows of 4.1 (about 12 GB) plus room for transient CPU work and kernel overhead (`ATLAS_ARBITER_HEADROOM_GIB` in `orchestrator.env`, whole GiB from 4 to 64; a bad value stops only the orchestrator's start, never the backup freeze, Sentinel or a task), and budgets from the smaller of the GTT total and MemTotal (S47). Each measurement also reads `/proc/meminfo` (MemTotal − MemAvailable − the GPU's own pages: `GPUActive` + `GPUReclaim` on kernels from 7.0.0-38, else the GTT counter) and logs a warning when CPU-side use exceeds the reserve, without ever budgeting from it, so a passing CPU job cannot change a grant; `/arbiter/status` shows both (`headroom_bytes`, `host_used_bytes`), and `atlas-admin arbiter status` prints the CPU-side use beside the reserve, computed by the same rule (`arbiter.host_used_bytes`, with the GTT counter it reads itself when the kernel lacks `GPUActive`) and marked when it is above the reserve, followed by MemAvailable and MemTotal (review of option (c)). The reserve's history: 16 GiB in the first v0.3.5 build; cut to 12 GiB by the review while kdump-tools' crash-kernel reservation (`crashkernel=` on the GRUB line, which this row first put at 4 GiB; it is 4.25 GiB, S48) held MemTotal near 183 to 184 GiB, where 16 GiB would refuse the Apex engine's f16 and q8_0 KV-ladder rungs (S2: 160.0 and 160.3 GiB projected, the f16 rung being the fallback when quantised KV is incoherent) at the larger GPU resident set; 16 GiB again since the Principal's option (c) of 2026-10-10 (S48), because step 4 removes that reservation: MemTotal is expected near 187 to 188 GiB, every rung fits while it is at least about 182.3 to 184.3 GiB, and V3a fails if the reservation survives. All of it assumes that every page of MemTotal can hold GPU memory, which holds only with kexec handover off (`kho=off`, S49). V21 and V22 prove the fit on the node |
| S45 | 3.3, 17 steps 4 and 5 | "The 26.04.1 server ISO installs the GA kernel ...; the later HWE kernels are opt-in and not used" | The 26.04.1 server ISO does install `linux-generic` (its `install-sources.yaml`: `kernel: default: linux-generic`; VERIFIED 2026-10-09), and today `linux-generic-hwe-26.04` pulls the very same 7.0.0-38 image. But nothing on the node enforced the GA line: the 26.04 installer now runs its OEM lookup on Server too (`oem: install: auto`), so a platform metapackage matching the machine at install time would bring its own kernel (none matches today, INFERRED); a 26.04.2+ ISO may default to HWE (UNKNOWN); and the HWE metapackage is expected to move to the 26.10 kernel (7.3) around 26.04.2 (INFERRED: about January to February 2027) and to a new series about every six months after. An installed OEM image would also boot first, since GRUB sorts 7.0.0-10NN-oem above 7.0.0-NN-generic | Step 4 holds the node on the GA line before the update: when HWE metapackages sit over the same 7.0 image and no GA or HWE metapackage is held with `apt-mark hold`, `linux-generic` is installed and the HWE metapackages purged, but only after apt's simulation shows the purge removes nothing else; any other case (an OEM or platform metapackage, an image outside 7.0, a metapackage not from Ubuntu's `linux-meta`, a GA or HWE metapackage held with `apt-mark`, which is the Principal's and is left alone with a warning (fix verification v0.3.5), a purge that would take more) is the to-do `kernel-line`, never automatic; the to-do states the manual route (release any hold first, `apt-mark showhold` then `apt-mark unhold NAME`; install `linux-generic`; boot a 7.0.0-NN-generic kernel, through `GRUB_FLAVOUR_ORDER="generic"` in its own `/etc/default/grub.d/*.cfg` for an OEM kernel or GRUB's Advanced options for a newer -generic one; only then purge, never the running kernel). Every new line runs under the step's errexit, pipefail and ERR trap: a dpkg-query pattern that matches nothing is an empty answer, not an abort, and held packages count as installed (review v0.3.5). Step 5 records the line after the reboot: it closes the to-do once the line is GA 7.0 and otherwise raises it again with this boot's report on every run, so the to-do list shows the kernel the node runs now, not step 4's view from before the upgrade and the reboot; step 4 also closes an open to-do when it finds the line GA (review of option (c); `phase1/kernel_line_test.sh`) |
| S46 | 2, 3.7 | NPU "Not used. Linux LLM tooling is Windows-first" | FastFlowLM, maintained by AMD under ROCm since v1.0.0 (2026-08-11), runs LLMs, Whisper and EmbeddingGemma on XDNA 2 NPUs under Linux (since 2026-03-11) and ships an Ubuntu 26.04 package. 26.04's 7.0 kernel already carries the `amdxdna` driver and the NPU firmware. The report "Linux 7.4 supports the Gorgon Halo NPU" concerns a two-line patch that only names the chip; binding never depended on it, and kernel 7.0 has no name table to patch | The decision stands (not used, no design dependency); its reason is corrected (Section 2) and three watch items are recorded (3.7: W-NPU, W-PERFOPT, W-KERNEL). Step 5 records what the NPU shows |
| S47 | 3.3, 21 (V3a) | V3a: "amdgpu clamps the requested gttsize to physical RAM (VERIFIED on amdgpu_ttm.c v7.0)", so the pool is compared with min(196608 MiB, MemTotal) | Mainline v7.0 and Ubuntu 7.0.0-38, the kernel a dist-upgrade installs today, do not cap it: `mem_info_gtt_total` is the requested 196608 MiB whatever MemTotal is. The cap ("drm/amdgpu: cap GTT size to physical RAM on APUs", upstream 5e70f6804b4d, mainline from v7.2) reaches Ubuntu in 7.0.0-39, in -proposed on 2026-10-09. So V3a would have failed a correctly configured node and stopped the Phase 1 gate (present since fix round 3; VERIFIED in the 7.0.0-38.38 and 7.0.0-39.39 sources by the 2026-10-09 research) | V3a passes when the pool equals the requested 196608 MiB or MemTotal (1 % below, 1 MiB above), quoting the kernel's "Capping GTT" line when there is one; the ~50 % default or anything in between still fails (`verify/v03a_gtt_test.sh`). The Arbiter budgets from the smaller of the two, since MemTotal is the real limit on either kernel (S44) once kexec handover is off (S49: with it on, about 13 GiB of MemTotal would be CMA scratch that GTT pages cannot use) |
| S48 | 3.3, 4.1, 17 steps 4 and 5, 21 (V3a), App. B | The kernel line is the three GPU parameters of 3.3; S44 left the crash-kernel reservation that kdump-tools puts on the GRUB line in place ("reclaiming it would be the Principal's call"), put it at 4 GiB and cut the Arbiter's reserve to 12 GiB around it; what a kernel panic does to the headless node was not stated | kdump-tools 1:1.10.7ubuntu3 is on the 26.04.1 server image (the base layer both install sources carry; a Recommends, not a Depends, of `ubuntu-server-minimal`). Its conffile `/etc/default/grub.d/kdump-tools.cfg` appends `crashkernel=2G-4G:320M,4G-32G:512M,32G-64G:1024M,64G-128G:2048M,128G-:4096M` to `GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX_DEFAULT` unconditionally, whatever `USE_KDUMP` says (VERIFIED). The installer's automatic rule most likely switched kdump itself off on this node, since it wants more than 5 × the memory in GiB, about 935 to 940 GiB, free on `/var` (INFERRED), but it never touches that line. On this machine kernel 7.0 then reserves 4096 MiB above 4 GiB plus an automatic 256 MiB below it: 4.25 GiB (4,563,402,752 bytes) out of MemTotal at every boot (VERIFIED, v7.0 `kernel/crash_reserve.c`). A dump cannot help here. The capture initrd is built by initramfs-tools without cryptsetup: cryptsetup itself is on the image (a Depends of `ubuntu-server-minimal`, which step 2 relies on), but `cryptsetup-initramfs`, the initramfs-tools hook that would add it, is not (cryptsetup only Recommends dracut or `cryptsetup-initramfs`, and dracut, on the image, meets that), so it cannot open the LUKS root by TPM or by passphrase and gives up waiting for it (INFERRED; each step VERIFIED). A dump would hold the keys of both open LUKS volumes and, with model weights in kernel-owned GPU pages that its filter cannot drop, approach the size of the loaded model, on a system volume of about 100 GiB (the installer's default; INFERRED). And the failures most expected here, a GPU hang (amdgpu's job timeout resets the GPU) and memory exhaustion (`vm.panic_on_oom` is 0, so the OOM killer ends a process), do not panic the kernel, so kdump would never see them (INFERRED; the settings VERIFIED). While a crash kernel is loaded, kdump-config sets `kernel.panic_on_oops=1` (hard-coded after every successful load, never reset by an unload), so an oops the kernel would survive becomes a full crash (VERIFIED). Ubuntu's amd64 kernel has `CONFIG_PANIC_TIMEOUT=0` and no shipped sysctl file sets `kernel.panic`: after a panic it waits forever (VERIFIED). With a crash kernel loaded a panic also writes nothing to pstore, because the kernel jumps to the crash kernel first; without one, a panic or an oops writes the compressed tail of the kernel log (about 10 KB) to the firmware's pstore backend, and `systemd-pstore.service` (enabled on new installs) archives it to `/var/lib/systemd/pstore` at the next boot (VERIFIED; whether this board offers a backend, EFI variables or ACPI ERST, is UNKNOWN) | Decided by the Principal on 2026-10-10 (option (c)). Step 4 writes `/etc/default/grub.d/zzz-atlas-crash.cfg` in the same pass as `90-atlas.cfg`, before the one update-grub. grub-mkconfig sources the drop-ins in byte order whatever the locale, so `90-atlas.cfg` comes before `kdump-tools.cfg` and could not undo it, while `zzz-` comes after both and after a `zz-kdump-tools.cfg` (VERIFIED). The drop-in removes every `crashkernel=` and `panic=` word from both kernel-line variables, following the kernel's quoting rule, and appends `panic=10`; it is POSIX sh written so that it cannot abort update-grub under grub-mkconfig's `set -e` (a failing drop-in would also break kdump-tools' upgrades and every kernel install). kdump is switched off without a purge, as the Principal instructed: the debconf answer `kdump-tools/use_kdump=false` and `USE_KDUMP=0` in `/etc/default/kdump-tools` agree, so no upgrade re-enables it or stops at a prompt; `kdump-tools.service` is disabled and masked (both survive upgrades); a crash kernel loaded at that moment is unloaded and `kernel.panic_on_oops` reset to 0. The package stays installed and none of its dpkg conffiles is edited (`/etc/default/kdump-tools` is managed by ucf, not dpkg). kdump-tools is only a Recommends, so unlike snapd (S34) a purge would not have taken a metapackage with it (INFERRED from apt's rules), but a plain removal keeps its conffiles (`crashkernel=` with them), and editing or deleting a conffile makes the next kdump-tools upgrade that changes it stop at a dpkg prompt. Known residue, left on purpose: `/etc/kernel/postinst.d/kdump-tools` still builds an unused capture initrd under `/var/lib/kdump` at every kernel install; and `dpkg-reconfigure kdump-tools` must never be run afterwards (it reads the file back into debconf). After update-grub the step stops if `grub.cfg` carries `crashkernel=` anywhere or its first kernel line does not carry exactly one `panic=10`. V3a also fails on `crashkernel=` anywhere on `/proc/cmdline` (the kernel finds it anywhere, even inside another word), on any reserved crash-kernel memory (`/sys/kernel/kexec_crash_size` non-zero, 4563402752 with kdump-tools' value, or a "Crash kernel" region in `/proc/iomem`), on anything but exactly one `panic=10`, on `kernel.panic` other than 10 and on `kernel.panic_on_oops` other than 0. Step 5 adds a read-only record of the pstore backend, `systemd-pstore.service`, archived crash logs and kdump's state; it warns (no backend, kdump enabled again, archived logs present) and never stops. The Arbiter's reserve is 16 GiB again (S44, 4.1). Tests: `phase1/crash_params_test.sh` (the drop-in under grub-mkconfig's own sourcing lines with the real `kdump-tools.cfg`, `_kdump_off` and the step-5 record) and new cases in `verify/v03a_gtt_test.sh`. Accepted trade-offs: no crash dumps (none could be written); a panic that recurs at every boot restarts the node in a loop instead of leaving it halted. Not covered by `panic=10`, each a separate decision and nothing added: a panic before the kernel reads its parameters; soft lockups, hard lockups and hung tasks, which only warn; a crash of systemd itself, which freezes; a failed TPM unseal, which waits at the passphrase prompt; recovery boots. The restart does not flush the filesystems, the same as a power cut at that moment (which a panic that halts amounts to anyway); a panic before the boot completes adds GRUB's 30-second menu; the TPM unlock that follows is unattended (INFERRED), and the PIN-less TPM key is exempt from dictionary-attack lockout, so a restart loop cannot lock the TPM (VERIFIED in systemd 259.5). Only a deliberate test panic on an idle node would settle this board's reset path and pstore backend; it is on the platform watch-list for the live ATLAS to lead with the Principal present (W-DRILL, 3.7), and in no Day 1 script |
| S49 | 3.3, 4.1, 17 steps 4 and 5, 21 (V3a), App. B | 4.1, S44, S47 and S48 took every page of MemTotal to be able to hold GPU memory: the Arbiter's pool is the smaller of the GTT total and MemTotal, "since MemTotal is the real limit on either kernel" (S47). The kernel line carried no `kho=` | Ubuntu's 7.0 kernel is built with `CONFIG_KEXEC_HANDOVER=y` and `CONFIG_KEXEC_HANDOVER_ENABLE_DEFAULT=y`, alike in 7.0.0-38.38 (resolute-updates and -security) and 7.0.0-39.39 (-proposed on 2026-10-10), so kexec handover (KHO) is on unless the command line says otherwise (VERIFIED from both kernels' configs; Ubuntu's own changes to the code leave that alone). KHO keeps memory for handing state over to a kernel started by kexec. At every cold boot, just before the boot-time allocator hands memory to the page allocator, `kho_reserve_scratch` sets aside scratch areas of 200 % of the kernel's early memblock reservations (a low-memory and a global area) plus 200 % of node 0's share of them again (a per-node area), and at `fs_initcall` `kho_init` frees them to the page allocator as `MIGRATE_CMA` pageblocks (VERIFIED, v7.0 `kernel/liveupdate/kexec_handover.c` and `mm/mm_init.c`). They count in MemTotal, but CMA pageblocks serve only movable allocations, and TTM, which allocates the pages behind every GTT buffer, asks with `GFP_USER` or `GFP_HIGHUSER` and never `__GFP_MOVABLE` (VERIFIED, v7.0 `drivers/gpu/drm/ttm/ttm_pool.c` and `mm/page_alloc.c`). On this machine the page map of about 3 GiB dominates the early reservations, so the scratch comes to about 13 GiB (12.8 to 13.8; INFERRED from the sizing rule; a pull request to NVIDIA's NV-Kernels measured 9.3 GiB on the 128 GB GB10, whose page map is 2 GiB, which scales to about 13.3 to 13.9 GiB here). Only about 174 GiB of MemTotal could then hold GTT pages, while the Arbiter's budget of about 164 GiB lets the Apex f16 and q8_0 rungs (160.0 and 160.3 GiB, S2) take 166 to 168 GiB of them with the 6 to 8 GiB resident set, beside the kernel's own unmovable memory: they would likely fail with a Vulkan out-of-memory in Phase 3 although the Arbiter granted them (INFERRED). Before S48, with kdump-tools' crash-kernel reservation in place, the scratch would have been about 21.8 GiB, because both crash regions count twice, the 4096 MiB region above 4 GiB in the global area and the 256 MiB region below 4 GiB in the low-memory area (neither in node 0's area, since both are reserved without a node) (INFERRED; the counting VERIFIED). `CmaTotal` in `/proc/meminfo` never counts the scratch (it counts named CMA areas only) and `CmaFree` counts only its free part; the zone `cma` lines of `/proc/zoneinfo` count all of it, in 4 KiB pages (VERIFIED, v7.0 `mm/vmstat.c` and `mm/cma.c`). Nothing on ATLAS uses KHO. It serves a kernel started by kexec, which ATLAS never does (systemd 259.5 and kexec-tools load no kexec image by default; Ubuntu's build skips KHO for kdump's crash images, and kdump is off, S48), and the live-update feature built on it, which stays off unless `liveupdate=on` is given (VERIFIED). `kho=off`, an early parameter read as a boolean, makes `kho_reserve_scratch` and `kho_init` return at once: nothing is set aside or marked CMA, the same pages are freed as ordinary memory, MemTotal is unchanged (to within one 4 KiB page) and all of it can hold GTT pages (VERIFIED). Field reports, VERIFIED from their commits: the NV-Kernels pull request (#590, a community contribution) found "about 9.3 GiB of CMA pageblocks even though CmaTotal is zero" on GB10, with pinning memory for an mlx5 network adapter failing with ENOMEM under GPU memory pressure, and NVIDIA's own 7.0 kernel branch carries an equivalent commit that turns the default off on arm64 only, keeping amd64 on; Supabase's Postgres images put `kho=off` on the kernel line through a GRUB drop-in ("we don't use kexec to reboot anyway"). A Proxmox patch one verifier cited could not be confirmed, and Proxmox's own kernel configuration has no such change up to 2026-10-09 (UNKNOWN) | Decided by the Principal on 2026-10-10. `zzz-atlas-crash.cfg` (S48), the drop-in grub-mkconfig sources last, also removes every `kho=` word from both kernel-line variables, quoted or not, and appends `kho=off` after `panic=10`, so the normal and advanced entries carry exactly one `kho=`; recovery entries get none, since no engine runs there. It is not in `90-atlas.cfg`, which sorts before `kdump-tools.cfg` and any later drop-in, because the kernel takes the last `kho=` it can parse. `kho_scratch=` and `liveupdate=` are left alone; with `kho=off` neither does anything. After update-grub step 4 stops unless the first kernel line of `grub.cfg` carries exactly one `kho=` word and it is `kho=off`, and its log records the scratch the running kernel holds (zone `cma` pages less `CmaTotal`). On the first run that boot still has KHO on and also kdump-tools' crash-kernel reservation, so the figure should come out near the 21.8 GiB estimated for that case; less about 8.5 GiB, the reservation counted twice, it is the measured counterpart of the 13 GiB estimate (INFERRED). V3a fails unless `/proc/cmdline` carries exactly one `kho=` word and it is the literal `kho=off` (the kernel would also read `kho=0` or `kho=n` as off, but the gate wants one spelling); it also fails on kexec handover scratch still set aside (zone `cma` pages beyond `CmaTotal`, or `CmaFree` above 0 with `CmaTotal` 0, the case of a boot that is itself a handover), on any named CMA area (`CmaTotal` above 0: GTT pages cannot use one either, and this kernel's configuration creates none unless the command line asks for one, so a correct node cannot fail it) and when `/proc/zoneinfo` is unreadable or has no `cma` lines; its summary records the figures. The TPM's PCR 7 binding does not cover the kernel command line, so the unattended unlock is unaffected, as with `panic=10` (INFERRED). The Arbiter, its 16 GiB reserve and the 4.1 figures are unchanged and now rest on `kho=off` (`arbiter.py` and `config.py` say so). Accepted: a kernel started by kexec could no longer receive handover state; ATLAS never kexecs. Tests: new cases in `phase1/crash_params_test.sh` and `verify/v03a_gtt_test.sh` |
| S50 | 3.6, 17 steps 4, 5 and 8, 21 (V24), App. B | Nothing armed a watchdog: 3.6 named none, and S48 listed what `panic=10` leaves uncovered as "each a separate decision and nothing added": a panic before the kernel reads its parameters; soft lockups, hard lockups and hung tasks, which only warn; a crash of systemd itself, which freezes (`CrashAction=freeze`); a failed TPM unseal, which waits at the passphrase prompt; recovery boots. A hard hang that never panics would leave the headless node stopped until someone cycled the power | The platform's watchdog is the TCO timer of the FCH SMBus controller, PCI 1022:790b at 00:14.0, revision 0x71 on Strix Halo (a Framework Desktop and a second machine) and on the Zen 5 siblings Strix Point and Ryzen AI 7 445 (VERIFIED from field `lspci` output). No public log from a Ryzen AI Max+ 495 exists; Gorgon Halo is reported as a clock and memory refresh of the same silicon, as the Ryzen AI 7 445 is of Krackan with the same controller and revision, so the same controller is expected here (INFERRED; step 4 reads it). Kernel 7.0's `sp5100_tco` drives it on its MMIO path, used for revision 0x51 and later (VERIFIED, v7.0 `tco_reg_layout`; Ubuntu 7.0.0-38.38 only adds Hygon's vendor id to that test). The driver stops the timer when it loads and never reports it as running, so loading it arms nothing; opening `/dev/watchdog*` starts the timer, and closing it without the magic 'V' leaves it running with nothing to ping it (VERIFIED, v7.0 `sp5100_tco.c`, `watchdog_dev.c`). Its timeout runs from 1 to 65535 seconds (60 by default), it has no pretimeout, `nowayout` is 0 (`CONFIG_WATCHDOG_NOWAYOUT` is not set in 7.0.0-38), it has no suspend handling, and the kernel stops it in its reboot notifier, before the kernel's own device shutdown (VERIFIED). When the firmware has disabled the timer the probe fails with "Watchdog hardware is disabled", but module init still returns 0: `modprobe` exits 0 and leaves the module loaded with no device (VERIFIED, v7.0 `sp5100_tco.c` and `drivers/base/dd.c`). In the field it binds on the HP ZBook Ultra G1a and the GMKtec EVO-X2 (Strix Halo) and on the Zen 5 siblings, logging "Using 0xfeb00000 for watchdog MMIO address" and "initialized. heartbeat=60 sec (nowayout=0)", and it has ended hard freezes on the EVO-X2, whose next boot logged "Previous system reset reason [0x02000800]: hardware watchdog timer expired" (VERIFIED). One report from an unnamed Strix Halo mini PC says it did not fire during a 6-hour freeze; that is inconclusive, since the freeze was an amdgpu wedge during which systemd may still have been pinging. No family 0x1a log read shows "Watchdog hardware is disabled"; whether this board's firmware leaves the timer enabled, offers a BIOS setting for it or has an ACPI WDAT table (none in the Strix Halo, Strix Point and Krackan boot logs read) is UNKNOWN until step 4. Ubuntu blacklists the driver: `linux-modules-7.0.0-38-generic` ships `/usr/lib/modprobe.d/blacklist_linux_7.0.0-38-generic.conf`, which its packaging generates ("blacklist all watchdog drivers (LP:1432837)") from every module in `drivers/watchdog` except `bcm2835_wdt` and `sbsa_gwdt`: 69 entries, `sp5100_tco`, `softdog` and `wdat_wdt` among them, with a new copy for each kernel ABI (VERIFIED). udev, systemd-modules-load (`/etc/modules-load.d`, `modules_load=`) and `modprobe@.service` apply that blacklist even to a module named explicitly; systemd 259.5 logs "Module 'sp5100_tco' is deny-listed (by kmod)" at INFO and still succeeds, so those routes load nothing. Only a plain `modprobe NAME` (kmod 34.2, without `-b`) loads a blacklisted module, and kmod has no directive that lifts a blacklist line (VERIFIED, systemd 259.5 `module-util.c`, kmod 34.2 `libkmod-module.c` and `modprobe.c`). The three modules ship in `linux-modules`, not `-extra`, and are signed, so they load under Secure Boot (VERIFIED). systemd 259.5 on Ubuntu 26.04: `RuntimeWatchdogSec` off, `RebootWatchdogSec` 10min, `KExecWatchdogSec` off, `CrashAction` freeze, `WatchdogDevice` unset (systemd then tries `/dev/watchdog0`, then `/dev/watchdog`); Ubuntu's `system.conf` and patches change none of them, and no package ships a watchdog drop-in (VERIFIED). With a runtime timeout T, systemd pings every T/2 when idle and at most every T/4, retries opening a missing device at most every min(T/2, 5 s), and after a crash its `freeze()` closes every descriptor, the watchdog's too, without the magic 'V' (VERIFIED, systemd 259.5 `manager.c`, `watchdog.c`, `process-util.c`). A reboot arms an existing device for 10 minutes even without `RuntimeWatchdogSec`, so a loaded driver alone already guards systemd's last reboot phase; a poweroff or halt disarms it (VERIFIED). dracut 110 puts the watchdog modules into the initramfs and, from the next rebuild, the S50 drop-in too, but it also copies the blacklist in, and the blacklist alone keeps any watchdog driver from loading there (INFERRED); a timer left running when the board reset is stopped only by the driver in the real root, and whether this board's reset clears it first is UNKNOWN. In the field, 30-second timeouts reset live, recoverable Strix Halo machines (a hung NFS mount stalled systemd on a Framework Desktop, whose owner moved to 2 minutes; memory-reclaim thrash starved userspace on an EVO-X2); systemd raised its own services' watchdogs to 3 minutes after disk stalls of over a minute (NEWS, v227); no report of a false reset by `RuntimeWatchdogSec` under memory pressure was found (UNKNOWN). softdog is a timer inside the kernel: it covers a frozen or crashed systemd, since closing it without 'V' leaves it active, but not reliably a hard lockup (INFERRED); with `soft_panic=1` its expiry is a real panic, so a pstore record and then the `panic=10` restart (VERIFIED, v7.0 `softdog.c`). It must never be loaded beside a hardware driver: whichever registers first becomes `watchdog0`, the device systemd opens, and systemd's "Using hardware watchdog" wording is the same for both (VERIFIED). No lockup panic is needed for the hardware watchdog to work: the timer resets the node on its own once the pings stop (INFERRED from the driver and the field resets) | Decided by the Principal on 2026-10-11 ("add the hardware watchdog"). Step 4 ends, before the reboot marker, with `_watchdog`. It records the SMBus controller's ids (`/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:00:14.0/{vendor,device,revision}`; 0x1022 0x790b 0x71 expected), whether `/sys/firmware/acpi/tables/WDAT` exists, what `/sys/class/watchdog` held and systemd's `RuntimeWatchdogUSec` (0 on a fresh node). It runs `modprobe sp5100_tco` by name and does not take a zero exit status as proof: hardware counts only when a `/sys/class/watchdog/watchdogN` has the identity exactly `SP5100 TCO timer` and its `device/driver` resolves to `sp5100-tco`. Otherwise it unloads the module, tries `wdat_wdt` (identity `wdat_wdt`, driver `wdat_wdt`) only when the WDAT table exists, unloading it too if it makes no device, and with neither chooses `softdog soft_panic=1` for the next boot without loading it now. It never opens `/dev/watchdog*` and never loads softdog itself. It writes `/etc/systemd/system/atlas-watchdog-driver.service` (`DefaultDependencies=no`, `After=systemd-modules-load.service`, `Before=sysinit.target shutdown.target`, `Conflicts=shutdown.target`; a oneshot with `RemainAfterExit=yes` and `ExecStart=/usr/sbin/modprobe <driver>`; `WantedBy=sysinit.target`), enables it with `systemctl --no-reload enable`, and then writes `/etc/systemd/system.conf.d/90-atlas-watchdog.conf` with only `[Manager]`, `RuntimeWatchdogSec=2min` and `RebootWatchdogSec=10min`. Both files are rewritten whole on every run, and the dpkg conffile `/etc/systemd/system.conf` is never edited. Nothing reloads systemd during the step (a daemon-reload with the drop-in present and the driver loaded would arm the timer mid-step), so the watchdog is armed from the reboot on; a hardware driver found by the probe stays loaded, so step 4's own reboot already runs under the default 10-minute reboot watchdog. `WatchdogDevice` (with one driver, systemd's default is its device), `RuntimeWatchdogPreSec` (no pretimeout), `KExecWatchdogSec` and `CrashAction` are left alone. The step never stops for a missing hardware watchdog; it stops only when the unit cannot be enabled. The to-do `watchdog` is raised by every run that falls back to softdog, with that run's findings and the re-run command `phase1 --force 04 --force 05`, and closed by the run that finds a hardware watchdog. Step 5 runs V24 (`verify/v24-watchdog.sh 120 600`) right after V3a and stops on a fail, as for V3a. V24 reads only `systemctl show`, sysfs, `/proc` and this boot's journal; it never opens `/dev/watchdog*`, never reads the sysfs `status` file (a read clears a flag) and never runs `wdctl`. It passes when the drop-in exists; systemd reports `RuntimeWatchdogUSec` 2min, `RebootWatchdogUSec` 10min and `KExecWatchdogUSec` and `RuntimeWatchdogPreUSec` 0; the unit is enabled and active; `/sys/class/watchdog` holds exactly one device, `watchdog0`, which is hardware (identity not `Software Watchdog`, bound to a driver), active, with timeout 120, `nowayout` 0 and a timeleft of 1 to 120; systemd's `WatchdogDevice` is `/dev/watchdog0` (or `/dev/watchdog`), systemd holds it and no other process holds any `/dev/watchdog*`; its last ping (`WatchdogLastPingTimestampMonotonic` against `/proc/uptime`) is under 120 seconds old; and this boot's systemd journal has "Using hardware watchdog /dev/watchdog0: '<identity>', ..." with no open or ping failure after the last such line. The same with softdog is deferred; anything else is a fail, no watchdog device at all included (step 4 always installs a driver). It also records `bootstatus` (32 when the driver saw that the previous boot ended in a watchdog reset; whether that bit survives a reset on this board is UNKNOWN) and this boot's "Previous system reset reason" line, which a watchdog reset turns into "hardware watchdog timer expired" and which the kernel clears at boot, so that boot's journal is the only record. The Phase 1 gate is `gate phase1 V2 V3a V5 V19 V24 -- V1`, and `tools/fill-workbook.py` fills rows V1 to V24. Not added, each outside "the hardware watchdog" or left to the Principal: lockup panics (`softlockup_panic`, `hardlockup_panic`, `hung_task_panic`; they could reset the node during a long but legitimate I/O or GPU stall), `CrashAction=reboot`, `watchdog.stop_on_reboot=0` (it would also cover the kernel's own device shutdown, but a timer that survived into the next boot could reset a LUKS passphrase wait into a loop; UNKNOWN), `rd.driver.pre=sp5100_tco`, masks on the sleep targets, the `watchdog` daemon package, any kernel-line or initramfs change, and a deliberate test reset, the only proof of this board's reset path (in no Day 1 script; on the platform watch-list for the live ATLAS to lead with the Principal present, W-DRILL in 3.7). Open for the Principal: whether to keep the softdog fallback or record no watchdog instead, whether 2 minutes (or 60 seconds) is the timeout wanted, and each item just listed. Tests: `phase1/watchdog_test.sh` (76 cases: `_watchdog` against stubbed `modprobe`, `dmesg` and `systemctl`, the rendered unit under `systemd-analyze verify`, step 5's hand-off, the Phase 1 gate with the real `lib/common.sh`, and `tools/fill-workbook.py`) and `verify/v24_watchdog_test.sh` (52 cases); neither opens a watchdog device |
| S51 | Header, 0, 3.1, 3.3, 3.4, 5.2, 17 (Phase 4 step 1), 18 (C2), 20 (R1, R3), 21 (V11), 23 (S15, watch-list), App. D | The Principal asked on 2026-10-11 whether Ubuntu would adopt AMD's ROCm 10.1 (released 2026-10-05). The baseline's reason for keeping ROCm off the host was that it "does not install cleanly" on 26.04 (3.4, C2; R3 "Certain"). It cited the hang reports as "ROCm/pytorch #6530, #6182", all on kernel 7.0, with a 6.18 kernel as the mitigation (R1, S15), and called 10.0.0 "the version the gfx1151 wheels were validated with" (watch-list) and the wheel "community" (17, V11).
