The 26.04.1 server image has no ROCm or HIP package, nothing in Ubuntu installs ROCm on detecting an AMD GPU, and no Day 1 script installs one. Ubuntu's own ROCm is a separate line: 7.1 in 26.04's `universe`, 7.2.4 in `-proposed`, 10.0 only in 26.10. Even a later Canonical update to a 10.x line would not reach the node, because unattended-upgrades touches only installed packages, from the release and security pockets. ATLAS's ROCm changes only when the Phase 4 pins are edited and the image is rebuilt; what changes unattended is the kernel, through `-security` (3.4). 10.0.0 is AMD-documented for gfx1151 on 26.04 with the GA 7.0 kernel and is the version the Day 1 scripts were written against; nothing has run on the node yet. The hang reports are corrected in 3.4 (R1). The one leak: AMD's docs install both 10.0.0 and 10.1.0 builds from the same stable index, and pip takes the newest version a requirement allows. AMD's 10.1.0 docs name torchaudio 2.11.0.3+rocm10.1.0 and 2.11.0.2+rocm10.1.0 on that index, and upstream torchaudio 2.11 declares no torch requirement, so the bare `torchaudio` would most likely have put a +rocm10.1.0 build into the 10.0.0 image (INFERRED). Whether that build loads on torch 2.13.0 is UNKNOWN, and neither the build checks nor V11 would have noticed. AMD's documented gfx1151 pairing for torch 2.13.0 on ROCm 10.0.0 is torchaudio 2.11.0.2+rocm10.0.0; the +rocm label is needed, because `==2.11.0.2` alone also matches 2.11.0.2+rocm10.1.0. Following the "drop the pins" advice would now silently adopt 10.1 and torch 2.14 (INFERRED) | Fixed with the Principal's approval of 2026-10-11 ("fix the torchaudio pin and the doc corrections"). torchaudio is pinned to `2.11.0.2+rocm10.0.0`: `ARG TORCHAUDIO_VER=2.11.0.2` in `docker/rocm-base/Dockerfile`, installed as `torchaudio==${TORCHAUDIO_VER}+rocm${ROCM_VER}`, and `base_image.torchaudio` in `config/phase4-engines.json`. Four checks hold the pin: the build fails unless the constraints file has exactly that torchaudio line (as it already did for torch); Phase 4 step 1 stops before the build when the Dockerfile ARG is missing or the json disagrees with it; the wheel pre-flight (`P4-wheels`) looks up the exact pin instead of the bare name; and V11 fails unless torchaudio's installed version is exactly the json pin, recording whether it imports (the opt-in community-image diagnostic only reports it). The Dockerfile and the build-failure message now say never to drop the pins: a pin that cannot be resolved means re-checking AMD's install page for the same ROCm version, and a move to another ROCm version is the deliberate re-test on the watch-list, never a build fix. The scripts' hang-report references are corrected too. The decision on 10.1 is unchanged: Phase 4 stays on 10.0.0 until a deliberate re-test after Day 1 (watch-list, below). After a kernel ABI change or a GPU firmware update V11 is re-run by hand; nothing does it on its own (3.4). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/test_phase4_pins.py` (24 cases: the Dockerfile, json and wording checks, the driver's pre-flight call, V11's script and `selftest.py`'s torchaudio check) and `orchestrator/tests/test_wheel_preflight.py` (34 cases, 4 of them new) |
| S52 | Header, 4.1, 4.2, 4.3, 4.4, 17 (Phase 3 steps 2 to 4), 21 (V21), 23 (S4, S22, watch-list), App. A, App. B, App. D | The Principal asked on 2026-10-11 about the XDA article "Your local LLM is quietly wasting RAM on context you never use, and one setting gives it back" (published on or before 2026-10-05; the proxy refuses the site, so it was read through search excerpts and two reposts, INFERRED). Its argument: a runner reserves memory for the whole configured context window when the model loads, not for what is typed, so the window should be sized to real use (its example: `num_ctx 8192` in Ollama), with a quantised KV cache and fewer parallel requests as further levers. The baseline already set every context explicitly and quantised the cache per engine (4.3). 4.1 and 4.3 ran the vision engine at 2 slots "while co-resident" with gpt-oss, which is what lets the everyday pair fit (157.7 GiB projected); the scripts rendered that profile only for an `overrides.json` coresident=true, which Phase 3 step 3 set for its own test and then restored to 8 slots. 4.3 said context shift with `--keep` means "anchors never evicted; generation never hard-stops". The 16 GiB host reserve (S44) named no llama-server cache | VERIFIED in the source of the pinned llama.cpp (v0.4.1, commit b29c606) unless marked. The article's mechanism holds here: the KV cache is allocated in full for `--ctx-size` when the engine loads, cleared and never resized, and on this integrated GPU every page is written, so the memory is committed until the engine unloads; attention covers only the filled cells, so unused context costs memory, not decode speed. `--ctx-size` is llama-server's total pool and `--parallel` divides it; it is Ollama that multiplies `num_ctx` by `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL` (VERIFIED, Ollama v0.40.3), so on ATLAS fewer slots free no attention cache, only the small per-slot parts (sliding-window cells, recurrent and compressor state). Without `--kv-unified`, which ATLAS does not pass, each slot is a hard limit per request: 32k on the core engines, 8k on the router, 4k on Meditron. What follows for ATLAS. (1) The vision pair: the Arbiter projects what the unit's env file says, and that file rendered 8 × 32k (KV 42.5 GiB, 121.5 GiB projected, about 37 GiB of the cache in idle slots) unless `overrides.json` said coresident=true, so in production the Arbiter would have swapped gpt-oss out instead of pairing (VERIFIED in simulation with the real Arbiter code). At 2 × 32k the vision cache is 10.6 GiB. One generation runs at a time (4.2 rule 3), so the extra slots are warm prompt caches, not concurrency (INFERRED). (2) Context shift is switched off, with the log line "ctx_shift is not supported by this context" (or "by multimodal"), on 6 of the 8 chat engines: both gpt-oss engines (a sliding-window cache without `--swa-full`), Qwen3.5-122B and the Qwen3.5-4B router (IMROPE), Qwen2.5-VL (MROPE and its mmproj) and DeepSeek V4 (its compressed cache cannot shift). Only Nemotron 3 Super and Meditron keep it. On every engine, whatever context shift does, a prompt longer than its slot is rejected (HTTP 400, `exceed_context_size_error`), never truncated: context shift acts only during generation, so restoring it (`--swa-full` on gpt-oss) would not admit a longer prompt. On the six, generation also stops at the slot end (finish `length`), and `--keep` does nothing; a request without `max_tokens` has no other limit. (3) Host RAM outside the budget: each chat llama-server keeps a prompt cache in host RAM, up to 8192 MiB by default (`--cache-ram`), which should fill in normal use because `--cache-idle-slots`, on by default, copies every idle slot into it at each new task; and where the cache cannot roll back (sliding-window and recurrent models: both gpt-oss engines, Nemotron, Qwen3.5-122B, the router and DeepSeek V4; INFERRED from the code) up to 32 context checkpoints per slot (`--ctx-checkpoints`). Neither is on the GPU memory counter or in the Arbiter's grants; only the 16 GiB host reserve covers them, and two engines and the router at the defaults could exceed it (INFERRED). Their sizes are TRACE lines, which llama-server does not log at its default verbosity. (4) The Arbiter's per-token KV estimates err high on purpose: estimates from the models' structure put gpt-oss at about 2.7 GiB against 5.06 projected, Qwen3.5's attention cache at about 3.2 GiB against 15.00 before its unmeasured recurrent state, and DeepSeek V4 far lower (INFERRED); the real figures are UNKNOWN until Phase 3. (5) Since a May 2026 change (commit 67b2b7f2f) the pinned build logs the library's INFO lines as TRACE, which llama-server prints only from verbosity 4 (its default is 3): the K/V, recurrent and compressor size lines, the compute-buffer lines, `llama_context: flash_attn =` and V4's proof line (S22) never reach the journal at the default, so V4 could not pass on any engine, a gap older than S52 that its review found (VERIFIED in the source and by running a build of the pin). At verbosity 5 or with `-v`, the trial context `--fit` always builds first prints a second set of K/V and compute lines. Nothing has run on the node | Approved by the Principal on 2026-10-11 ("fix the vision setting and the cut-off handling now, and add the other two as Phase 3 items"). **Vision profile.** The co-resident profile, 2 × 32k (`--ctx-size 65536 --parallel 2`, `ATLAS_CORESIDENT=1`), is the vision engine's standing profile, alone or paired: `phase2/engine-env.py` renders it for any engine whose engines.json entry defines one (`has_coresident_profile`); the Arbiter projects the same profile before a unit exists (`config.py` `EngineSpec.standing_coresident` and `profile()`; the earlier rule that an engine ending up alone is projected at its full profile is gone); `atlas-admin engines list` shows it. 8 × 32k is rendered only for an explicit `overrides.json` coresident=false (`engine-env.py --set-override qwen2.5-vl-72b coresident false`, root), the Principal's choice; Phase 3 never sets or clears that field. The unit still wins over engines.json: a unit at 8 slots is projected at 8 slots and evicts gpt-oss, and the grant then names `engine-env.py --clear-override qwen2.5-vl-72b coresident`. With the real engines.json rendered by engine-env.py and the real Arbiter code (stub probe and controller), gpt-oss (68.06 GiB) and then vision (89.62 GiB) stay resident together, 157.69 GiB charged against a 163 or 164 GiB budget, where the code before this change evicted gpt-oss (VERIFIED in simulation; on the node UNKNOWN until Phase 3). Cost: 6 fewer warm vision prompt caches when vision runs alone; every request still gets 32k. A measured footprint (4.2 rule 1) is charged only at the profile the engine ran when it was registered (`Arbiter.measured_profile`; registered while not resident, the standing profile; `GET /arbiter/status` shows it): the review found that the 8 × 32k figure Phase 3 measures under coresident=false would otherwise be charged to the 2 × 32k unit once the override was cleared and evict gpt-oss (VERIFIED in simulation with the real code). Measurements are held in the orchestrator's memory only, so after a restart the estimates govern again. Phase 3 step 3 writes, journals and restores no override: it checks `ATLAS_CORESIDENT=1` and the 2-slot values on the rendered unit and stops (an infrastructure stop, not a V21 fail) when `overrides.json` says coresident=false, naming the `--clear-override` command. **Per-slot window.** Every generation the orchestrator sends to a llama-server leaves through one method, `engines.LlamaClient._open_chat`: the chat pipeline, `/internal` (the Celery tasks, LightRAG, the Phase 3 load test), inline Deep Think, the router's classifier, and the strong cross-checker's client once it is wired. The window is one slot of the profile the unit runs, `ATLAS_CTX_SIZE // ATLAS_PARALLEL` from its env file, read on every request (`api.engine_window`; the smallest per-slot figure in engines.json when no env file exists). The engine counts the prompt itself (`POST /v1/chat/completions/input_tokens`, the generation's own template and tokenizer pass, image tokens included; `/apply-template` and `/tokenize`, with 4,098 tokens per image, if that route answers 404). `engines.fit_to_window` keeps every leading system message (layers 1 to 4 of 4.4) and the current turn, and drops the oldest whole exchanges (a user message with all its replies) only until prompt + a bounded reply reserve + 32 fits the slot. The reserve is min(`max_tokens`, max(512, slot / 4)), with the chat API's 4,096 (`DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS`) when a request sets no `max_tokens`: 4,096 on a 32k slot, 8,192 there for any `max_tokens` from 8,192 up, 2,048 on the router and 1,024 on Meditron. The fewest exchanges that leave it are found by bisection; when the prefix and the current turn alone cannot leave it, every earlier exchange goes (no bisection). It then caps `max_tokens` so prompt + reply + 32 never reaches the slot end (a request without one gets the room left). The review found that reserving the whole reply asked for dropped history that fit: LightRAG's entity-extraction gleaning call (on by default in 1.5.7, sent to the router's 8k slot without `max_tokens`) lost the text it extracts from, and a chat whose client asks for `max_tokens` 32768 lost every earlier exchange; with the bounded reserve both are sent whole (VERIFIED against the stub llama-server). The quarter-slot cap is a policy figure, the Principal's to change. When the prefix and the current turn alone leave less than min(`max_tokens`, 512) tokens, nothing is sent: `ContextWindowExceeded`, which the API answers with status 413, type `context_window_exceeded`, naming the engine, its window and the prompt size: the HTTP status of a non-streamed request, public or `/internal`; a streamed request, Open WebUI's included, has already been answered HTTP 200, so the 413 arrives as the error chunk's `error.status` with the `[ATLAS]` text (ledger row failed, no 9.4 strike, since nothing was generated); the classifier then falls back as for any classifier fault, and a cross-check is recorded failed. The server's own exceed-context error is mapped to the same error. Each trim or clamp is one log line with counts only, never content, the reply reserve included. The rule holds on all eight chat engines, Nemotron and Meditron included, and the context-shift flags stay. Cost: one extra loopback count per request, 2 + ceil(log2 n) when n earlier exchanges must be weighed. Phase 3's own measurement requests straight to the engines' ports are short, fixed-length and outside the orchestrator. **Phase 3 records and to-dos.** Each load of step 2, each DeepSeek ladder rung that loaded included, adds a record to its result file (field `memory`) and one "memory (S52)" log line: the context memory from that load's own log (the `llama_kv_cache`, `llama_memory_recurrent` and `llama_dsv4_comp_state` "size =" lines, sliding-window and DeepSeek sub-caches labelled), the compute buffers (the mmproj's apart), the slot window, the context-shift state (switched off and why, kept, not requested, or UNKNOWN), the prompt-cache and checkpoint lines, and VmRSS, VmHWM, RssAnon, RssFile and RssShmem of the unit's main process after the load and after the tests, beside the Arbiter's projection for the same profile (`projected_footprint`, run in the orchestrator's venv). These size, compute and cache lines are TRACE in the pinned build, so every chat and vision engine now starts with `--verbosity 4` (`phase2/engine-env.py`), a log level only, which V4 needs too: exactly 4, because at 5 or with `-v` `--fit`'s trial context adds a second set of K/V lines; the parser also discards everything logged before `--fit`'s closing line, so they are never counted twice. A journal without them (a unit started before that render, a rotated journal) leaves the figures UNKNOWN, and RssAnon's growth over the tests is the measured bound. Step 4 first runs `loadtest.py memory`, which also reads the resident router's current journal and host RAM, and raises the to-dos `kv-estimates` and `cache-ram` through `todo_add` (a new `TODO` line on loadtest.py's stdout, printed by `memory` only); a re-run of step 4 raises them again with the latest figures, and only the Principal closes them. The values the `cache-ram` to-do suggests are INFERRED: `--cache-ram` = the engine's measured context memory rounded up to 256 MiB, at most 8192; `--ctx-checkpoints` = slot window / 8192 + 1 (5 for 32k slots), where checkpoints are made and also where the log cannot tell (no K/V, recurrent or compressor size line: the item then says checkpoints UNKNOWN, never "none made", and its totals read "at least"; the cap does nothing on an engine that makes none); both would go in engines.json `extra_args`. The `kv-estimates` to-do says that a measured footprint replaces the estimate only at its own profile and only until the orchestrator restarts, after which the estimates govern every load, pairing and eviction, so a corrected estimate changes real decisions. A record never fails anything (a line not found is UNKNOWN), and Phase 3 changes no engine setting. **Recorded, not adopted (the Principal's call):** `--swa-full` on the gpt-oss engines (it restores context shift there for about 2.35 GiB of real memory per engine, within the 5.06 GiB the Arbiter already budgets, INFERRED); `--kv-unified` on Qwen3.5-122B (one request could use the whole 262,144-token pool, with no memory saved, at the cost of the other slots' cached prompts, and never with `--cache-ram 0`; long documents stay on retrieval, 4.3); a smaller context on any engine (little memory back, and on Arthur's engines, which always run alone, nothing that could use it). Tests: `orchestrator/tests/test_context_window.py` (31 cases, new, among them LightRAG's gleaning call on the router and a fitting chat asking for `max_tokens` 32768, both sent whole; the stub llama-server is in `tests/stubs.py`), `test_phase3_loadtest.py` (64, 20 of them new), `test_arbiter.py` (42, among them the measured profile) and `test_engine_env.py` (24, with the end-to-end render-then-Arbiter pairing and `--verbosity 4`); the package suite runs 476 passed and 4 skipped (421 before) |
| S53 | Header, 9.4, 9.6, 10.4, 16.2, 20 (R26 to R28), 22, 23 (watch-list), App. B, App. C, App. D | The Principal asked on 2026-10-12 whether the 72-hour semantic pruning (9.6) should be improved or made usage-adaptive: whether ATLAS should measure how its memory is used and adjust what it keeps. 9.6 said "a Celery beat job every 72 hours" sweeps both layers and compresses temporal data into a dated archive under `/srv/cold`; it named no setting, gave no reason for 72 hours and did not say what the archive holds. 10.4 (D9) fixes the retention numbers. Appendix B listed no retention key | VERIFIED in the code at commit e6cadea unless marked; nothing has run on the node. (1) The sweep is a Celery task on the `cpu` queue that a systemd timer enqueues (S23): `atlas-prune.timer` fires daily at 03:10 (up to 30 minutes of random delay) and `atlas-prune.service` enqueues only when its stamp is older than 71 hours, so the cadence is at least 71 hours and survives reboots; the stamp is touched after a successful enqueue (`atlas-admin enqueue prune`, no `--wait`), so it records that the message was sent, not how the sweep ended. (2) The 72-hour window was already a setting, not a constant: the task reads `PRUNE_WINDOW_HOURS` from `orchestrator.env` and falls back to 72 only when the key is absent, but no Day 1 step wrote the key, so it was invisible and undocumented; and the only write helper, `ensure_kv`, rewrites a key's value on every run, so a value the Principal set by hand would have been reset by any re-run of step 2 (`--force 02`). (3) The window governs no current writer: a document is expired when its writer's `expires_at` has passed, and only a document with no expiry falls back to the window; the only temporal writers are the raw chat turns (`CHAT_TURN_TTL_HOURS`, 90 days, D9) and Sentinel's BLUFs (`SENTINEL_KEEP_DAYS`, 366 days, D9), and the only graph writer, the nightly chat summary, writes permanent documents, so the graph part of the sweep is idle today. In practice "72 hours" is the cadence, so an expired note can linger up to three days. (4) Two independent 90-day values exist: the chat-turn TTL constant in `atlas.api` and `OPENWEBUI_CHAT_RETENTION_DAYS=90` in `orchestrator.env`, read by the nightly 04:15 chat-retention task that summarises Open WebUI chats into the Vector Cortex and the graph and deletes them; a test pins the Sentinel default at 366. (5) The ledger's 30-day purge of operational rows (`Ledger.purge_expired`; `RETENTION_S`: `arbiter_decisions`, `routing_decisions`, `tasks` and decided `approvals` 30 days, `sentinel_pulses` 365 days; `strikes` and `meta` permanent) is written, takes its archive callback as a required argument, names the prune as its intended caller in its own docstring, and has no caller: the ledger grows without bound (R26). (6) The archive held vector documents with id, text and metadata, but graph documents as index rows only (id, timestamp, hemisphere, expiry and the caller's metadata; the wrapper's index never held the text), so a pruned graph note could not be restored; no restore command exists for anything. (7) A failed sweep was silent: `TaskRecord.failed` updates the ledger row and logs, ntfy fired only on a vault breach, Sentinel does not watch the prune, and with the stamp touched at enqueue a sweep that failed in the worker was neither retried nor reported before the next 71-hour slot (R28). (8) No usage signal exists: the chat path keeps only the text of the notes it retrieves (no last-used time, no count), only the hemisphere collection and scars are read during chat, and the sentinel and documents collections and the graph have no reader yet, so a usage counter for them would read zero by construction. (9) The pinned Chroma 1.5.9 (image and client) soft-deletes: a delete marks the vector in the HNSW index, labels are never reused, capacity grows to the next power of two of everything ever added and never shrinks, there is no TTL (feature request #2139, open since 2024) and no compaction, so a collection's index tracks inserts ever, not live notes, and only a rebuild from the live documents reclaims it (R27); the image runs the Rust local segment, and the Python segment soft-deletes the same way (VERIFIED in the source re-fetched at the tag and in its hnswlib fork). On persist that segment writes only the elements dirtied since the last persist plus the whole id map, not the index files whole. (10) LightRAG 1.5.7 deletes a document by id and rebuilds the affected entities and relations from its extraction cache; the rebuild is faithful only while that cache is kept (`delete_llm_cache` defaults to false); it also keeps every document's full text in its own KV store, reachable only through an internal attribute of a built per-hemisphere instance. (11) Shipped memory systems do time-based expiry with a per-writer TTL (LangGraph's store TTL with refresh on read; Mem0's expiration date, which hides rather than deletes); the evidence that usage-based forgetting improves answers is self-reported preprint results of small gains (about 1 to 3 F1 points over Mem0 on public benchmarks), unreplicated, with one paper's own ablation reversing on fact-consolidation tasks, and none of it about a single-owner store; the large demonstrated benefit is storage, which is not a constraint here (INFERRED from search snippets; the paper sites are unreachable from the sandbox). (12) Fully automatic adjustment is prevented by unit hardening for the three enqueue-only processes (prune, Sentinel, beat: `/etc/atlas` read-only, no docker group, no sudo) and by convention only (R22, accepted for Day 1) for the docker-group processes, among them the `cpu` worker that runs the sweep; the prohibition rests on 16.3 item 6 and the D9 closure, not on a physical guarantee. (13) The approval machinery can carry a proposal as a Standard-tier item in the Principal register, but only a hemisphere lead (Ren or Arthur) may submit there, not Domain 20's owner Valerie; the production Sender has no channel on Day 1 (S26), so an approved item would end in HTTP 502 unless its kind gets a recording Sender; a draft body goes through the never-delegate rewrite, which would turn "you will need to ..." apply instructions into questions to ATLAS; and decided approvals are purged after 30 days once the ledger purge runs, so a rejection cooldown must be read from the permanent strikes table (`reject()` writes a `rejected-draft` strike) | Approved by the Principal on 2026-10-12: the three fixes and the document changes now; the measurement layer and the proposal mechanism recorded as phased items, not built; fully automatic adjustment excluded (16.3 item 6); the D9 values (90-day chat TTL, 366-day Sentinel TTL, 30-day ledger) stay code constants. **(a) The window key.** `lib/common.sh` gains `ensure_kv_default FILE KEY VALUE`, which writes `KEY=VALUE` only when the file has no such key (a blank value counts as present) and otherwise leaves the line exactly as it is; Phase 2 step 2 seeds `PRUNE_WINDOW_HOURS=72` with it into `orchestrator.env`, so a Principal-approved value survives a re-run of the step (`ensure_kv` would have reset it); the file's header and the step's comment state what the key means (the fallback expiry of a temporal note whose writer gave no TTL; it governs no current writer; the cadence is the timer) and how to apply a change (edit as root, restart the three Celery units, which read the file at start). Step 8 never wrote the key (App. B, README). **(b) A failed sweep reaches the Principal.** When `prune_sweep` raises, or finishes with errors (a read, delete or graph-delete error, each already in the result), the task writes the ledger row as before, then pushes one ntfy line through `atlas.tasks.notify`, the vault-breach mechanism: title "ATLAS prune: sweep failed", priority high, the task id, the first 300 characters of the error and the note that the sweep is not retried before the next timer slot; then it re-raises. The push is guarded twice (`notify` never raises and has a 10-second timeout; `notify_failure` catches everything and logs), so it can neither mask the original exception nor block the sweep. A clean sweep and a spooled graph deletion (an AEGIS freeze) push nothing. The service and the module say in comments that the stamp is touched at enqueue, so a failed sweep is not retried before the next slot and the alert is what makes it visible. The ledger-then-push order follows the vault-breach precedent (strike first, then push); reserved for the Principal if the phone should come first. **(c) The graph archive carries the text.** The wrapper's own SQLite index of graph documents gains a `text` column, written on the one insert path the direct write and the AEGIS spool replay share, and `graph.json` members gain `document`, the same key the collection members use, so a pruned graph note is restorable like a vector note (id, text, hemisphere, metadata; no vectors, so a restore re-embeds, and no restore command exists yet). The source is the wrapper's index, not LightRAG's KV store: the same text sits there, but behind an internal attribute of a built per-hemisphere instance, outside the API set the wrapper records as verified, and the index costs one column (VERIFIED in the pinned source). An index created before the column is migrated in place; a row from it archives with `document` null, is counted in the manifest (`graph_without_text`) and warned about in the journal, so the archive never claims a restorability it lacks (none exist: nothing has run). The archive still reads back and verifies before anything is deleted; chat turns are still purged without archive (D9); scars are still exempt; vault handling is unchanged; plaintext exposure is unchanged (LightRAG's own `kv_store_full_docs.json` beside the index already holds the text). **Phased, not built.** In the first months (the event: the live ATLAS in daily use, so there is something to measure): the measurement layer, which is a permanent usage table of the ids each chat task retrieved, a `prune_runs` table with live counts before and after each sweep and archive bytes, `Ledger.purge_expired` wired into the sweep with the cold archive as its callback (closing R26), the graph part of the result separated from the vector part, and a quiet weekly summary through ntfy (the two-failures-in-a-row rule with it). The proposal mechanism (16.2, W-RETAIN) only once three things hold: 90 days of usage data (one chat-TTL cycle), three consistent sweeps, and a real tuning question from the reports (a class with meaningful near-expiry demand, disk pressure, or a degraded index); it then needs a configuration-proposal approval kind with a recording Sender, submission as Ren or Arthur in the Principal register, the deciding identity taken from the authenticated request, apply steps phrased as bare imperatives or carried in the reasoning field (so the never-delegate rewrite leaves them alone) and a cooldown read from the strikes table. Expect the usage numbers to be thin and inconclusive for a long time; that is the honest state, not a defect. The collection rebuild (W-CHROMA-REBUILD, R27) when the inserted-ever to live ratio warrants it, at one owner's volumes a yearly question at most. A restore command (re-embed an archived note's id, text and metadata from the dated archive into its collection; restored notes are new vectors, the old tombstones stay, R27; chat turns are never recoverable, D9) only when the Principal first asks for a pruned note back; until then it is unneeded code. Until then the weekly report and the Principal's judgement are the adjustment mechanism, and an env edit plus a Celery restart is the apply step. Not adopted: re-anchoring a note that is retrieved often (the self-reinforcing loop, and a contradiction of D9 for chat turns), consolidation of other temporal classes (only Sentinel BLUFs exist, already summaries), supersession metadata for permanent facts (no Chroma metadata update path in the wrapper today), and any recency or usage weighting in retrieval. UNKNOWN until the node runs: the ntfy server's acceptance of the push (as for the vault-breach push) and the real worker path, which the tests drive in-process. Tests: `lib/common_test.sh` 100 cases (93 before; the seven new ones cover the write-if-absent helper: a hand-set value kept, leading whitespace, a blank value, a commented key and a longer key not matching, a bad key refused), `orchestrator/tests/test_vault_tagging.py` 34 (30 before: the graph archive with its text and a migrated pre-S53 index, the failure push on both paths with a breaking push never masking the error, a locked ledger row neither skipping the push nor replacing the sweep's error, and a clean sweep pushing nothing, and the step 2 contract); the package suite runs 480 passed and 4 skipped (476 before). |
| S54 | Header, 0 to 10, 12.5, 13, 14.1, 14.4, 15, 16.1, 16.3, 16.5, 17 to 23, App. B to D | v0.3.5 pinned llama.cpp v0.4.1 and ran seven GGUF engines: gpt-oss-120b and its abliterated twin in MXFP4, Nemotron 3 Super, Qwen3.5-122B-A10B and Qwen2.5-VL-72B at `Q8_0`, DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 at `UD-Q4_K_XL`, and Meditron-70B, with the bge retrieval pair. Two engines could be resident where a pair fitted, with a standing two-slot vision profile; each file's decimal GB was read as GiB; Phase 3 pulled about 690 GB, gated decode on bands and proved a two-engine pair in V21; diarisation was pyannote 3.1 | The Principal approved every row of the action register on 2026-10-16 (D-25 option (a), D-26 option (c); D16 to D27). The scripts were changed to match before this revision (VERIFIED in the repository at commit 51dcac3 (scripts and configuration) and the v0.4.0 document commit that follows it (`tools/build-baseline-workbook.py` and its test, and the review fixes of 2026-10-16 below), unless marked; nothing has run on the node). (1) Pin: Phase 2 step 1 builds b11474 (commit b9acf138, CMake 0.6.0) with `llama-perplexity`, and v0.4.1's `llama-perplexity` alone from a worktree for V25; the reserved flag and environment lists carry the b11474 names; up to 20 engine slots, ports 8101 to 8120 (S-01, S-02, S-33). (2) Roster: `engines.json` holds seven units in this order: gpt-oss-120b (F16 trunk, `q8_0` cache), the abliterated twin (byte-identical to v0.3.5, which a test pins), GLM-5.3-Flash (`mode: vision`, f16, 8 × 32k), DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 (`UD-Q8_K_XL`, f16, the ladder f16 then `q8_0`, 1 × 131,072, reasoning on), the router, and the Qwen3 embedder and reranker on the CPU. Every file carries its bytes and sha256, each new LLM seat its projected decode beside a null floor, and every entry its licence; the phased engines sit in a `phased` list that no script reads (S-11, S-17 to S-20, S-27, S-42). (3) Arbiter: it budgets the exact bytes plus the KV cache, the per-slot state and a compute term (2.0 GiB per seat, 0.5 per companion, INFERRED); one LLM seat at a time, `ATLAS_ARBITER_MAX_RESIDENT` (default 1, range 1 to 2) seeded once by Phase 2 step 2; every seat exclusive; the CPU pair charged as the host resident set, its weights in full plus its measured anonymous memory (RssAnon + RssShmem), never below its projection, read outside the Arbiter's state lock, and the host-reserve warning looking past that anonymous share alone, in the Arbiter and `atlas-admin arbiter status` alike; the co-resident profile removed (D-31, D-32, S-22, S-23). (4) Personas, routes and presets: the 6.2 bindings with a recorded `fallback_engine`; `arthur-long` replaces `arthur-qwen`, and a rules file that still names the old route is refused; the long-document trigger stays at 24,000 tokens and moves a document only to a per-slot window no smaller than the routed engine's; a classifier-chosen deep Deep Think run is held at standard (D-43 (a)); TF_OMEGA keeps DeepSeek; the Deep Think pair and its Day 1 third opinion; the cross-check plan with the Apex allowed (S-29 to S-32, S-43). (5) Retrieval: Phase 2 step 4 serves the Qwen3 pair with `--device none`, truncates to 1,024 dimensions with the query instruction, and stops on a collection from another embedder (S-36, S-41, D-45). (6) Voice: pyannote community-1, with 3.1 as V6's diagnostic only (C-06). (7) Phase 3: the four-repository pull, listing the repository root for GLM's projector; the f16-first ladder; V10 recorded against the projections with the to-do `decode-floors`; V22 per newer engine; new steps 2b (parity, V25) and 2c (quality record, to-do `kld-run`); step 3 single residency at the limit of 1, the Arbiter's grant naming the evicted seat; the gate `V4 V10 V14b V21 V25 -- V22` (S-24 to S-26, S-34). (8) V3b's floor is the larger of 160000 MiB and the largest seat's projected footprint (D-36 (c), S-28); `tools/fill-workbook.py` fills V1 to V25, and the workbook builder is kept beside it (D-27 option (a)). (9) Pre-flight and the Phase 1 comments give Secure Boot's settled state (B-28, B-29) | Day 1 runs the v0.4.0 set: about 448 GB in Phase 3 and about 13 GB in Phase 2 (15.4). The gates are those of Sections 17 and 21: V3b's raised floor, V25 required at the Phase 3 gate, V22 recorded only. The scripts raise eight to-dos for these items, `decode-floors`, `context-targets`, `trial-task-set`, `seat-test-sets` and `seat-profiles` (Phase 3 step 2), `kld-run` (step 2c), `kv-estimates` and `cache-ram` (step 4); the other inputs and phased items are named in the document with their defaults and events (Section 22 and the list below). Not built: a reranker caller (5.3), slot save and restore, swap batching, the specialist seats, the drafters, the phased companions and the V4.1 trial (each phased with its to-do and event), and the memory measures that 4.2 rule 5 left to the Principal (approved and built on 2026-10-17, S55). After the review fixes of 2026-10-16: pytest 541 passed and 4 skipped; ruff clean; the suites above unchanged, with `pull_engine_files` 11 and `build_workbook` 20 beside them; `bash -n` and `shellcheck -x` clean on the four scripts changed or added |

The Celery `cpu` worker ran nproc − 2 prefork children (30 on this CPU). Kokoro, speaches and Docling had no memory cap, and no unit set `OOMScoreAdjust`. The source facts are VERIFIED at b11474 (commit b9acf138): `-cram`/`--cache-ram N` and `LLAMA_ARG_CACHE_RAM` (`common/arg.cpp:1719-1725`), a default of 8192 MiB (`common/common.h:638`), 0 disables the cache (`tools/server/server-context.cpp:1484-1495`) and with it `--cache-idle-slots` (`:1553-1556`), and a negative value means no limit (`tools/server/server-task.h:654`). (1) Measure 5 (B-33): `phase2/engine-env.py` renders `--cache-ram N` on every engine after `--no-webui --metrics` (`:296-297`). N is the `engines.json` field `cache_ram_mib`, an integer of 0 or more, or else the mode default (`CACHE_RAM_DEFAULT_MIB`, `:99`: 4096 for chat and vision, 0 for embedding and reranking; `cache_ram_of`, `:230`). -1 and every other value are refused, and `--cache-ram`, `-cram` and `LLAMA_ARG_CACHE_RAM` are reserved, so `extra_args` and `env` cannot undo the field. `engines.json` sets the router to 1024 (line 190) and the embedder and reranker to 0 (lines 222 and 253, whose old `extra_args` tokens are removed); the seats take 4096; `_meta.cache_ram_rule` (line 17) states the rule. The Phase 3 to-do `cache-ram` reads the cap each env file renders and asks whether to change it (`phase3/loadtest.py` `rendered_cache_ram_mib`, `cache_suggestion`, `CACHE_TODO_TITLE` at `:2720`). An env file that still carries a negative cap (hand-edited or out of date; `engine-env.py` refuses one) is reported as no limit, its worst case a lower bound at 8,192 MiB. (2) Measure 7: `phase1/03-mounts.sh:143` caps `/tmp` at `size=4G` (it was 16G), from the next boot. An audit of every `/tmp`, `mktemp`, `TMPDIR` and `tempfile` use found no Day 1 writer near 4 GiB: pulls, package caches, builds and large unpacks are on disk, and containers have their own `/tmp` (the sizes INFERRED). (3) Measure 6: `phase2/02-orchestrator.sh:414-416` computes `CELERY_CPU_CONCURRENCY` as min(8, nproc − 2), at least 1, and `:442` writes it (`ensure_kv`, so every run rewrites it): about 1.2 to 2.5 GiB less host RAM (22 fewer children at 60 to 120 MB each; INFERRED). (4) Measure 8: `docker/core/compose.voice.yml` caps kokoro at 3g (line 57), speaches at 4g (line 104) and docling at 8g (line 155), each with an equal `memswap_limit` (no swap). Each is raised from the review's suggested 2g, 3g and 6g, and INFERRED, not measured. The unit is stopped, which also cancels the pending `Restart=on-failure` restart (INFERRED from systemd semantics), and the GPU memory counter is polled back to its reading before the start with rule 5's poll, tolerance and time-box (`_release_target` and `_await_release`, `:1253` and `:1257`, now shared with `_stop_and_confirm`). A stop that fails, or a release not confirmed in time, halts the Arbiter as rule 5 does (`_halt_failed_start`, `:1301`; `ReleaseTimeout`). The resident small-model path and a class-external plan stop nothing; `Restart=on-failure` stays on `llama-server@.service` (`:59`) for a companion that crashes after loading. The reserve stays 16 GiB until host use has been measured on the node (measure 15, reserved for the Principal and not scripted as a to-do; `atlas-admin arbiter status` shows the CPU-side use against the reserve). **Watch-list additions from the build:** the platform watch-list of 3.7 (W-NPU, W-PERFOPT, W-KERNEL; v0.3.5; and W-DRILL, the crash and watchdog tests of S48 and S50 that the live ATLAS leads with the Principal present, 2026-10-11); UI-TARS 2.0 (S16); Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate and Qwen-Image-2.1 (the Principal keeps FLUX.1-dev for quality, 2026-10-05); ROCm 10.1.0 (general availability 2026-10-05; reviewed 2026-10-11, S51, below); a 26.04 security update for xrdp (R24), and any Cockpit security advisory (3.1); W-RETAIN (S53): usage-adaptive retention proposals, considered, not adopted; the event that brings it in is 90 days of usage data from the measurement layer, three consistent sweeps and a real tuning question, and the form is then an approval-gated proposal (16.2) that the Principal applies as root, since fully automatic adjustment is excluded by 16.3 item 6; and W-CHROMA-REBUILD (S53, R27): a collection rebuild when the inserted-ever to live ratio of a Chroma collection warrants it, since the pinned Chroma only soft-deletes. *v0.4.0 (S54, S-57, S-58):* the engine watch-list of 15.5 gains the DeepSeek V4.1 trial and its runtimes, single-stream speculative decoding, GLM Legal, Mistral Large 4, Nemotron 4, Nemotron 3 Puzzle, the 3-bit 300B-class engines, Harrier-27B, EmbeddingGemma 2 if dropped and llama.cpp PR #26603, with the record of what was considered and not adopted beside it (S-52); 3.7 gains W-DGPU, W-PLACEMENT, W-MEMGUARD, W-MANIFEST and W-SESSIONLOG; Qwen3-TTS leaves the watch-list for the test plan (14.1, T-22). **ROCm 10.1 (S51).** Phase 4 stays pinned to 10.0.0 until a deliberate re-test. 10.0.0 is AMD-documented for gfx1151 on Ubuntu 26.04 with the GA 7.0 kernel and is the version the Day 1 scripts were written against; it has not yet run on the node. On Ryzen, AMD offers 10.1 only with PyTorch 2.14.0 (torch 2.14.0, torchvision 0.29.0a0, torchaudio 2.11.0.3); torch 2.13.0+rocm10.1.0 is offered for this chip only through AMD's generic `device-all` path (a gfx1151-specific command sits in the page source, hidden from Ryzen; VERIFIED). 10.1 does not address the S15 hang reports (INFERRED). 10.0.0 keeps one CPU core busy per ROCm process at idle (TheRock #7051: VERIFIED on gfx1100 and gfx1201, INFERRED on gfx1151); that 10.1 removes it rests on a single gfx1151 report, which compared idle 10.1.0 clients with 7.14.1 clients, not with 10.0.0: thin evidence. The re-test comes after the Phase 4 gate passes on 10.0.0, sooner if V11 fails on 10.0.0 for a reason the kernel does not explain; 10.2 is due mid-November at the earliest (INFERRED). **Confirmed by the Principal on 2026-10-16** in the returned action register (B-03, B-30, B-45, B-73, B-74, B-75 and B-76, and B-44 for Day 1), kept as built: the softdog fallback (B-30) and the two-minute watchdog timeout (B-73); the three choices the build made in the window rule, the 512-token minimum reply, the reply reserve capped at a quarter of the slot (or 512 tokens, whichever is larger), and no 9.4 strike for a refused request (B-45); `--swa-full` on the gpt-oss engines and a smaller context on any engine, both left unadopted (B-74); `--verbosity 4` on every chat engine, a log-level-only change that V4's proof lines and the S52 records need (B-75); the prune failure path, the ledger row first and then the push as for a vault breach, with no push on a clean sweep or a spooled graph deletion (B-76); lockup panics, `CrashAction=reboot` and `watchdog.stop_on_reboot=0`, all off for Day 1 (B-44); and the to-do `kv-estimates`, which now adopts Phase 3's measurements into the Arbiter's estimates for every kept engine before any long context (B-03). **Reserved for the Principal:** the hang reports in S15 (corrected in S51) and their workarounds, if V11 fails for that reason (B-42); from S51, moving Phase 4 to ROCm 10.1, after Day 1 (B-41); from S52, the to-do `cache-ram` (whether to change the Day 1 `--cache-ram` caps set in S55, and `--ctx-checkpoints`, decided at Phase 3 from the measured host RAM, B-33); from S50, masking the sleep targets and loading the driver in the initramfs, decided from the W-DRILL results (B-44). From S54 (v0.4.0): the to-dos the scripts raise, `decode-floors` (the per-seat decode floors, D-33, D-47), `kld-run` (the reference KLD run, T-03, with each seat's reasoning setting, D-30), `context-targets` (the D-16 context targets), `trial-task-set` (D-46), `seat-test-sets` (D-49) and `seat-profiles` (the DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 context target of 131,072 tokens, GLM-5.3-Flash's cache type and slot profile with `--kv-unified`, T-04, D-48, and the 24,000-token long-document trigger); the inputs the document records with a stated default: a dedicated coder seat (D-19), EmbeddingGemma 2 (D-60) and MedGemma 1.5 4B (C-12); the routing-accuracy set for the 4B router (P-10, 5.3), wiring the reranker (5.3), a longer embedder input profile (C-02, T-15) and retuning the scar threshold for the new embedder on Chroma's squared-L2 scale (9.4, T-15); and a second resident LLM seat (`ATLAS_ARBITER_MAX_RESIDENT=2`, 4.2 rule 3). The V25 thresholds and the memory measures v0.4.0 left here (fewer Celery CPU workers, a smaller `/tmp`, memory limits on the voice and document containers, an order for the kernel's out-of-memory killer, and stopping a seat whose start failed) left this list on 2026-10-17: the Principal confirmed the thresholds and approved the measures, now built (S55). From S55: the optional `MemoryHigh` on the Celery `cpu` worker (answers.md measure 8, not built) and the size of the host reserve once host use has been measured (measure 15). The vision engine's 8-slot profile (`overrides.json` coresident=false) and `--kv-unified` on Qwen3.5-122B left this list with the engines they concerned (S-58).

## Appendix A — Request lifecycle

```
Principal (phone/laptop, Open WebUI over WireGuard or LAN)
   |
   v
Open WebUI Filter  --relay-->  Orchestrator
                                  |-- 4-Way Router: keyword hard rules -> Eleanor classifier -> task-force detection
                                  |-- Approval-tier assignment
                                  |-- Domain card compilation (max 3) onto the stable persona prefix
                                  |-- Celery task (cpu or gpu queue), task ID, ledger entry
                                  |-- Engine Arbiter: load/queue/refuse; confirm previous engine released
                                  |-- Per-slot window (S52): prompt counted by the engine; oldest exchanges dropped
                                  |          after the stable prefix only until a bounded reply reserve fits, reply capped;
                                  |          413 if the message alone does not fit (HTTP status; in a stream, its error chunk)
                                  v
                        llama-server (Vulkan) or a Phase 4 engine container (ROCm)
                                  |-- tools: code interpreter, retrieval (ChromaDB + graph), Docling, MCP servers,
                                  |          browser, Google/Xero/Cloudflare/Wix/Pay.com APIs, file share, sandbox
                                  v
                        Director output -> optional relay to next director -> Ren/Arthur synthesis
                                  |-- Cross-check (cheap or strong) per tier
                                  |-- Never-delegate rewrite pass; register set
                                  |-- Outbound gate: routine auto-sends, standard/sensitive wait for approval
                                  v
                        Memory writes (unless vault-tagged) -> Ouroboros strike on failure -> logs -> ntfy
```

## Appendix B — Configuration reference

**Kernel (GRUB):** `amdgpu.gttsize=196608 ttm.pages_limit=50331648 amdgpu.lockup_timeout=10000,60000,10000,10000 panic=10 kho=off`, and no `crashkernel=`. The three GPU parameters come from `/etc/default/grub.d/90-atlas.cfg`; `panic=10`, `kho=off` and the removal of kdump-tools' `crashkernel=` (and of any other `panic=` or `kho=`) come from `/etc/default/grub.d/zzz-atlas-crash.cfg`, sourced last (S48, S49). kdump stays installed but off: debconf `kdump-tools/use_kdump=false`, `USE_KDUMP=0` in `/etc/default/kdump-tools`, `kdump-tools.service` disabled and masked.

**Watchdog (S50, 3.6):** `/etc/systemd/system.conf.d/90-atlas-watchdog.conf`, `[Manager]` with `RuntimeWatchdogSec=2min` and `RebootWatchdogSec=10min` and nothing else (`/etc/systemd/system.conf` is never edited). `/etc/systemd/system/atlas-watchdog-driver.service`, enabled (wanted by `sysinit.target`), runs `/usr/sbin/modprobe sp5100_tco` before `sysinit.target` at every boot (`wdat_wdt`, or `softdog soft_panic=1` with the to-do `watchdog`, when step 4 found no SP5100 TCO timer). Only systemd holds `/dev/watchdog0`: no watchdog daemon, no `wdctl`. Rollback: delete the drop-in and run `systemctl daemon-reload` (systemd disarms the timer), then disable the unit.

**llama-server, every Day 1 unit (v0.4.0, S-53; rendered by `phase2/engine-env.py` from `engines.json` into `/etc/atlas/engines/<key>.env`, which `llama-server@<key>` reads):** `--model <file> [--mmproj <file>] --alias <key> --host 127.0.0.1 --port <8100 + index> --ctx-size <pool> --parallel <slots>`; on chat and vision units `--keep <n_keep> --context-shift --verbosity 4 --slot-save-path /srv/atlas/data/slots`; then `--flash-attn on --cache-type-k <type> --cache-type-v <type>` (K and V always identical), `--n-gpu-layers all` (or `--device none` on the CPU pair), `--no-webui --metrics`, `--cache-ram <MiB>` (S55: the entry's `cache_ram_mib`, else 4096 for chat and vision and 0 for embedding and reranking; -1, and the flag in `extra_args` or `env`, refused), then the entry's `extra_args`. A value that is JSON goes through its `LLAMA_ARG_*` variable in the env file, never the command line. `--ctx-size` is the total pool across slots.

**GLM-5.3-Flash `UD-IQ4_XS` (`glm-5.3-flash`, port 8103; Ren, Gideon, Silas, Helena, Minerva, Victor, Alaric, Eleanor's persona work, vision, long documents):** `--mmproj mmproj-F16.gguf --ctx-size 262144 --parallel 8 --keep 4096 --cache-type-k f16 --cache-type-v f16 --jinja --temp 1.0 --top-p 0.95 -b 2048 -ub 512 --cache-ram 4096` (32k × 8 slots, without `--kv-unified`). The f16 cache is provisional until T-04; flash attention stays on unless T-04 finds that `glm5-next` needs it off on Vulkan, which would also rule out a quantised cache (R4); the slot and context profile is D-48's Phase 3 measurement (4.3).

**DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 `UD-Q8_K_XL` (`deepseek-v4-flash`, port 8104; Arthur, the Apex engine):** `--ctx-size 131072 --parallel 1 --keep 4096 --cache-type-k f16 --cache-type-v f16 --jinja --reasoning on --reasoning-effort high --temp 1.0 --top-p 1.0 --min-p 0 -b 2048 -ub 512 --cache-ram 4096`: one slot of 131,072 tokens, the stated default until the Principal sets a context target (4.3). Phase 3's ladder writes `q8_0` to `overrides.json` only if f16 fails; `q4_0` is never a rung (S-42).

**gpt-oss-120b, F16 trunk (`gpt-oss-120b`, port 8101; fast utility, fallback floor, the Day 1 Deep Think third opinion):** `--ctx-size 262144 --parallel 8 --keep 4096 --cache-type-k q8_0 --cache-type-v q8_0 --jinja --temp 1.0 --top-p 1.0 --min-p 0 -b 2048 -ub 2048 --cache-ram 4096`, with `LLAMA_ARG_CHAT_TEMPLATE_KWARGS={"reasoning_effort":"high"}` in its env file. The abliterated twin (port 8102) is rendered as in v0.3.5: the same profile with `--cache-type-k q4_0 --cache-type-v q4_0`.

**The router (`router-qwen3.5-4b`, port 8105, iGPU):** `--ctx-size 32768 --parallel 4 --keep 2048 --cache-type-k q8_0 --cache-type-v q8_0 --jinja --reasoning off --temp 0.2 --cache-ram 1024` (8k × 4 slots; `cache_ram_mib` 1024, S55).

**The Qwen3 retrieval pair (`embed-qwen3-4b`, port 8106, and `rerank-qwen3-4b`, port 8107, on the CPU; D-14 option (e)):** `--ctx-size 4096 --parallel 1 --flash-attn on --cache-type-k q8_0 --cache-type-v q8_0 --device none -b 2048 -ub 2048 --threads 8 --cache-ram 0` (since S55 from `cache_ram_mib` 0, no longer an `extra_args` token), with `--embedding --pooling last` on the embedder and `--reranking` on the reranker; no `--n-gpu-layers`, no context shift, no slot directory. `memory.env` carries `EMBEDDING_MODEL=embed-qwen3-4b`, `EMBEDDING_DIM=1024` (each vector truncated and re-normalised) and `EMBEDDING_QUERY_INSTRUCTION`, seeded once (10.1).

**Every chat engine (S52, re-verified at b11474):** `--context-shift --keep` is requested on every chat and vision launch line, but the pinned build switches context shift off on every Day 1 chat engine (4.3); the orchestrator keeps every request inside one slot. Since S55 every engine passes an explicit `--cache-ram` (4096 MiB on the seats, 1024 on the router, 0 on the CPU pair; llama-server's default without it is 8192); `--ctx-checkpoints` is not passed, so 32 checkpoints per slot apply. The to-do `cache-ram` measures whether to change either, and a changed cap goes in `cache_ram_mib`. `--verbosity 4` is on every chat and vision launch line: the pinned build logs the size lines V4 and the Phase 3 records read only from verbosity 4 (exactly 4: 5 or `-v` would add `--fit`'s trial lines); it changes the log level only. No drafter flag is passed on Day 1: `--spec-type draft-mtp` or `draft-dspark` is decided per engine after Day 1 (D-61, 15.5).

**Arbiter (D-32 option (a)):** `ATLAS_ARBITER_MAX_RESIDENT=1` in `/etc/atlas/orchestrator.env` (range 1 to 2), seeded once by Phase 2 step 2 so that a value the Principal sets survives a re-run, beside `ATLAS_ARBITER_HEADROOM_GIB`.

**Host RAM bounds (S55, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-17; 4.1, 4.2 rule 5):** `/etc/systemd/system/tmp.mount.d/atlas.conf` mounts `/tmp` with `mode=1777,strictatime,nosuid,nodev,size=4G,nr_inodes=1m`, from the next boot. `CELERY_CPU_CONCURRENCY` in `/etc/atlas/orchestrator.env` is min(8, nproc − 2), at least 1, rewritten by Phase 2 step 2 on every run. `docker/core/compose.voice.yml` sets `mem_limit` and `memswap_limit` to 3g on kokoro, 4g on speaches and 8g on docling. `OOMScoreAdjust=500` on `atlas-celery-cpu.service` and `oom_score_adj: 500` on docling; `OOMScoreAdjust=-500` on `atlas-orchestrator.service` and `llama-server@.service`. `llama-server@.service` keeps `Restart=on-failure` (`RestartSec=5`) for a companion that crashes after loading; the Arbiter stops a seat whose start failed.

**Build:** llama.cpp build tag `b11474`, commit `b9acf138a1e28ce1fc23b5a4fc4b12444b50f7ea`, `-DGGML_VULKAN=ON -DLLAMA_OPENSSL=ON -DLLAMA_USE_PREBUILT_UI=OFF -DLLAMA_BUILD_TESTS=OFF -DBUILD_SHARED_LIBS=OFF`, with `LLAMA_BUILD_IS_DEV` left on (a build tag, not a release); `llama-server`, `llama-cli`, `llama-bench`, `llama-quantize` and `llama-perplexity` in `/usr/local/bin`. The parity build: tag `v0.4.1`, commit `b29c606e28a01b1bc8c1351026a0fa6e616bf6c4`, `llama-perplexity` only, with `-DLLAMA_BUILD_IS_DEV=OFF`, in `/opt/atlas/llama-parity`, never linked into `/usr/local/bin` (3.4).

**Desktop:** XFCE with xrdp bound to the LAN and WireGuard interfaces, no autologin, session started on demand.

**Ollama equivalents, if D1 chooses Ollama:** `OLLAMA_FLASH_ATTENTION=1`, `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0|q4_0` (global, so per-persona asymmetry needs two daemons), `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE=-1`, `OLLAMA_NUM_PARALLEL=8`, `OLLAMA_MODELS=/srv/atlas/models`, `num_ctx` set per Modelfile. Note the q-cache allowlist fallback (V4).

**Open WebUI offline:** `OFFLINE_MODE=true`, `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1`, `ENABLE_COMMUNITY_SHARING=false`, web search disabled, update check disabled, audio STT/TTS endpoints pointed at local Whisper and Kokoro.

**Retention (S53; 9.6, 10.4):** the keys of `/etc/atlas/orchestrator.env` (root:atlas 640) that govern retention are `PRUNE_WINDOW_HOURS=72` (the fallback expiry, in hours, of a temporal memory note whose writer gave no TTL; seeded once by Phase 2 step 2 and never rewritten by a re-run of it, so an approved change survives `--force 02`; it governs no current writer, and the sweep's cadence is `atlas-prune.timer`, daily 03:10 plus the 71-hour stamp, installed by step 8 and not a key), `COLD_DIR=/srv/cold` (the sweep's dated archives; steps 2 and 8 both write it) and `OPENWEBUI_CHAT_RETENTION_DAYS=90` (Open WebUI chats older than this are summarised into the Vector Cortex and deleted by the nightly 04:15 task). The chat-turn TTL (90 days, `atlas.api`), the Sentinel TTL (366 days, `atlas.tasks.sentinel`) and the ledger's 30-day window (`atlas.ledger`, not yet applied, R26) are code constants fixed by D9, not settings. To apply an approved change (16.3 item 6: ATLAS proposes, the Principal applies): edit the file as root, then `systemctl restart atlas-celery-cpu atlas-celery-gpu atlas-celery-beat` (the workers read the file only at start).

**Containers needing the GPU:** pass `/dev/kfd` and `/dev/dri`, run as the service user in `render` and `video`, set the ROCm userspace inside the image, never on the host.

## Appendix C — Storage plan

| Path | Drive | Contents | Backed up |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/srv/atlas/models` | 8 TB | GGUF weights: about 461 GB on Day 1, about 1.3 TB with the phased engines (v0.4.0, 15.4). The phased V4.1 trial keeps one file at a time (about 366 GB) on a path of its own on this drive (D-54 option (a)) | No, manifest only |
| `/srv/atlas/engines` | 8 TB | PyTorch engine weights and images | No, manifest only |
| `/srv/atlas/data` | 8 TB | ChromaDB, graph store, SQLite, slot saves | Yes |
| `/srv/atlas/workspace`, `/srv/atlas/sandbox` | 8 TB | Working files, sandbox runs | Yes |
| `/srv/atlas/vault/cipher` | 8 TB | gocryptfs ciphertext | Yes, as ciphertext |
| `/srv/atlas/vault/open` | 8 TB | gocryptfs mount point, populated only while unlocked | Never |
| `/srv/cold` | 4 TB | Pruned-memory archives (`prune/prune-<UTC stamp>.tar.zst`: vector and, since S53, graph documents with their text; chat turns are never archived, D9); no rotation; bounded by disk and Sentinel's hourly disk telemetry | Yes |
| `/srv/backups` | 4 TB | restic repository | Is the backup |
| `/var/log` | 4 TB | Logs, 30 days hot | Rotated |

## Appendix D — Sources relied on during review

Hardware: GMKtec EVO-X5 Pro announcement and VideoCardz and T3 specification reports for the Ryzen AI Max+ PRO 495 "Gorgon Halo" platform; Notebookcheck's processor page. Quantisation sizes: Unsloth's DeepSeek V4 and Qwen3.5 run guides, bartowski's Nemotron 3 Super GGUF listing, ggml-org's Qwen2.5-VL-72B GGUF. Ubuntu: OMG Ubuntu and Phoronix on the 24.04.5 point release and the amd64v3 archive experiments. The v0.1 hardware sources for the MS-S1 Max are superseded. Platform: AMD ROCm Strix Halo system-optimisation guide; community Strix Halo local-LLM guides; LucRoot known-good ROCm llama.cpp recipe; llama.cpp discussion on the known-good Strix Halo stack; Phoronix Ubuntu 26.04 Strix Halo benchmarks. Models: Unsloth Nemotron 3 Super guide; Beinsezii Qwen3.5-122B-A10B Strix Halo GGUF; Huihui gpt-oss-120b abliterated MXFP4 GGUF; gpt-oss model card. KV cache: Ollama FAQ and environment reference; llama.cpp server README on `n_keep` and context shift; StreamingLLM paper. Engines: kyuz0 and matthewhand Strix Halo ComfyUI toolboxes; TRELLIS.2 ROCm forks; Evo2StrixHalo port; SAM 2 ROCm issues; MCP4IFC project page and paper; Radiance at LBNL; Genusys and Auto BIM Route for the MEP market. Watch-list: Qwen3.8-LiveTranslate announcement and Model Studio API page; Qwen-Image-2.1 model card, Hugging Face licence file and GGUF listing; Black Forest Labs FLUX.1-dev and FLUX.1-schnell licence pages. Voice: Kokoro-82M VOICES.md; Trelis and Pinggy 2026 TTS comparisons; Qwen3-TTS repository; Fish Audio S2 licence page. Legal: Apple EULA analyses of OSX-KVM. NPU and kernel line (v0.3.5): Phoronix on the Gorgon Halo NPU patch and on AMD IOMMU PerfOpt (search summaries only); the `amdxdna` driver source at v7.0, v7.2 and master; the patch "accel/amdxdna: Add support for AIE2 revision 9" (intel-lab-lkp mirror); Ubuntu's linux 7.0.0-38.38, 7.0.0-39.39 and 7.3.0-9.9 sources and changelogs; the resolute archive indices (linux-meta, linux-meta-oem-7.0, linux-firmware-amd-misc, libxrt-npu2); the 26.04.1 server ISO (install-sources.yaml, manifests, the shipped subiquity snap); subiquity, curtin, livecd-rootfs, ubuntu-drivers-common, apt 3.2.0, linux-base and grub2 2.14 sources; Ubuntu's kernel lifecycle, HWE and OEM kernel pages; FastFlowLM's README, TERMS.md, v1.0.7 release and Linux install guide; Lemonade; AMD's Ryzen AI Linux documentation; llama.cpp issue #21725; omarchy-pkgs issue #761. Kexec handover (v0.3.5, S49): the v7.0 kernel sources (`kernel/liveupdate/kexec_handover.c` and `luo_core.c`, `mm/mm_init.c`, `mm/memblock.c`, `mm/vmstat.c`, `mm/cma.c`, `mm/page_alloc.c`, `drivers/gpu/drm/ttm/ttm_pool.c`, `lib/kstrtox.c`, `kernel/params.c`, `kernel-parameters.txt`); Ubuntu's linux-buildinfo configs and source diffs for 7.0.0-38.38 and 7.0.0-39.39; the resolute archive indices; the systemd 259.5 and kexec-tools 2.0.32-3ubuntu1 sources; NVIDIA's NV-Kernels pull request #590 and its `24.04_linux-nvidia-7.0-next` branch; Supabase's postgres pull request #2472. ROCm 10.1 (v0.3.5, S51): AMD's blog "ROCm 10.1: Breaking the Data-Movement Bottleneck"; AMD-Ecosystem/ai-ecosystem's PyTorch install pages for ROCm 10.0.0 and 10.1.0 (`rocm10.0.0-pip-install.rst`, `rocm10.1.0-pip-install.rst`, `install.rst`) and its `gpus.yaml`; ROCm/ROCm's `os-support.yaml` and `driver-firmware-support.yaml`; TheRock's `RELEASES.md`, `SUPPORTED_GPUS.md` and `build_prod_wheels.py` at therock-10.1, and its issues #7051 and #7578; ROCm/legacy-rocm-build issues #6182, #6508 and #6530; Linux commit 342981fff328; pytorch/audio's release/2.11 `setup.py`; Ubuntu's resolute archive indices, the linux 7.0.0-30.30 and 7.0.0-38.38 changelogs, unattended-upgrades 2.12ubuntu9 and the 26.04.1 live-server manifest; Canonical's blog "AMD ROCm on Ubuntu"; Phoronix on ROCm 10.1 (search summaries only). The v0.4.0 arsenal (S-40, approved by the Principal on 2026-10-16 in the returned action register): the three research reports in `reports/` ("ATLAS LLM roster refresh 2026", "ATLAS specialist LLM arsenal" and "Lucebox engine and memory hierarchy") and their notes in the folders of the same names under `research_notes/`; the action register (`docs/ATLAS_ACTION_REGISTER.xlsx`) and the Principal's responses returned on 2026-10-16; llama.cpp at build tag b11474 (commit b9acf138) against v0.4.1 (commit b29c606), re-read on 2026-10-16 for the pin facts (`common/arg.cpp`, `common/common.h`, `common/log.cpp`, `CMakeLists.txt`, `src/llama-kv-cache*.cpp`, `src/models/`, `tools/server/`, `tools/mtmd/`, `ggml/src/ggml-vulkan/ggml-vulkan.cpp`), and its git history for each architecture's first-support tag (`git tag --contains`); the Hugging Face tree API for the bytes and sha256 of every Day 1 and phased file, and the GGUF headers of the Day 1 files (2026-10-16); pyannote's speaker-diarization-community-1 repository tree and licence (2026-10-16). The Day 1 research files that the script comments cite for earlier pins and sizes (`gguf-models.md`, `llama-cpp-vulkan.md`, `rocm-containers.md`, `voice-stt.md`, `services-tools.md`) are not in the repository, so their first-support tags and sizes cannot be re-read there. v0.4.0 re-read the tag and sizes of every Day 1 entry at b11474 and from the Hugging Face tree, records them in `engines.json` (`min_llama_cpp_tag`, `files[]`), and still cites the old files for the unchanged abliterated entry, the router's thinking note and the `_meta` files, layout, KV and parallel rules (the env rule is re-cited to the build record below). The v0.4.0 build record is in the repository, `research_notes/ATLAS v0.4.0 build record/`: `engine_data.md` and `engine_data.json` (the Hugging Face tree and GGUF header data of 2026-10-16), `pin_facts.md` (the llama.cpp facts re-read at b11474 against v0.4.1) and `answers.md` (the RAM-headroom, D-14 placement and P-10 analysis); the scripts and `engines.json` cite it by that path.
